The build-kit `scssPlugin` now threads the consumer's esbuild `alias` map into a Sass `findFileUrl` importer so SCSS `@use`/`@forward` can pull these partials through the `@newspack-nodes/*` surface (previously the alias resolved for JS imports only). `src/event-dashboards/styles/base.scss` is migrated as the proof: it now `@forward`s the tokens + mixins (so siblings keep resolving `base.$dark-text`, `@include base.dark-search-input`, …) and `@use`s the shared components — the compiled `event-dashboards` CSS is byte-identical before vs after. The other four dashboard `base.scss` files (in `newspack-event-logger-nodes`) are deliberately a follow-on, pending the `--np-*`-vs-universal theming decision.
- **`useBatchedPoll(opts)` + `addSliceFetcher(interpreter, slice)` — the dashboard batched-poll toolkit (`@newspack-nodes/shared`).** The two helpers that lift the poll-pattern dashboard boilerplate the example used to hand-wire. `useBatchedPoll` (a React hook — page-visibility needs React; `src/shared/hooks/useBatchedPoll.js`) owns the entire mount/batch skeleton: it `mountExospine`s the backbone, pre-creates the `_shell` Tap + `_http` HttpOut (with an injectable `commandClient` test seam), creates a fan-out `Tee` (`teeName`) + a router-hitchhike `Timer` (`timerName`) that fans each tick to it, brackets the router TIMER tick with `http.lock()` / `http.flush()` so a tick's commands batch into ONE POST, and gates the Timer on page visibility (HIDDEN unregisters it from the router TIMER → no POST; VISIBLE re-registers). The caller passes a `build( { interpreter, tee } )` that adds only its dashboard-specific nodes and the egress target path `_shell/_http/<ci>`; it returns `{ interpreterRef }` and re-renders after each build (the `bumpBuild` semantics) so widgets' `useNodeState` re-subscribe to the freshly-mounted view nodes. `addSliceFetcher` (a pure function; `src/shared/helpers/addSliceFetcher.js`) is the per-slice `SLICES.forEach` body as one call: it wires `makeNode('Fetcher', fetcher, '<receiver> <command>')` → `connectNode(target)`, fans the supplied Tee to it, creates the receiver `Tee` connected to the view node, makes the view node, and (optional) inserts a `transform: { name, nodeClass, args }` node on the receiver-Tee → view edge so a per-slice merge/dedup lands on a graph edge instead of inside the view; it returns the receiver name.
- **`Fetcher` JS runtime node (`Control` category).** A reusable dashboard-composition primitive that turns ANY trigger message into ONE configured command send — the building block for `Timer → Tee → Fetchers → _shell/_http/<ci>` graphs where the Timer tick hitchhikes every fetcher's command into one HTTP POST. `arguments = <receiver> <command> [<command_args>...]` (Tachikoma positional style, parsed in the `arguments` setter): `receiver` is the local node the server's reply routes back TO (stamped as the emitted command's `FROM`), `command` is the verb, and the joined remainder is the command arguments. `fill( message )` IGNORES the trigger payload — every message is just a trigger — and emits ONE `TM_COMMAND` whose `FROM` = the configured receiver and `VALUE = { name: <command>, arguments: <command_args> }`, forwarded through `sink` with `TO` stamped from `target` by the base `fill()` (the dashboard `connect_node`s the Fetcher's target to `_shell`/`_http`/`<ci>`; the node itself hardcodes no path). The command is configured on the node, never read from the message (a node that sends the command carried in its message is a Shell). Registered as `Fetcher` in `CommandInterpreterNode.includeNodes`, so `makeNode( 'Fetcher', name )` resolves it.
- **`wp nodes activate <topology>` / `wp nodes deactivate <topology>` CLI verbs.** A headless path to activate (add to the persisted active set + spawn the fleet now) and deactivate (remove + drain) a topology — closing the gap where the only way to toggle a topology was the Settings → Topologies UI (and `wp nodes restart` rejects a not-yet-spawned type). `activate` validates the name against the catalog (`Topology_Registry::describe()`), erroring with the available catalog names on a miss, is idempotent (already-active re-spawns, no duplicate), and refuses a write-conflict before persisting. The option-write + cache-invalidate + spawn/drain logic is extracted onto the shared `Topology_Registry::activate()` / `Topology_Registry::deactivate()` primitives, which the existing `Topologies_CI_Node` REST/UI verbs now also call (the inline closures were de-duplicated onto them).
- **Generic `Node::dump_metadata(): array` hook.** A per-node seam to contribute extra fields into its `dump_metadata` row; the base returns `[]`. `cmd_dump_metadata()` merges it with `+=` (the fixed metadata keys always win — the hook can only add), so the interpreter stays generic with no per-subclass special-casing.
- **Time-travel transport verbs on `Consumer_Node` (base, so `Tail` and every consumer inherit them).** Back a debugger UI that pauses a consumer, seeks it to an offsetlog keyframe, and single-steps it message-by-message. The READ surface (the offsetlog keyframe list + the live cursor) rides `dump_metadata` via `dump_metadata()` — see "Changed" below. Auth-gated commands (`{name}:config` verbs — the TM_REQUEST path bypasses `interpret()`'s auth gate, so every MUTATING verb is a command): `STEP` implies PAUSE (stops the self-rearming `fire()` loop so it can't interleave full-batch polls between steps), forces one-message granularity, emits at most one message, advances the durable cursor, and replies with the `{ seg, off, at_eof }` cursor as JSON; `SEEK_FRAME <segment_id>` reads that ONE offsetlog segment (its id comes from `dump_metadata`'s `frames[].id`), `restore_state()`s its co-committed `cache` into the snapshot node, and repositions the read cursor to the record's SOURCE `{seg,off}` (stays paused — does not re-arm the timer); `PAUSE` stops the poll timer; `PLAY` restores the line_mode `STEP` captured (not hardcoded false — `line_mode` is a legitimate production setting) and re-arms the `fire()` loop. New `Consumer_Node::$saved_line_mode` holds the pre-STEP mode for the session.
- **Time-travel PLAY truncate-on-resume + `Partition_Node::truncate_after( int $segment_id )`.** A rewind seek among offsetlog keyframes stays non-destructive while paused (STEP/PAUSE don't checkpoint), but on `PLAY` — the commit-to-this-branch moment — a rewound `Consumer_Node` now truncates its offsetlog before re-arming the timer, so the re-written forward timeline stays monotonic instead of going out of sync (a new frame at the highest id but with a SOURCE cursor behind the retained later frames). `seek_frame()` records the rewind point in the new private `Consumer_Node::$rewound_to`; `play()` calls `$this->offsetlog?->truncate_after( $this->rewound_to )` then clears it (a normal pause→play with no prior seek leaves `rewound_to` null → no truncation; a second seek overwrites it with the newer branch point). The new substrate primitive `Partition_Node::truncate_after()` deletes (unlinks) every segment with id > `$segment_id`, then resets the write state so the log continues coherently FROM `$segment_id` (`current_segment_id`/`current_size` reset to that segment, the cached file handle is closed, and `segments_cache` becomes the survivor list maintaining the rotation-cache invariant) — the next checkpoint rotates cleanly to `$segment_id + 1` (monotonic, no gap, no survivor overwritten). No-op when `$segment_id` is the newest, past the newest, or absent. The OFFSETLOG only — never the source log: `truncate_after()` is single-writer only, so it throws a `\RuntimeException` (mirroring `allow_large_writes()`'s fail-loud contract) when the partition holds an exclusivity `write_lock` (an `allow_large_writes()`-locked, multi-writer-guarded source) — the consumer's lock-free `void_warranty()` offsetlog passes the guard.
- **The `Consumer_Node` offsetlog is now an exact keyframe timeline for time-travel** — `OFFSETLOG_SEGMENT_SIZE` 65536→**1** and `OFFSETLOG_NUM_SEGMENTS` 2→**10**. `Partition_Node::do_rotate()` adopts the still-empty newest segment on the first commit and rotates to a fresh segment on every commit after (so `segment_size=1` produces no empty-segment spam), making **one checkpoint = one segment = one frame** uniformly for stateless (small offset records) and stateful/snapshot (offset + cache) consumers. `num_segments=10` retains the last 10 keyframes; `load_offsetlog()` crash-resume still reads the newest segment (now exactly one record). The keyframe list in `dump_metadata`'s `frames` IS this segment list, so each frame's id is its offsetlog segment id.
- **`newspack_nodes/devtools_overlay_pages` filter + `Admin::devtools_overlay_pages()`.** The shared registry of admin page slugs (besides the hub) that embed `<DebugOverlay>`. An overlay-tab-providing bundle (e.g. ELN's `current-request` "Request" tab) enqueues its tab on these pages, so a consumer plugin gets the full overlay tab set just by declaring its page on the filter — not only the hub and the tab provider's own pages. The substrate's own hub-host tab bundles still enqueue only on the hub, so consumer pages don't pull in the hub dashboards.
- **Universal `--chart-1..8` categorical-series tokens and `--{oxide,sage,brass,cyan}-subtle` tint tokens** in the hub theme vocabulary. Defined once in the ROOT `.topology-app` block as `color-mix`/`var()` derivations of each theme's own accents (`--cyan`/`--sage`/`--brass`/`--oxide` + their `-dark` shades), so chart series and status tints re-skin per theme with no per-theme-block edits; the `theme-newspack` skin maps `--chart-1..6` onto the sanctioned `--np-chart-*` brand palette.
- **General `'hidden' => true` flag on a node command schema** keeps a `:config` verb out of the topology-console inspector's generic per-command verb-button list. The four `Consumer_Node` time-travel verbs (`SEEK_FRAME`, `PAUSE`, `PLAY`, `STEP`) carry it because they are already driven by the inspector's Time Travel transport bar — the standalone buttons were redundant. Declarative, not a hardcoded name list in the JS: `Classes_CI_Node::strip_commands()` carries the flag through the catalog (default-omitted when false/absent, like `multiple`), and the inspector filters out any command whose `hidden` is truthy. The Time Travel panel is unaffected (it builds its own invoke transport bar). Non-transport config verbs (`set_snapshot_node`, `set_line_mode`, …) stay visible.
- **`Consumer_Node` exposes its poll state as a `polling` signal in `dump_metadata` (`INIT` | `ACTIVE` | `PAUSED`), and the topology console consumes it.** `arguments()`/`play()` set `ACTIVE`, `pause()` and now `step()` set `PAUSED` (via `set_state('POLLING', …)`); `dump_metadata()` reports it as `polling` (default `INIT`). `step()` previously stopped its timer — leaving the consumer genuinely paused — but never updated the signal, so a direct STEP left `polling` stale at `ACTIVE`; it now sets `PAUSED` consistent with `pause()`. The console threads the field onto the node (`parseMetadata`, present-only) and uses it as the source of truth for the Time Travel transport: the Inspector passes `paused={ 'PAUSED' === node.polling }` to `TimeTravelPanel`, which syncs the gate to the signal with an **optimistic override** (a PAUSE/PLAY click flips the gate instantly for feedback, then the next metadata signal reconciles it). The graph canvas shows a ⏸ badge (tokenized `--brass`) on the card of any node whose `polling === 'PAUSED'`, so a held consumer is visible without selecting it.
- **`Consumer_Node` reports its cursor POSITION as `at_frame` + `on_frame` in `dump_metadata`, describing where the cursor sits relative to the offsetlog keyframes for BOTH live status and time-travel — so a quiet live consumer reads "on frame N" and the topology console's Time Travel panel survives a remount.** A single pair replaces the prior `parked_frame`/`stepped`. `at_frame` (int|null) is the keyframe the cursor is at-or-just-past — its current checkpoint: `$this->rewound_to` when seeked, else the NEWEST offsetlog frame id when live (the cursor reads forward from its last checkpoint), null only when there are no frames yet. `on_frame` (bool) is whether the cursor sits exactly on `at_frame`'s committed position vs has advanced past it — seeked it's `! stepped_since_seek`, live it's `cursor == last checkpoint` (a QUIET consumer sits ON its last checkpoint; an actively-reading one is past it). Because `at_frame` tracks the newest frame while live, the panel now reads a quiet live consumer as "**on frame N**" instead of the old "live — after frame N". The private `$stepped_since_seek` flag (set by `step()`, cleared by `seek_frame()`/`play()`) is retained to feed `on_frame`'s seeked case. `parseMetadata` threads `at_frame`/`on_frame` onto the node present-only (`'at_frame' in meta`, since null is meaningful), the Inspector passes them to `TimeTravelPanel` as `atFrameSignal` / `onFrameSignal` (alongside `paused`), and the panel SEEDS `atFrame` / `onFrame` from those signals on mount and RECONCILES them on each signal change — mirroring the existing `paused` optimistic-override pattern. Rewind snaps onto `at_frame` when off it, else steps to the previous keyframe; fast-forward seeks the next; the position label reads "on frame N" / "between frame N and M" (paused) / "after frame N" (live, reading ahead); follow-head falls out for free (live ⇒ `at_frame` = newest each poll). Transport clicks still drive the position optimistically; the next poll's signal reconciles.
- **Read-only "Effective Configuration" panel on the Nodes Runtime settings page.** Below the settings form, a `widefat` table now reports, per substrate setting: the stored option value (or "— (file default)" when unset), the value the next worker will load (overlay-resolved `Config::load_config()`), any active `Options_Overlay` override, and the live restart impact — "Applies on next supervisor tick" for `supervisor_only`, "Takes effect immediately" for no-restart fields, or "Restarts: <topologies>" (resolved through `Restart_Planner::topologies_for()` against the live topology graphs; "Restarts: (no active consumer)" when the classification matches no active topology). `Admin::render_effective_config_section()` (hooked to `newspack_nodes/settings_after_form`) renders it. Large array-valued settings (e.g. an event-logger hook list with hundreds of entries) are summarized as a count + first-6 sample (`"<n> values: a, b, … (+<rest> more)"`) so they can't dominate the row; small arrays (≤6) render in full and an empty array shows "(none)". An associative-array setting (e.g. `custom_events` `{event => true}`) shows its keys, not the `true`→`1` values.

### Changed

- **`Command_Interpreter_Node::resolve_class()` is memoized.** It loops the registered namespace prefixes doing `class_exists` + `new ReflectionClass(...)->isAbstract()` per candidate, and `Restart_Planner` calls it once per graph node per active topology — re-resolving the same handful of types (`Partition`, `Topic`, `Log`, …) every time. It now caches **successes only** (type → FQCN) in a static map; misses are NOT cached, so a type resolvable only after a later `register_namespace()` still resolves, and since registration is append-only + resolution is first-match, a cached FQCN can never go stale — no invalidation hook needed.

- **Single-writer Partition rotation reads the warm segment cache instead of force-scanning.** A `void_warranty()` (single-writer, no-peer) Partition — the Consumer's offsetlog, which rotates on every ~30s checkpoint with `OFFSETLOG_SEGMENT_SIZE=1` — was calling `scandir()` on every `do_rotate()` for peer-detection it can never need. `do_rotate()` now force-refreshes only for multi-writer partitions (`get_segments( ! $warranty_voided )`), and `get_segments()` treats a warranty-voided log's cache as never-stale (no peer can invalidate it, and every in-process modifier — rotate, cleanup, `truncate_after`, the size-sync, the scan path — keeps it in sync). Eliminates one `scandir` per checkpoint (and from `dump_metadata` poll reads, finally honouring its "no scandir on the poll path" promise). Multi-writer partitions are unchanged — they still force-refresh to detect a peer's rotation. A `Partition_Node::$scandir` test seam (lazily-defaulted to the real `scandir`) makes the no-rescan behaviour assertable; production is unaffected when it's null.

- **The shared chrome `ConnectionBanner` + `DevtoolsTabHost` now reskin under decorative themes.** Both `src/shared/` stylesheets used fixed `var(--np-*)` tokens, so they stayed Newspack-coloured under the console-selected skins. `ConnectionBanner.scss` now paints the universal `--oxide` error fill with `--paper` text — `--paper` is the surface colour, so it contrasts with `--oxide` on every skin (a fixed white would have vanished on bright-oxide skins like CRT's lime); `DevtoolsTabHost.scss`'s focus ring + active-tab underline use the universal `--cyan` accent. Under Newspack the tokens resolve to exactly the prior values (look identical), and literal fallbacks keep the prior look for a host without the token sheet. Consumers that inline these shared styles (`newspack-event-logger-nodes`) pick up the reskin on their next build.

- **De-god the Vault admin dashboard graph.** The single `vault:view` node that held the whole model (credential list + test results + loading/error + every verb's pending replies) is decomposed into two focused per-concern views, each behind its own receiver `Tee` so the debug overlay shows reply traffic per concern: `vault:list` (`VaultListViewNode`, fed by list/add/update/delete) and `vault:test` (`VaultTestViewNode`, per-server connection-probe results, correlated by server id). Built on the shared `SliceViewNode` base + its optional `PendingReplies` settle path; `VaultAdmin` reads each view via its own `useNodeState`. CRUD behavior unchanged; the old god node is removed.
- **De-god the Event Aggregator status dashboard.** The single `AggregatorViewNode` (fed by one `status` verb) that held the whole status model is decomposed into two genuine per-concern slices, each with its own slice verb, `SliceViewNode` subclass, and inspectable reply path. Server: `Aggregator_CI_Node` extracts the snapshot into a shared `build_snapshot()` and adds two read-only `slice_verb()` verbs — `summary` (`{ connected, total, server_now }`, connected rollup computed server-side) and `servers_status` (the snapshot as a sequential array); the legacy `status` verb is kept verbatim for the REST shim. Client: `useAggregatorStatusGraph` is rebuilt on `useBatchedPoll` + `addSliceFetcher` with two independent `Fetcher → receiver Tee → view` paths (`summary:view`, `servers:view`) batched into one POST per tick; `AggregatorStatus` reads each slice from its own view node. Behavior-preserving; the retired `AggregatorView` god node is removed.
- **The bundled example dashboard (`PublisherInsightsPage`) now mounts the `@newspack-nodes/debug-overlay`**, making its live per-slice node graph inspectable on the page (self-gated by `isDebugEnabled`). `docs/writing-a-dashboard.md` gains a "Make it inspectable: mount the debug overlay" section covering the mount, the `storageKey` naming convention, the gate, and that the overlay reads the live `mountExospine` graph (every node/edge/counter — the payoff of building the dashboard as a genuine graph).
- **A `Timer` with an interval > 1 s now ALWAYS hitchhikes the router TIMER and throttles in `fire_cb()` / `fireCb()`.** Instead of a dedicated event-framework / `setInterval` slot, a `>= 1000` ms interval registers on `_router` and fires only once `interval_ms` has elapsed since `last_fire_time` (`Core::$now` seconds; `interval_ms / 1000`); intervals `< 1 s` keep their own slot (the ~1 s router tick is too coarse for sub-second) and the no-interval hitchhike still fires every tick. Mirrored in the PHP (`class-timer-node.php`) and JS (`timer-node.js`) runtimes.
- **`TopicProbe_Node`, `HeartbeatNode`, and `UptimeNode` dropped their bespoke last-fire self-throttles** and now rely on the base Timer throttle via `set_timer( interval_ms )` (behavior unchanged); `MetadataNode` keeps its own gate because its `cd`-driven immediate re-poll mid-interval can't be expressed by the base throttle.
- **`useBatchedPoll` accepts an `intervalMs` option** that arms the owned poll Timer at that cadence (hitchhike + throttle, batching preserved) and re-arms when it changes — the seam the Performance Dashboard's refresh-rate selector drives.
- **`FetcherNode.command_args` may be a fire-time getter, and `addSliceFetcher` accepts an `argsFn` to set one — the minimal affordance for parameterized poll slices.** Until now a `Fetcher` (and therefore every `addSliceFetcher` slice) emitted a STATIC command-args string fixed at construction, so the batched-poll toolkit could only drive parameterless verbs (`counts`/`top`/`accumulated`). A poll dashboard whose verbs take live, UI-state-driven args (a perf URL list's `--sort`/`--order`/`--search`/`--offset`/`--server`, an overview's `--server`/`--breakdown`) had no way to track that state without re-wiring the graph each change. `FetcherNode.fill()` now resolves `command_args` at fire time: if it's a FUNCTION it CALLS it each tick to get the current args string (a non-string return coerces to `''`); a static string stays byte-identical to before (only opt-in callers pass a getter — every existing static-args test is unchanged and green). `addSliceFetcher` gains an optional `argsFn` that, when supplied, assigns the Fetcher's `command_args` to that getter; without it the Fetcher keeps the static (empty) args. This unblocks the Performance Dashboard's overview/urls poll slices (dashboard-restructure D1b) — the React hook gives each Fetcher a getter that reads the current filter/sort/page state, so each batched tick emits live args.
- **The hub's Worker Status / Topology Manager dashboard (`useTopologyManager`, driving Overview) is rebuilt as a genuine node graph on the `useBatchedPoll` + `addSliceFetcher` toolkit (plan D4).** The hook dropped the `useDashboardGraph` one-view-+-one-poll shortcut for `useBatchedPoll`, and now wires its two slices with `addSliceFetcher`: `topologymanager:timer → topologymanager:tee → { fetch-workers, fetch-topologies }`, each fanned to its own receiver Tee → view, so a tick's two commands batch into ONE HttpOut POST and each slice's reply has an independent pivot path. The 244-line `WorkerStatusTransformNode` enrich-join moves onto a graph EDGE via `addSliceFetcher`'s `transform` slot (the `workerstatus:in → workerstatus:view` edge) instead of being hand-wired as a separate `transform.target = view` hop. SERVER APPROACH: `dump_graph` stays ONE verb — its four sections (workers/consumers/logs/graph) must be joined from one coherent atomic snapshot (`reconstructWorkers`), so it can't be shattered into independently-timed slice verbs; it's fanned to one transform→view on the client. `topologies list` is genuinely independent → its own slice. (Neither needs a server change; the existing `Service_CI_Node::slice_verb()` builder is in place for CIs that genuinely do split.) The three mutations (`restart`/`activate`/`deactivate`) are now on-demand, graph-visible commands routed through `_shell/_http/<ci>` (so the debug overlay's `connect _shell` sees them) carrying an ID correlator that settles the matching reply on the view's `PendingReplies` — replacing the old hidden `_http`-only `interpreter.fill`. UI animation state stays out of the polling model: the `removingSegments` 400ms slide-out clear remains the `workerstatus:view` node's own UI concern, not a slice. The hook's return contract is unchanged (`{ topologies, supervisor, currentTime, readRate, writeRate, logPartitions, activate, deactivate, restart, connected }`), so `Overview.js` and its children render identically — the existing `useTopologyManager` jest suite passes as the behavior-preserving regression gate, plus new structural tests assert the genuine-graph wiring (toolkit boundary, transform-on-edge, one-batched-POST-per-tick).
- **`useBatchedPoll` gains an immediate first-paint poll on mount and a `paused` option.** The hook now fires ONE batched poll on mount (matching the old `useDashboardGraph`'s immediate-on-mount behavior — without it a dashboard waited a whole interval for its first data), gated on visible-AND-not-paused and bracketed by the same `http.lock()`/`http.flush()` so it's one POST; and a new `paused` opt stops the Timer's router-TIMER hitchhike (like a hidden tab) so a caller can suspend polling without unmounting (Overview passes `paused: dragging` to freeze the poll during a drag-reorder). Both benefit the example dashboard too (the example's render-only page test now flushes the mount poll under `act()`).
- **`example-ai-newsletter`: `usePublisherInsightsGraph` is rebuilt on the new `useBatchedPoll` + `addSliceFetcher` toolkit — the dashboard's proof, and the §4 of `docs/writing-a-dashboard.md` honesty seam closed.** The hook's ~50 lines of hand-wired `mountExospine` boilerplate (the `_shell` Tap, the `_http` HttpOut + injectable client, the fan-out Tee, the router-hitchhike Timer, the lock/flush batching bracket, and the page-visibility start/stop) and the per-slice `SLICES.forEach` make-Fetcher / receiver-Tee / view-node block both collapse into the two helpers; the hook is now `useBatchedPoll({ build: ({ interpreter, tee }) => SLICES.forEach( s => addSliceFetcher( interpreter, { ...s, tee, target: '_shell/_http/insights-demo' } ) ), … })` (175 lines → 76). Behavior is identical — the existing example jest suite (the one-router-TIMER-tick-batches-three-commands-into-ONE-POST test and the page-visibility test) passes unchanged as the regression gate. The guide's §4 now teaches `useBatchedPoll`/`addSliceFetcher` instead of the hand-wired wiring; its "← the next boilerplate to lift" sidebar becomes "← a substrate refinement", and the §9 recap table gains the two helper rows — the last hand-wired seam in the walkthrough, lifted into the substrate the moment a third caller copied it.
- **`example-ai-newsletter`: the `insights` god-verb on `Insights_CI_Demo_Node` is split into three slice verbs — `counts`, `top`, `accumulated`.** A god command is as bad as a god node: the one `insights` verb read the offsetlog snapshot and returned the whole `{sources, top, accumulated}` model. Now each verb returns ONE slice (`counts` → `{"sources":{…}}`, `top` → `{"top":[…]}` (top-10 by score), `accumulated` → `{"accumulated":N}`), so the dashboard fetches each independently — one `Fetcher` per verb, all batched into one POST per tick. The offsetlog read is extracted into a memoized per-request reader (`items()` calls `read_snapshot_items()` ONCE behind the new `$read_items` test seam, then caches the flattened items), so the three batched verbs share a single glob+unpack instead of reading thrice. Each verb still calls `require_manage_options()` (via the `Service_CI_Node` gate). `build_insights_json()` and the `insights` verb are removed; `read_insights_model()` stays for the end-to-end Pipeline test and is now built from the shared `read_snapshot_items()` + per-slice shapers (`shape_sources`/`shape_top`), never duplicating the glob/merge logic.
- **The `Consumer_Node` time-travel READ surface is consolidated into `dump_metadata`.** The three read-only request verbs `LIST_FRAMES`, `READ_STATE`, and `GET_OFFSET` are removed; their data now rides the existing canvas-poll payload via `Consumer_Node::dump_metadata()`, which the debugger UI already round-trips: `frames` is the offsetlog segment list `[{id,size}]` (one checkpoint = one segment = one keyframe; empty for ephemeral/no-offsetlog consumers) and `cursor` is the live source position `{seg,off}`. This is cheap — it reads only the warm `segments_cache` (no record reads, no `scandir` on the poll path). `SEEK_FRAME` accordingly changes from `<seg> <off>` to a single `<segment_id>` (the keyframe's offsetlog segment id from `frames[].id`): it reads that one offsetlog segment, restores the co-committed snapshot cache, and repositions the cursor to the record's source `{seg,off}`. The now-unused `list_frames()`/`read_state()`/`find_frame()`/`scan_offsetlog_entries()` methods and the `MAX_LISTED_FRAMES` constant are dropped. `GET_LAG` (and `PAUSE`/`PLAY`/`STEP`) are unchanged.
- **The topology-console Time Travel inspector panel reads from `dump_metadata` and drives the consumer's `:config` verbs through the existing invoke path** — no bespoke fetch/client. The inspector detects a consumer by the read surface it already holds (`node.frames` is an array AND `node.cursor` is present, threaded through `parseMetadata` from `dump_metadata()`), not a request verb. `TimeTravelPanel` renders a keyframe ruler (one marker per `node.frames` entry, ordered by id, the selected frame flagged current) plus a transport bar — rewind ⏮ / pause ⏸ / step ▌▶ / play ▶ / fast-forward ⏭ — where each button fires `onAction('invoke', nodeId, …)` as the consumer's `PAUSE` / `PLAY` / `STEP` / `SEEK_FRAME <segment_id>` `:config` commands. Frame selection is a **client-side model** that follows the live head by default — the newest frame is current and tracks live as new keyframes append. Rewind/FF PARK on a frame (`pinnedId`, walking the `node.frames` list prev/next, disabled at the ends — a paused keyframe scrub); PLAY un-parks (go live), as does a parked frame ageing out of the retained window. The live `cursor` ({seg,off}) is **informational only** and does NOT drive selection: a frame id is its offsetlog segment id (monotonic, climbs forever), an independent number space from the source-partition `cursor.seg`, so the two coincide only near zero. No state blob is rendered (`save_state` is a large nested object). The SLICE-2a bespoke request path is removed: `useConsumerFrames`, the `CommandClient.request()` / `buildRequestMessage()` additions, and both `unwrapRequestResponse` copies (`src/shared/utils` + `src/topology-console/utils`) are deleted along with the `LIST_FRAMES` / `READ_STATE` / `GET_OFFSET` references.
- **The Time Travel transport panel is now pause-gated with snap-to-keyframe rewind/fast-forward and a position readout.** PAUSE gates the whole bar: while following the head only ⏸ Pause is enabled (rewind / step / fast-forward / play are grayed); once paused, ⏸ disables and the rest enable subject to their own edge-disables. The `pinnedId` follow-head model is replaced by two pieces of client state — `parkedFrameId` (null = live/following-head; clamps back to null when the parked frame ages out of the retained window) and `steppedSincePark` (set by STEP, meaning the cursor now sits between keyframes). Rewind ⏮ snaps to keyframes: from live it lands on the NEWEST keyframe; after a STEP it snaps BACK to the current keyframe (not the one before); otherwise it walks to the previous keyframe (disabled on the oldest when not stepped). Fast-forward ⏭ walks to the next keyframe (disabled while live or on the newest). STEP keeps the panel paused and sets `steppedSincePark`; PLAY resumes following the head (un-parks, clears stepped). A new position line reports where the cursor sits ("live — after frame N", "on frame N", "between frame N and M", "after frame N", or "stepped past frame N"), and the current ruler marker carries a `--stepped` modifier when the cursor is between keyframes. The props contract (`frames`, `cursor`, `onTransport`) and the follow-head + clamp behavior are unchanged; verbs still fire as bare `onTransport('PAUSE'|'PLAY'|'STEP','')` and `onTransport('SEEK_FRAME', String(id))`.
- **The interactive `wp nodes cli` Dumper (`_output`) now sinks to the command interpreter.** A reply that lands on the cli's output node continues into the interpreter → router instead of terminating at the Dumper.
- **The hub's control buttons now follow the same stock-WP-under-Newspack / themed-under-decorative rule as the rest.** The Overview "+ New Topology", "Fold all" / "Unfold all", per-row "Edit" and "Restart" (↻), the Vault "+ Add Server", and the Raw Logs Pause/Clear buttons were custom-classed `<button>`s (never WP-stock); they now carry the WP `.button` (/`button-small`) classes in markup, and their bespoke `secondary-button`/`dark-button` SCSS visuals are dropped (layout kept) so WP core styles them under Newspack and the shared `.topology-app:not(.theme-newspack)…` rule themes them under decorative skins. (The Restart ↻ keeps its amber `--brass` icon cue under Newspack.)
- **Hub buttons are stock WP under the Newspack/Newspack-Brand themes and themed under the decorative themes.** One shared `devtools-hub.scss` ruleset — scoped to `.newspack-nodes-theme:not(.theme-newspack):not(.theme-newspack-brand)` and drawn from the universal theme tokens (`--cyan`/`--paper`/`--ink-3`/`--oxide`/…) — replaces the Vault tab's per-surface `.button` re-skins. Under the two Newspack skins the selector doesn't match, so buttons fall through to stock WP core-ui; under every decorative theme they take that theme's colors. Defined once for the whole hub instead of per tab.
- **The Event Aggregator hub tab reads the universal theme tokens, so it re-skins under every theme** (identical under Newspack). Its `base.scss` token layer and `aggregator-status.scss` read `--paper`/`--ink`/`--cyan`/`--sage`/`--brass`/`--oxide` and the new `--*-subtle` tints instead of the Newspack-only `--np-*` colors.
- **The Event Dashboards hub tab (overview, topology rows, worker status, summary cards, raw logs) reads the universal theme tokens, so it re-skins under every theme.** Its `base.scss` token layer and the per-surface SCSS read `--paper`/`--ink`/`--cyan`/`--sage`/`--brass`/`--oxide` + the `--*-subtle` tints; the summary-card chart accents use the new `--chart-*` tokens. The intentionally-dark `$term-*` log-surface literals are unchanged.
- **The debug overlay's status accents read the universal theme tokens** (`--brass`/`--oxide`/`--paper-2`), so its warning/error markers re-skin with the active theme; the existing hex fallbacks are kept.
- **Topic chart series colors re-skin with the active hub theme.** `TopicsChart` resolves the theme's `--chart-1..8` tokens at render time (via `getComputedStyle` off the themed `.topology-app` root) and re-resolves on theme change — a new `useThemeToken` hook tracks the live skin through the `storage` event and a `MutationObserver` on the themed ancestor's class. The shared 20-color `PALETTE` remains the fallback when the tokens are absent.
- **The Vault tab surfaces are driven from the universal theme tokens, so the whole tab re-skins under every theme** (identical under Newspack, which aliases the universal tokens to its `--np-*`). Its table/header/row/modal styling reads `--paper`/`--paper-2`/`--paper-shadow`/`--ink`/`--ink-3` instead of the Newspack-only `--np-*` color tokens; the structural `--np-radius-md` is unchanged.
- **The per-field config `overlay` opt-out is removed; every setting now overlays the config file uniformly.** `Config_System\Field` no longer takes an `overlay` flag and no longer exposes `is_overlaid()` — `Schema::overlay_keys()` returns every field with a non-empty key (every settable option is an overlay key). The substrate's three `remote_*` settings (`remote_num_segments`/`remote_segment_size`/`remote_max_lifespan`) are no longer special-cased as direct-read: they overlay into `Config::load_config()` like every other setting, so a stored `remote_*` resolves through the overlay and an unset one carries the config-file default. `Settings_Renderer`'s Effective/Override columns are now uniform across all fields (no `overlay`-flag branch). `Options_Overlay::apply()` and `Config::load_config()` are unchanged — they simply receive the now-complete key list.
- **The "Effective Configuration" panel now lives in the shared `Config_System\Settings_Renderer`** as `effective_config_rows( Schema, prefix, effective )` + `render_effective_config_section( Schema, prefix, effective )` (plus the restart-impact and value-formatting helpers), so both `newspack-nodes` and `newspack-event-logger-nodes` render the panel from one implementation instead of each carrying its own copy. The substrate `Admin::render_effective_config_section()` is now a thin delegate; the per-row data shape is exercised against the shared renderer directly. The Effective column reports the overlay-resolved `Config::load_config()` value for every field — an unset setting shows its file default, uniform across all fields instead of a blank cell.

### Fixed

- **Dashboard stylesheets cache-bust on their own content hash.** `Admin::enqueue_react_page()` enqueued `index.css` with the JS bundle's `index.asset.php` version, so a SCSS-only rebuild (JS hash unchanged) left the stylesheet behind a stale `?ver=` — a CSS-only change needed a hard-refresh to land. It now versions the stylesheet by `md5_file(index.css)`, independent of the script bundle — centralized in the reusable `Admin::css_cache_version()` helper, which the sibling plugins (event-logger-nodes, pyrobase, nuclear-gyrobase) route their dashboard/admin stylesheets through too.
- **The shared `.entry-rid` (request-id link) no longer goes invisible on dark log surfaces.** `src/shared/styles/_components.scss`'s `.entry-rid` used the un-lightened `var(--cyan)` accent with no fallback; on standalone dashboard pages `--cyan` is undefined (only the topology-console theme defines it), so the `color-mix` went invalid and the link inherited the `#1e1e1e` log background. It now uses `color-mix(in srgb, var(--cyan, #003da5) 58%, white)` — lightened to read on the dark surface, Cobalt-floored so it can never go invalid. Fixes the request-id in every dashboard that renders the shared log-entry component (Request Log, Gyroscope, …).
- **`example-ai-newsletter` Publisher Insights: a string-VALUE transport error no longer leaves a slice widget blank/stale, and polling pauses while the tab is hidden.** `SliceViewNode.fill()` early-returned on any non-object VALUE *before* the `TM_ERROR` branch, so a Router-minted error (`NOT_AVAILABLE\n` — a bare STRING VALUE with the TM_ERROR bit) was silently swallowed; the TM_ERROR branch now runs first regardless of VALUE shape (string passed straight to `errorMessage`, object via `value.payload`), and only a *non-error* non-object reply is ignored (keeps the prior slice). `usePublisherInsightsGraph` re-adds the page-visibility gating the rebuild dropped: via the shared `usePageVisibility`, a HIDDEN tab `stopTimer()`s the `insights:timer` (unregisters it from the router TIMER, so a tick fans out to nothing and no HttpOut POST goes out), and becoming VISIBLE `setTimer()`s it again — the visible 1s router-tick cadence and the one-POST-per-tick lock/flush batching are unchanged. The now-redundant per-slice `require_manage_options()` in `Insights_CI_Demo_Node` is dropped (the `Service_CI_Node` base already wraps every verb with the gate; an added negative test proves the auth branch stays covered).
- **`Partition_Node` now maintains its segments cache through rotation instead of nulling it and re-scanning.** `do_rotate()` used to null `segments_cache` in both the adopt and spawn branches right after the line-351 force-scan already populated it with the truth, and `cleanup_segments()` force-scanned the directory a SECOND time (so a single rotation cost two `scandir`s) before discarding its own correctly-pruned list by nulling the cache too. With `touch_segments_cache()` bailing on a null cache, a rotate-heavy partition (the offsetlog runs `segment_size=1`, rotating every checkpoint) left `segments_cache` perpetually null, so every read re-`scandir`'d. Now the adopt branch keeps the just-scanned cache, the spawn branch appends the new empty segment to it, and `cleanup_segments()` prunes the maintained cache in place (force-scanning only as a fallback when the cache is null, for standalone callers) and stores the pruned list back — one directory scan per rotation, cache stays warm. Multi-writer staleness is still governed by the existing `get_segments()` `SEGMENT_CACHE_TTL` and `maybe_rescan_segments()` drift check (unchanged), so the per-rotate nulling was redundant.
- **The Console transcript fills to the tab bar, its resize handle stays reachable, and Chrome no longer shows a spurious horizontal scrollbar.** The console transcript's ceiling is `replCeilingFromAppHeight()`, derived from the MEASURED canvas-frame height — but it still subtracted a `64px` header row, stale since the "shared header above the tabs" refactor collapsed the console's own header row to `0` (the header moved up out of the canvas frame). That stranded the maximized transcript 64px below the tab bar. The ceiling is now `appHeight − repl-bar(38) − a small handle reserve`; the reserve is `0` for the console (the frame is measured exactly, so the transcript top lands precisely at the canvas top) and `4` for the debug overlay (`replMaxHeight()`, which hardcodes its header/bar and needs to absorb ~4px of slop), in both cases landing the transcript at the tab bar with its 6px resize handle still grabbable. `ReplFooter`'s `FIXED_CHROME_PX` (now `174` = `32 WP-bar + 64 header + 40 tab-bar + 38 repl-bar`) is the **pre-layout fallback only** — both surfaces pass an explicit measured `maxHeightPx`. Separately, the transcript now sets `overflow: hidden auto` instead of `overflow-y: auto`: with only the y-axis declared, the spec forces `overflow-x` to compute to `auto`, so a few px of spurious overflow (the sticky `[✕ ▼]` actions corridor / sub-pixel slop) gave Chrome a horizontal scrollbar (Firefox hid it behind overlay scrollbars).
- **The Tap node's palette glyph is distinct from Tee again.** The palette builds each item's class as `topology-palette__item--<shell_name>` (lowercased), and `&__item--tee &__glyph::after` carried a two-dot box-shadow that `&__item--tap` lacked — so a Tap node fell through to the bare glyph and rendered identically to a Tee. Added the matching `&__item--tap &__glyph::after` rule.
- **The Topology Settings panel drops from under the toolbar and paints over the hub header.** Its `top: 56px` was calibrated for when the SAVE/SETTINGS toolbar lived inside the console canvas; the "one shared header" refactor moved that toolbar up to the shared header above the tabs. The panel is now `position: fixed` and rendered through a `createPortal` to the hub root. The hub is `display:flex`, so its header is a flex item whose `z-index` establishes a stacking context — and the panel's in-tree home (the canvas frame, `z-index: 2`) is a LOWER context, so NO `z-index` on the panel could ever paint it above the header. Portaling to the hub root (themed by inheritance, and the `z:99` context where the panel's own `z-index` finally wins) frees it to drop from under the toolbar and over the header.
- **Worker segment bars: the "beyond" (live-past-probe) fill is readable on every skin.** It was `color-mix(--sage-subtle, white)`, which — built from an already-faint tint and washed toward white — went near-white and vanished on the light Newspack track. It now uses the neutral `--ink-4` mid-gray (the "soft light-mid gray" its own comment describes), which reads against the track in both light and dark skins.
- **The Vault "Add Server" modal renders correctly on dark themes.** Its WP-native form inputs were stark white and the card had no edge against the dimmed backdrop. The inputs now read the theme tokens (`--paper-2`/`--ink`/`--paper-shadow`, with an `--ink-3` placeholder) and the card gets a `--paper-shadow` border, so the dialog is legible and self-contained under decorative skins (unchanged under Newspack). Its form labels (`.form-table th`, which WP core hard-sets to a dark color) re-theme to `--ink` so they don't go dark-on-dark.
- **The Topology Settings panel's inputs read the theme tokens too.** Its `var name = value` frontmatter fields were WP-white; they now use `--paper`/`--ink`/`--paper-shadow` (with an `--ink-3` placeholder) so the panel is legible on dark skins.
- **Aggregator status pills (CONNECTED / SUCCESS / connecting / backoff / error / …) are readable in every theme.** Same root cause as the worker-status badges — they paired hardcoded `#fff`/`#000` text with solid or `--sage-subtle` fills. Each `.aggregator-status-badge` / `.aggregator-heartbeat-badge` state now uses the accent-on-its-own-tint pattern (`--sage`/`--cyan`/`--brass`/`--oxide` on the matching `--*-subtle`, and `--ink-3` on `--paper-3` for the inactive states), so text always contrasts its fill across skins.
- **Worker-status badges (RUN / ALL RUN / per-partition) are readable in every theme.** They paired a hardcoded white text with a `--sage-subtle` fill that follows paper luminance — so under the light Newspack skin it was white-on-near-white. Each state badge now uses the accent-on-its-own-tint pattern (`--sage` on `--sage-subtle`, `--oxide` on `--oxide-subtle`, `--ink-3` on `--paper-3`) — both tokens track the theme, so the text always contrasts its fill (dark accent on light tint under Newspack, bright accent on dark tint under decorative skins). No hardcoded `#fff`.
- **The Vault server table no longer shows light zebra stripes under dark themes.** WP core stripes odd rows with a hardcoded `#f6f7f7` that ignores the theme; the table now clears that so rows sit on the themed surface (hover still tints).
- **Hub buttons now actually fall through to stock WP under the Newspack skins.** The shared themed-button rule was gated on `.newspack-nodes-theme:not(.theme-newspack)`, but `.newspack-nodes-theme` is also set on inner hub wrappers (e.g. `.nodes-devtools-hub`) that carry no theme class — so the `:not()` matched under the Newspack skin too, leaving Vault's Test/Remove buttons themed instead of stock. The rule is now gated on `.topology-app:not(.theme-newspack):not(.theme-newspack-brand)` — the single themed root that carries the `theme-<slug>` class — so Newspack skins render stock WP buttons and decorative skins get themed ones.
- **Worker-restart-on-save targeted dead worker-group labels (`request-workers`/`job-workers`) that never matched a live lock dir, so storage-geometry/memcache saves silently never restarted workers.** Classification is now by consumer node type, resolved to live topologies: `num_segments`/`segment_size`/`max_lifespan` restart any active topology that runs a `Partition`/`Topic`/`Log`, while `base_directory`/`memcache_servers` (process-wide) restart every active topology. `Admin::maybe_request_worker_restart()` resolves the field's node-type classification through `Restart_Planner` against the live topology graphs and touches each matching topology's per-partition lock dir. The unused `newspack_nodes/worker_restart_groups` filter (no consumers in any plugin) is removed.

### Removed

- **The console grid's dead 0-height `header` row is removed.** The brand header moved to the shared hub/panel header above the tabs, but the `.topology-app` CSS grid still reserved a collapsed `0`-height `header` track + `header` grid-area (in all four layout variants) plus a now-inert `grid-area: header` on `.topology-header`. The `0` track contributed no height, so the visible layout is byte-identical — pure tidy. A `replCeiling.test.js` guard asserts the dead row/area stays gone, and the stale debug-overlay comment that justified its drag-wrapper `display:contents` by the (now-removed) grid-area is corrected.

- **The unused `newspack_nodes/before_worker_spawn` action is removed** from `Topology_Registry::spawn_worker()`. Its only consumer (the event-logger StreamMerger rewrite + RemoteManager worker-runtime init) was migrated off the hook long ago, leaving the `do_action` firing on every spawn with no listeners in any plugin. (`newspack_nodes/before_supervisor_run` / `after_supervisor_run` are unaffected — they remain in use by `newspack-event-logger-nodes` to wrap the supervisor tick in a `Log_Manager` job context.)

## [0.20.1] - 2026-06-24

### Fixed

- **The Aggregator dashboard's per-partition "Connected" time no longer sticks at "1s ago".** `Remote_Link_Node::publish_status()` stamped `last_connection_attempt` to `Core::$now` on every tick, so the timestamp tracked the 1-second status-publish cadence instead of the actual connection — a healthy, long-lived link read "connected 1s ago" forever. It now reflects the genuine connect time: `SSE_In_Node::connection()` exposes `last_attempt` (set once per socket open, `null` until the first attempt), and `publish_status()` passes that through verbatim — no `Core::$now` fallback, so an un-attempted link reads `-` rather than a fabricated "now".
- **The topology console's header mode buttons (NEW / OPEN / SAVE / SETTINGS / DELETE / EDIT / LIVE) regained their padding.** The debug-overlay close-X tightening dropped `padding: 7px 14px` from the shared `.topology-mode__btn` base rule, which left every hub-header button cramped. The padding is restored on the base and overridden to `0` only on the overlay's `&--close` variant, so the X stays tight without flattening the hub buttons.

## [0.20.0] - 2026-06-24

### Added

- **New `Tap` node: a `Tee` that also passes the message through.** Like a `Tee` it fans a copy out to each live target (pruning a target only when its head node is gone), then forwards the original message on to its sink — so it can sit inline on a pipe to branch/observe traffic without diverting it. As a `Tee` subclass it inherits the family's subclass-aware treatment (catalog `is_tee`, `'tee'` graph kind, the multi-target Inspector editor + tail/tap button).
- **Debug overlay Overview tab: a Debug card + a classified message list below the rate charts.** `Core.stderr` now classifies every line by the Tachikoma prefix convention — `WARNING:` → warning, `ERROR:` → error, anything else → debug — and `IoTelemetry` keeps a bounded ring of the recent lines (`{ level, text }`, per page-load) alongside the counters. The Overview tab gains a Debug count card next to Warnings/Errors and renders the debug/warning/error messages (newest first, level-tinted) under the graphs.

### Changed

- **`Tee` special-casing is now subclass-aware, so any `Tee` subclass (e.g. `Tap`) gets the fan-out treatment.** Four sites stopped matching the `Tee` class name as an exact string and now key off the actual Tee lineage / the array-target signal: `Topology_Registry::graph_for()` promotes an unknown make_node type whose resolved FQCN `is_a` `Tee_Node` to kind `'tee'`; `Consumer_Node::resolve_downstream_targets()` expands a downstream node via `instanceof Tee_Node` (and its non-array fallback now reports the node's real class, not a hardcoded `'Tee'`); and the topology console Inspector renders the multi-target editor + the tail/tap button from a catalog-level `is_tee` flag (`Classes_CI_Node` flags every class whose FQCN `is_a` `Tee_Node`), keyed in both edit and view modes — with the runtime `Array.isArray( node.target )` shape as a fallback — rather than the exact `node.class === 'Tee'`. The type→FQCN resolution that `make_node()` inlined is extracted to a reusable `Command_Interpreter_Node::resolve_class()` (concrete Node subclasses only).
- **The overlay Overview's live I/O rate counters now smooth the same way as the Raw Logs lines/s.** Extracted that readout's windowed-average + EMA math into a shared `RateSmoother` (10-second per-second-bucketed window, 0.1 EMA) that both the Raw Logs `lps` and the overlay's byte/message In/Out rates consume, so they read steady instead of spiky and can't drift apart. Replaces the overlay's previous 1-second sliding-window rate; a Reset stats / telemetry reset drops the smoothed rate straight to zero (a backward counter rebaselines the smoother).
- **Overview tab cards: compact values, fixed widths, and 20Hz live updates.** The compact byte/rate/count formatters now drop the decimal once the in-unit value reaches 10 ("46 KB/s", never "46.4 KB/s"), keeping the number to ≤3 characters; the widest cards are fixed-width (byte rate + total bytes 200px, message rate + total messages 140px) so the row no longer reflows as values change; and the cards now refresh at 20Hz off the raw counters with a 1-second sliding-window rate, while the two charts stay on the 5-second sampled series (the message list is memoized so the fast refresh doesn't re-reconcile it). A **Reset stats** button clears the counters, rate, series, and message list (and the persisted series) without dropping the live subscription, via a new `IoTelemetry.clear()`. The ↓/↑ in/out numbers each sit in a fixed-width, right-aligned cell so the arrows hold position as the digit count changes instead of bouncing.
- **The debug overlay drags + resizes smoothly, regardless of panel size or the page behind it.** Two changes: (1) `useDebugFrame` no longer calls `setFrame` on every pointermove — it writes the panel's style directly during the gesture (a `transform: translate()` for moves, the box dimensions for resizes) and commits the frame to React state once, on pointerup, so the panel subtree doesn't re-render per frame. (2) The panel's drop shadow is lifted for the duration of the gesture (an `is-dragging` class): the `0 8px 40px` shadow extends past the panel, so every frame it moved the browser had to repaint the blurred page *behind* it — the real jank, proportional to how busy that page was (a static page never stuttered). Restored on pointerup. (3) The clamp bounds (admin-bar height, menu width, viewport) are read **once at gesture start**, not on every pointermove — those reads (`offsetHeight`/`clientWidth`) force a synchronous reflow, which stutters badly while a live page (e.g. the perf dashboard) is constantly dirtying its layout, but is invisible on a static page. Same clamping + debounced persistence.
- **Runtime log emits now carry a `WARNING:` / `ERROR:` prefix so the overlay classifies them correctly.** Audited the JS runtime's `stderr` / `printLessOften` call sites: a dropped malformed SSE frame, a Tee target that threw, and an invalid command struct are `WARNING:`; a failed `HttpOut` POST and a TM_NOREPLY command error are `ERROR:`. Unprefixed lines stay debug. (The emits that already had a prefix — the stamp-message guards, the forgot-to-unregister notices — are unchanged.)
- **The debug overlay's close button now uses the standard WP `close` icon** (the X ELN/pyrobase modals use) instead of the thin `×` text glyph — inlined as an SVG since `@wordpress/icons` isn't a nodes dependency.
- **`settings-sync` `add_setting` is now repeatable per local option.** The registry keys a list of `{to, remote}` mappings per local option (was a single mapping that the next `add_setting` overwrote), so one local option can fan out to several spoke targets — e.g. a `remote_*` setting seeding both the spoke's stripped option AND its own `remote_*` copy, so the value propagates onward to the spoke's spokes. Exact-duplicate mappings are deduped (idempotent topology reload); `push()` emits one `set` per mapping.

### Fixed

- **`Tee`/`Tap` no longer prune a path-shaped target on the first fill.** The dead-target prune checked `Core::node()` against the FULL target path while computing an unused head segment, so a path-shaped target like `_sse/workers` (only `_sse` is registered) returned null and was pruned immediately — killing `connect_node <tee>` tail/tap fan-out. Both `Tee_Node`/`Tap_Node` (PHP) and `tee-node.js`/`tap-node.js` now keep a target iff its HEAD node is registered.
- **Nodes that mint messages now stamp `FROM` with their own identity.** `workerstatus:transform` emitted its `{action:'model'}` push to the view with an empty `FROM`; it now stamps its own node name. The Raw Logs control messages (`pause`/`select`/`logs`) now carry `FROM=rawlogs:view` like their sibling commands. The remote-IPC `connect_worker_input` mount command now stamps the link's own name (`FROM` always prepends, so the server still builds its boundary path on top). The two remaining un-stamped mints are deliberate: `CommandClient.buildMessage` (a synchronous UI→server RPC with no generating node — its reply is the awaited return value, not routed by `FROM`) and `Core.stderr` (the runtime's text channel, not a graph node).
- **The overlay Overview charts now clear immediately on a stats reset.** `TopicsChart`'s render bailed on an empty series *before* its canvas-clear ran, so the old lines lingered after `IoTelemetry.clear()` emptied the series; it now wipes the canvas when the series is empty (then stops), so Reset stats blanks the charts at once.
- **The overlay Console's "Reset Layout" chip no longer appears when an external view mutates the shared graph (e.g. switching the hub's main tab).** `useCanvasLayout` flagged the layout `modified: true` whenever it auto-placed a newly-appeared node — but the overlay reflects the shared process-wide `Core`, which gains/loses nodes as hub tabs (or other dashboards) mount, so any hub-tab switch tucked in new nodes and surfaced Reset Layout spuriously. Auto-placement now preserves the existing `modified` flag; only genuine user actions (drag, rename) set it. (Layouts persisted with the old stale `modified: true` keep it until the user resets that layout once — the fix prevents new occurrences.)
- **The overlay Console's "Reset Graph" chip no longer shows spuriously (it appeared only when the hub's Overview tab was active).** The hub's dashboard view-model nodes (`workerstatus:*`, `topologymanager:view`) are created by a *different* builder than the overlay's and leak into the shared process-wide `Core`; the overlay's reset-graph logic only excluded its own `reinitNames`, so it miscounted them as user-added nodes. View-model node classes now carry a static `isSystemNode` flag that `useGraphReset` excludes from the user-node count — so the chip only surfaces for genuinely user-added nodes, regardless of which builder registered the shared-Core node or which hub tab is active.
- **Time-series charts (`useTimeChart`) re-fit when their container resizes, not just the window.** They only listened for the browser `window` `resize`, so a chart inside the debug overlay stayed at its old width after a panel resize until the next data tick (up to ~5s). Now they observe their own container with a debounced `ResizeObserver` and re-fit once the resize settles — same treatment the flame graph got.
- **The page behind the overlay no longer drops sections / blanks when the pointer enters the panel.** `lockPageScroll` toggled `overflow: hidden` + a `paddingRight` gutter on `<html>`/`<body>` to pin the page while the cursor was in the overlay — but that reflows the page behind, and a live dashboard would drop sections the instant you hovered the panel. It's only actually needed on WebKit/Safari (which ignores a wheel `preventDefault()` over the SVG canvas); Chromium already blocks the scroll via the panel's wheel-eater. Gated the CSS lock to WebKit-non-Chromium so it no longer touches the page on Chromium. Also guarded the gutter computation (`innerWidth - html.clientWidth`) to a real scrollbar range (0–40px) — a 0/tiny `clientWidth` otherwise yielded a viewport-wide `paddingRight` that collapsed the page to nothing.
- **The console's PATH selector no longer clips glyph descenders.** `.topology-select` used `line-height: 1`, so the tails of `g`/`p`/`y` were cut off; bumped to `1.4` (padding trimmed to keep the control height).
- **Debug overlay's I/O Overview tab now styles correctly when embedded outside the hub.** The tab reuses the hub's `.nodes-card(s)` / `.nodes-overview` layout classes, but their styles live in the event-dashboards bundle — present on the hub page, absent on pages that merely embed the overlay (the ELN performance dashboards), so the cards rendered unstyled there. `OverviewTab` now imports those style partials directly, so the card/overview CSS ships in whatever bundle inlines the overlay (rebuild required in every embedder — the nodes hub + the three ELN performance bundles).
- **Debug overlay's tab labels are now legible when embedded outside the hub.** The shared tab bar colours its labels from `--nodes-devtools-fg`, which the hub set on its own container but the embedded overlay never did — so it fell back to `#fff` (invisible on the light panel). The overlay panel now binds `--nodes-devtools-fg` to the theme `--ink`, so the tabs read on any page that mounts the overlay.

## [0.19.0] - 2026-06-23

### Added

- **The debug overlay gains an "Overview" tab — a live I/O board, now the default tab.** It shows six metric cards (current byte rate and message rate, cumulative total messages and total bytes, all split in vs out, plus total warnings and total errors) and the same two Tachikoma-style rate panels the hub Overview carries — Message Rate and Byte Rate — but with **In/Out** series in place of per-topic. "In" is SSE frames + HTTP command responses; "out" is HTTP command requests. A new window-singleton accumulator (`runtime/io-telemetry.js`) is fed at the wire boundaries — `SseConnector`'s frame handler, `CommandClient`'s POST, and `Core.stderr` (lines opening `WARNING:` / `ERROR:`, plus inbound `TM_ERROR` frames) — surviving the overlay's per-tab node teardown and the anonymous live-SSE node swap that node-local counters can't. An always-on 5-second sampler (running whenever `nodes-debug=1`, independent of the panel being open) rolls the cumulative counters into a per-second rate ring kept to a 1-hour window (720 samples) and persisted trimmed to `localStorage`, so the charts carry history across reloads. The byte counter is unified into one `byteLength()` in `runtime/message.js` (was duplicated by `valueSize`).

- **`wp nodes ingest <topic> <file>…` replays packed segment records back through a Topic onto disk.** A rescue / restore / re-segment / re-partition tool: it reads each file line-by-line, `Message::unpacked()`s every packed record, and runs it through `Topic::fill()` — which re-partitions by the record's original KEY (or TO-pin / round-robin) and appends to the destination partition segments (it follows the newest segment, never clobbering live data). `<topic>` takes two forms. **Explicit** (carries a `{partition}`/`<partition>` token, e.g. `<config:logs_dir>/firehose.p<partition>`): trusted verbatim, config tokens resolved, count from `--num_partitions` (default 1). **Shortname** (bare `firehose`): expanded to `<config:logs_dir>/firehose.p<partition>`, count from `--num_partitions` or the global config `num_partitions` when omitted. **Segment geometry:** `--segment_size` / `--num_segments` set the destination segment size and count (e.g. to re-segment `topicprobe.p0` down to 1 MiB segments); `max_lifespan` is intentionally not exposed — re-ingested segments all carry a fresh mtime, so age-based retention is meaningless during the replay and the live writer remounts with its own. Records over PIPE_BUF (4 KB) are skipped and counted by default; `--allow_large_writes` (held write lock, enforced single-writer) or `--void_warranty` (no lock, caller-asserted single-writer) lift the cap to 10 MB (mutually exclusive). `--dry-run` samples record sizes and reports whether either large-write flag is needed, writing nothing. To route via `Topic::fill()` while honoring those flags, `Topic_Node` gains `allow_large_writes()` / `void_warranty()` that propagate the cap to every partition it materializes (current and future) — mirroring its existing `sink()` propagation.

- **Worker heartbeat now beats from inside long in-process jobs (`Event_Framework::pump()`).** A long synchronous job (e.g. a pyrobase `evTemplate` cron render) starves the drain loop, so the worker lock went un-heartbeated and `max_runtime` / restart / memory stops couldn't fire until the job returned — the 600s lock stale-timeout was a blunt backstop. The firehose write path now beats the heartbeat: `Partition::fill()` calls `Event_Framework::pump()` (just outside the `allow_large_writes` heartbeat gate), which re-runs the drain's continue-predicate (throttled to ≤1/s, off a fresh wall clock since `Core::$now` is frozen during a blocking job). If the predicate reports stop, it throws the new `Worker_Should_Stop`, which unwinds cooperatively from deep inside the job: `Job_Worker_Node` re-throws it past its per-job `Throwable` swallow (after_job still runs) and `Worker_Base::execute()` catches it as a clean stop (release + respawn). `pump()` is **opt-in per drain** (`Event_Framework::drain( …, cooperative_stop: true )`, set only by `Worker_Base`): a cli (`wp nodes cli`) or SSE-stream drain passes a generic "this loop is done" predicate it does NOT want raised as an exception, so those drains — and plain web requests — never throw. drain() save/restores the predicate so a nested drain can't disable an outer worker's seam. This keeps a genuinely-progressing chatty job's slot alive AND finally makes `max_runtime` enforceable mid-job. Limitations: it does NOT help a job blocked in a single non-writing call (no write = no pump seam), and a `Worker_Should_Stop` raised behind a `Tee_Node` or `Command_Interpreter_Node` `catch(\Throwable)` is swallowed — so the mid-job stop is guaranteed only on the direct `Log_Manager`→`Topic`→`Partition` firehose path (elsewhere the worker still stops at the next drain tick).

- **Canonical Newspack theme tokens (`newspack-nodes-theme` stylesheet) — the single source of truth for the in-product look across every dashboard.** A new `src/theme/newspack-theme.scss` defines the `--np-*` custom properties (Cobalt-led palette, Inter, light neutrals, status colors, type scale, spacing, radii, a sanctioned categorical chart palette) straight from `DESIGN.product.md`, built to `build/theme/index.css` and shipped as the `newspack-nodes-theme` style handle. The consumption model is a runtime CSS-variable cascade: a dashboard enqueues the handle as a style dependency, carries `.newspack-nodes-theme` on its root, and references `var(--np-*)` — so the three sibling plugins (this substrate, event-logger-nodes, pyrobase) share ONE definition with no cross-plugin SCSS sharing or build coupling (it works for standalone pyrobase because nodes is a hard runtime dependency). The Topology Console's `.theme-newspack` skin now `@use`s the same partial and maps its own `--paper/--ink/--cyan/…` onto the `--np-*` tokens, so the console and the dashboards can't drift.
- **Undocumented `set_skin` / `list_skins` REPL builtins replace the Topology Console skin picker.** The Header skin `<select>` is removed; skins now switch from the REPL (in both the console and the debug overlay, which share the `Shell` + local-dispatch path). `set_skin <name>` resolves a case-insensitive name against the `THEMES` registry — exact slug, then exact label, then label-prefix, then slug-prefix (so `set_skin CRT Phosphor` lands on `crt` and `set_skin Newspack` on `newspack`, not `newspack-brand`); an unrecognized name is rejected with an error and changes nothing. `list_skins` prints the registry with the live skin marked. Both emit `{ kind: 'local' }` signals the host resolves + applies via the existing global skin preference (persisted to `newspack-nodes:theme`, shared with the overlay), and are intentionally absent from `help` (they never reach the interpreter verb table). A new pure `skinCommands` helper (`resolveSkin` / `formatSkinList`) carries the resolution, and `getStoredTheme()` is now the single source for the live skin slug (collapsing the duplicate reader in `usePanelChrome`).


- **TopicProbe dashboard stream — foundation (browser data layer).** The SSE client can now request a server-side seek: `SseConnectorNode` (and so `SseInNode`/`RemoteLink`) gained a `positions` field that `start()` serializes into the `&positions=<json>` stream param the `messages/stream` endpoint already honors; `RemoteLink.connect(positions)` / `setSubscribe(subscribe, positions)` thread it through (default null → tail-seek, unchanged for existing callers). On top of it: a `TopicProbeView` node accumulates, per `offsetlog_dir`, a bounded rate+backlog series from the probe records (`rate` = Δ`bytes_read`/Δ`ts` with worker-restart counter-reset handling → never negative; `backlog` = `bytes_behind`; React-facing `setState` is time-throttled so a 24h `start` replay burst doesn't thrash), and a `useTopicProbeStream({ mode })` hook mounts one `RemoteLink` tailing `topicprobe.p0` — `mode:'history'` seeks from `start` (24h replay), `mode:'follow'` tails (a visibility reconnect always tail-follows so it never re-replays into the populated view). Foundation only — not yet wired into a tab; the Overview 24h graphs + Topologies follow consume it next.
- **Workers emit consumer stats to a shared `topicprobe` log (new `TopicProbe` node).** Every worker process now auto-mounts a `TopicProbe` (a `Timer_Node` that hitchhikes the Router TIMER, self-gated to 15s against `last_fire_time`) which sweeps its local `Consumer_Node`s — the analog of Tachikoma's TopicProbe over `%Tachikoma::Nodes` — and writes one small `TM_STRUCT` record **per consumer** into `{base}/logs/topicprobe.p0` (a 5 MiB × 2-segment, 24h Partition): `{ ts, host, consumer, offsetlog_dir, source, cursor_seg, cursor_off, bytes_read, bytes_behind, msg_sent, worker_type, target, targets }`. `bytes_read` (monotonic) drives byte-rate; `bytes_behind` (summed from real on-disk segment sizes — exact, no `segment_size` guess) is the backlog. One record per consumer keeps each append under PIPE_BUF, so the shared log stays multi-writer atomic (the firehose pattern — no lock, no oversize drop); a bad/uninitialized consumer is skipped, never failing the whole snapshot. `Consumer_Node::probe_stats()` is the read seam. `Log_Cleaner` whitelists `topicprobe.p0` (it's declared by no `.tsl`) so the orphan sweep spares it — but only once a real declared set already exists, preserving the fail-closed "empty set → skip sweep" guard. This is the durable, 24h-retained source for the dashboards' byte-rate + backlog graphs; the position-readers and the dashboards move onto it (and the per-consumer memcache publish retires) in follow-ups.
- **DevTools hub: a new Overview landing tab — a live fleet-health board, the default first paint.** Modeled on Tachikoma's topics dashboard (rate + backlog, ranked) and the Workers-&-Lag pattern: a fleet strip (topology/active counts, partitions-up, a worst-health pill), the supervisor card, then one row per ACTIVE topology — per-partition worker pills (running/dead + heartbeat freshness), a live consumer-**lag sparkline** + current lag, and fleet uptime — sorted worst-health first so trouble surfaces. Stopped topologies sink into a de-emphasized chip group (Edit only; nothing's live to open). The lag sparkline is a client-side rolling ring buffer of the recent poll samples (the hub has no time-series store — long history stays in Grafana) so you can see whether a backlog is draining or climbing, not just its current size. It's order 0 so the hub opens on it instead of building the Console's heavy graph; the Console moves to order 5 (second). Reuses the shared `consoleHref` deep-links.
- **Topology Console + debug overlay: a show/hide toggle for the inspector** — mirrors the palette. The inspector is always present as a slim 28px **rail** (the show/hide chevron is reachable even with nothing selected) that expands to the full panel. Selecting a node **auto-expands** it (and shows the node); the chevron collapses/expands it anytime; a canvas-background click deselects the node (the panel stays, showing "select a node"). Defaults collapsed; the manual choice persists (`newspack-nodes:inspector-collapsed`, shared by the console and overlay). The inspector grid column is the rail (28px) or panel (380px) via a new `--inspector-col` custom property. The debug overlay shares the same dock + toggle.
- **Topology Console: instant structural paint on open.** On entering live mode the console now fires a direct `topologies get` (the same REST command edit mode uses, independent of the SSE stream), parses the TSL, and publishes it as the metadata graph — so the schematic renders immediately instead of waiting for the SSE connect + first Router TIMER tick (up to ~1s) + the `dump_metadata` round-trip. The first real `dump_metadata` reply then overwrites it with the live-enriched graph (worker status, rates). The seed resolves the live `_metadata` node by name (rebuild-safe) and skips if a `dump_metadata` reply already populated the graph. The seed is gated to **worker scopes** (`scopeFromCwd( _cwd.target ).isWorker`): at the local root (`/`) or any non-worker cwd (`_http`, …) the canvas renders the in-browser graph, so seeding the selected topology there would paint the wrong graph (and stomp the local layout into a vertical stack, since the foreign node ids miss the scope's saved position map). `scopeFromCwd` moved to `topology-console/utils/scope` so the hook can share it without a hook→component import cycle.

- **Topologies tab: a "+ New Topology" button and a per-topology "Edit" button.** Both deep-link into the Console editor — Edit opens that topology (`?tab=console&topology=<name>&edit=1`), New opens a blank draft (`?tab=console&new=1`). The console honors `?edit=1` (edit the `?topology`) and `?new=1` (blank `handleNew` draft) on mount and consumes the param so a later LIVE toggle/refresh doesn't snap back. New uses a distinct `?new=1` rather than `?edit=1`-without-topology because the console's topology→URL sync writes the default `?topology` on mount, which would otherwise make a topology-less edit link open the default topology. (The live-mode NEW button is suppressed in the debug overlay, which has no editor to land in.)
- **Topology Console: a NEW button in live mode.** Sits next to EDIT and starts a fresh (blank) topology in the editor — `handleNew` now switches into edit mode (no-op when already editing) instead of only blanking the draft, so New works from live view as well as the edit toolbar.
- **Aggregator Status dashboard relocated into the substrate as a DevTools hub tab.** The Aggregator Status React tree (`AggregatorStatus`, `useAggregatorStatusGraph`, `AggregatorViewNode`/`register`, `aggregator-status.scss` + `base.scss`) moved here from `newspack-event-logger-nodes` and now ships as a `host:'hub'` DevTools tab (`id:'aggregator'`, order 40), mirroring the Vault tab: a `src/event-aggregator/index.js` bundle entry imports `./tabs` (side effect) which `registerDevtoolsTab`s the inner `AggregatorStatus` component (the hub supplies the page chrome + DebugOverlay, so the old standalone `AggregatorStatusPage` wrapper is dropped). New `build/event-aggregator` build entry; `Admin::register_aggregator_tab_bundle()` advertises it via the `newspack_nodes/devtools_tab_bundles` filter. Behavior-preserving: field reads (`last_connection_status`, …), poll logic, and component rendering are unchanged; only the i18n text domain became `newspack-nodes`.
- **`Settings_CI` + `Status_CI` service CIs moved into the substrate.** Both read only substrate concerns (`Settings_CI`: the four `newspack_nodes_*` integer settings via `Config`; `Status_CI`: `Config`, `Bootstrap::get_topologies()`, `Core::$memd`), so they now ship here as `Newspack_Nodes\Rest\Settings_CI_Node` / `Newspack_Nodes\Rest\Status_CI_Node` and mount in `newspack_nodes_mount_substrate_cis()` as `settings` / `status` (after `Aggregator_CI`). Verb logic is unchanged from their former `newspack-event-logger-nodes` `App\*` versions, except `Status_CI`'s `get` dropped the ELN-only `version` field (no consumer) and now reports `runtime_version` only; ELN no longer mounts either.
- **`Settings_Event_Writer` moved into the substrate.** The settings-sync producer (option-name-only atomic append to `settings.p0` on watched `newspack_*` option changes) references only substrate types (`Message`, `Partition_Node`, `Config::get_logs_directory()`), so it now ships here as `Newspack_Nodes\Settings_Event_Writer`. Its `init()` is called once in the substrate wiring block; ELN no longer initializes it.
- **`Aggregator_CI` service CI moved into the substrate.** The hub-side aggregator dashboard CI (`status`/`health`/`servers`) had zero application coupling — it reads only `Vault`, `Topology_Registry::graph_for('aggregator')`, the `Remote_Source` `np:remote:*` memcache snapshots, and `Core::$memd`, all substrate concerns — so it now ships here as `Newspack_Nodes\Rest\Aggregator_CI_Node` and mounts in `newspack_nodes_mount_substrate_cis()` as `aggregator` (after `Vault_CI`). Verb logic is unchanged from its former `newspack-event-logger-nodes` `App\Aggregator_CI_Node`; ELN no longer mounts it.
- **`RemoteLinkNode` + `RemoteIpcNode` (browser runtime foundation)** — the JS base for the per-worker interactive command channel and the dashboards' shared SSE+HTTP plumbing. `RemoteLinkNode` is the full-duplex "be the browser" channel as ONE node: it composes a `SseIn` (`{name}:sse-in`, inbound stream), an `HttpOut` (`{name}:http`, outbound `/command` POST), and a `Heartbeat` (`{name}:heartbeat`, slot keepalive routed through its own HttpOut), plus the `connected→slot` bridge — so a dashboard makes one node instead of hand-wiring three + a bridge. `RemoteIpcNode` is the per-worker command channel: it composes a `SseIn`, holds the single live connection via a static `active` (a send steals it from the previous holder — the same swap the console does on cwd change), wraps a reply-node `FROM` into the `_sse:{pid}/{node}` session pivot, and bundles `connect_worker_input {reader}` ahead of each command so `cd /{worker}` can route straight to the worker (the name IS the address — no `_sse/` prefix). Both mirror the PHP `Remote_Source_Node` patron (which owns its `SSE_In`+`HTTP_Out`); the durable offsetlog stays a PHP-only `Remote_Source extends Remote_Link` concern, so JS ships only `RemoteLink` + `RemoteIpc`. Registered via `make_node` (`RemoteLink` at the runtime level so dashboards across plugins resolve it; `RemoteIpc` console-only). The reply-pivot membership set is now a single `reply-nodes.js` module consumed by both `SseIn` and `RemoteIpc` (no lockstep-drift). Foundation only — not yet wired into any dashboard/console graph; the consumer migration + worker-pivot cleanup follow.

- **Remote Servers settings** — the three remote-spoke storage-geometry settings (`remote_num_segments`, `remote_segment_size`, `remote_max_lifespan`) moved here from event-logger-nodes onto the Nodes Runtime settings page (new "Remote Servers" section). They configure substrate storage geometry pushed to spokes, so they belong in the substrate. Each is a `Config_System\Field` (`overlay: false` — registered + resettable, read directly via `get_option` by the settings-sync node graph, never overlaid into `load_config()`), with substrate `Admin` sanitizers (clamp + blank-passthrough: `remote_num_segments` 2–16, `remote_segment_size` 1MB–256MB, `remote_max_lifespan` 60–604800s) and number-field renderers. A one-time `admin_init` migration (`Remote_Settings_Migration`, guarded by `newspack_nodes_remote_settings_migrated`) renames any set `newspack_event_logger_nodes_remote_*` option to its `newspack_nodes_remote_*` name and deletes the old row.
- **`SSE_In_Node`** — a generic, passive, hidden inbound-SSE-pull node (the pull-side counterpart of `HTTP_Out`), ported from the event-logger-nodes `Remote_Source_Node` SSE internals. Owns one cURL-multi handle (registered with the Event_Framework), one easy handle (the SSE GET to `/wp-json/newspack-nodes/v1/messages/stream`), one in-memory `{segment_id, offset}` cursor, and one connection's worth of SSE parser state.
