Configured programmatically by a patron via `configure( url, auth_username, auth_password, auth_token, subscribe, positions, source, verify_ssl, require_https )`; the patron owns durable persistence + status memcache and drives `maybe_connect()` / `check_stale()` (the node owns no timer). Parses `msg` frames into 7-field Messages, stamps `_source` into the array `VALUE`, guards against the `Partition_Node` PIPE_BUF cap via `Message::packed_size()`, and forwards to its sink with `TO=target`; consumes the `connected` handshake to capture `slot()` and `connection()` state; records `heartbeat` frames. Exposes `position()` / `restore_position()` / `slot()` / `connection()`. libcurl init/setopt/add is behind a static `$curl_dispatch` `\Closure` seam (mirrors `HTTP_Out`). `node_schema` is `I/O` + `hidden` (kept out of the topology palette). Foundational to the pull-side aggregator slice (issue #41).
- **`HTTP_Out_Node`** — a generic non-blocking outbound command-egress node (the push-side counterpart of `HTTP_In`), a `Timer_Node`. `fill()` buffers each incoming message as a packed `TM_COMMAND` and arms an immediate one-shot timer; `fire()` POSTs the **whole batch as one JSONL request** per spoke on the Event_Framework's cURL-multi (never blocks the drain), resolving url + credentials from the `Vault` by server id (Basic Auth, legacy Bearer fallback). Spoke replies in the response body forward to the node's sink and self-route by TO=FROM (modeled on the JS `HttpOut` lock/flush). Foundational infrastructure for topology-driven settings-sync and aggregation (issue #41).
- **`Remote_Source_Node`** — a self-sufficient, topology-visible (`I/O`, palette-visible; args `vault_id remote_topic partition`) SSE-pull aggregation node, a `Timer_Node` ported from the event-logger-nodes `Remote_Source_Node` + `Stream_Merger_Node` offsetlog code. It patrons two hidden siblings created/registered on first tick once the Vault entry resolves: a `SSE_In_Node` (`<name>:sse-in`, pulls one spoke `<topic>.p<partition>`, configured from the Vault entry with `vault_verify_ssl`/`vault_require_https` from `Config`, wired to forward to this node's OWN downstream sink/target) and an `HTTP_Out_Node` (`<name>:remote`, carries the heartbeat). A missing Vault entry leaves the node disconnected (rate-limited log, no mis-configured patrons). Owns a durable per-node offsetlog `Partition` at `<offsets_dir>/<name>.p<partition>` (keyed by NODE NAME): restores the latest committed `{seg,off}` into `SSE_In::restore_position()` before connect, and commits the live `SSE_In::position()` every ~5s. `fire()` (the recurring 1s tick) drives the passive SSE_In (`check_stale()` + `maybe_connect()`), maybe-commits the offsetlog, maybe-heartbeats, and publishes a generic per-node status snapshot to memcache under `np:remote:<name>:p<partition>` (connection fields from `SSE_In::connection()` + heartbeat fields). Every ~15s, once a slot is known, it mints a `workers.heartbeat` `TM_COMMAND` (`FROM=<this node>`, `TO=workers`, args `<slot> <ttl> <partition>`) and `fill()`s it into the patron HTTP_Out (no direct `wp_remote_post`); the reply self-routes back into this node's `fill()`, recording `last_heartbeat_response` + RTT. `remove_node()` tears down both patrons + the offsetlog. Foundational to the pull-side aggregator slice (issue #41).
- **`Settings_Sync_Node`** — a substrate `Timer_Node` that pushes registered WP-option changes to connected spokes. An `add_setting <local_option> <TO> <remote_option>` registry (configured from a topology) maps each watched option to a spoke target + remote name; `fill()` (fed option-name-only events by a Consumer) and the periodic `fire()` resolve the option's current value (via the app-overridable `newspack_nodes/settings_sync/value` filter) and emit one normalized `{name:'set', arguments:'<remote> <value>'}` `TM_COMMAND` per setting, addressed through a Tee to the per-spoke `HTTP_Out`. Foundational to topology-driven settings-sync (issue #41).
- Topology Console: **Newspack** and **Newspack Brand** skins; Newspack is now the default skin. The Newspack skins restyle the node chrome to the product system — clean white rounded node cards with a shaded title band and Cobalt-led metrics (sparkline + counter), so red is reserved for genuine errors instead of every node's throughput readout. **Newspack Brand** leads with the expressive palette to stand clearly apart from Product: Emerald metrics/edges/selection and a Morganite-tinted title band. Node cards gain a `topology-node__header` title-band element (transparent by default; other skins unaffected). The Newspack skins also add a Newspack "N" logo mark, rounded header/inspector controls, and on-brand REPL prompt/caret/transcript colors (Cobalt for Newspack, Emerald for Brand).
- DevTools Hub: the tab-bar chrome now follows the active WordPress admin color scheme — it reads the live admin bar's color so the hub blends with Fresh / Coffee / Ectoplasm / a custom scheme instead of a fixed dark strip.
- **Vault** — an encrypted credential store for remote-server connections (`\Newspack_Nodes\Vault`, option `newspack_nodes_vault`; libsodium secretbox keyed off `wp_salt('auth')`), moved here from event-logger-nodes' `Server_Registry`. Exposed via a `vault` service CI (list/get/add/update/delete/test) and a new **Vault** hub tab (credential CRUD + connection test) on the default WordPress admin background. Mutations fire the `newspack_nodes/vault/changed` action so applications can react (settings-sync, worker restart) without the substrate owning those concerns. A one-time `admin_init` migration copies the legacy `newspack_event_logger_nodes_aggregator_servers` option into the Vault (value copy; source left intact).

### Changed

- **The worker-status dashboard hides ghost readers of partitions that no longer exist.** After `num_partitions` shrinks (e.g. 2→1), the TopicProbe still carries the dead partition's last reader snapshot for up to the probe log's retention window (~24h), so the tree kept showing stalled `…p1` rows. `reconstructWorkers` now drops a consumer row whose `(source, partition)` isn't in the declared `logs` set **and** has no live worker backing it — cross-checking the probe row against the declared logs already in the payload, not a bare stall timer. A still-declared partition between worker respawns stays visible.
- **Stock consumers now write role-suffixed offsetlogs** (`hub-control`'s settings consumer → `settings.settings-sync.p0`, `job-worker`'s jobs consumer → `jobs.job-worker.p0`) instead of bare `settings.p0`/`jobs.p0`. The bare offsetlog name was indistinguishable from the source log it tails, so `wp nodes status` and the dashboard rendered a consumer as "a log reading itself." Suffixing by the owning role disambiguates reader from source; the old bare offsetlog dirs orphan and are reclaimed by the GC.
- **Log_Cleaner retention now follows the operator's ACTIVE topology set, uniformly for logs AND offsetlogs.** `declared_dirs()` built its keep-set from `Topology_Registry::list()` (every `.tsl` on disk), so a shipped-but-deactivated topology (e.g. the standalone `request-builder`/`job-router`/`flame-builder` superseded by `combined`) kept its logs and offsetlogs forever. It now iterates `Bootstrap::get_topologies()` — the same active set the supervisor spawns from — so a topology that isn't activated has both its log dirs and its offsetlog dirs reclaimed on the next sweep. No live worker's data is ever at risk (anything spawning is, by definition, in the active set), and the empty→skip fail-closed gate is unchanged. The PHP-producer logs (firehose/jobintake) and the topicprobe/settings whitelist stay log-only — substrate logs with no consumer-offset of their own.
- **The shared topicprobe log now uses 1 MiB segments (was 5 MiB).** The probe emits only small per-tick consumer-stats snapshots, so 1 MiB × 2 segments is ample for the dashboards' rolling window while lowering the on-disk footprint and re-segmentation cost. Existing 5 MiB segments can be re-segmented in place via `wp nodes ingest '<config:logs_dir>/topicprobe.p<partition>' <old-segments> --num_partitions=1 --segment_size=1048576 --num_segments=2`.
- **Dashboard page titles match the standard WP admin heading ("Posts").** The hub-tab titles (Raw Logs, Aggregator Status) were small bold labels; they now use the WP page-heading look (23px / weight 400 / the WP top padding), defined ONCE as a shared `.newspack-dashboard-title` primitive in the `newspack-nodes-theme` token sheet and applied by class — so every dashboard header across this plugin and the consumers reads identically (no per-dashboard copy of the rule).
- **The Vault tab is themed to Newspack.** Its WP-admin-native markup was inheriting the WP admin color scheme with an unstyled table; scoped rules now theme the table as a Newspack card (subtle uppercase header band + row hover) and the modal buttons (Cobalt-primary submit, white bordered Test/Cancel, red destructive Remove). The header **Add Server** trigger reuses the shared `.nodes-cards__new` button (the same "+ New Topology" affordance used on the Overview).
- **Overview polish pass:** the chrome buttons get a slightly thicker (1.5px) border so they read more clearly; the Supervisor card drops its redundant inner grey bar (the row sits directly on the white card); and the worker-tree fold caret is enlarged (10px → 13px) to match the topology-row chevron. The debug overlay panel gains rounded corners (10px), squared off only when maximized.
- **Consistent surface + button system on the Overview, and radii normalized to 8px.** The chrome buttons (Fold all / Unfold all, New Topology, the per-row restart + Edit, the supervisor refresh) all shared the same `surface-subtle` fill as their container and the page, so none read as a button; they now use one shared `secondary-button` look — a white fill with a `border-strong` edge that reads on both the subtle page and a white card. The Supervisor bar becomes a white bordered card matching the topology rows (it was styled differently), and each topology row's heading is transparent so a collapsed row reads as one clean white card (the divider moved to the expanded body). Card/panel/button corner radii (topology rows, Supervisor, Topics charts, summary cards, Aggregator server + partition cards, the Raw Logs pane) are normalized to 8px (`--np-radius-md`).
- **Aggregator Status partition cards redesigned for at-a-glance health.** Each partition card now carries a left **health rail** — green (connected + heartbeating), amber (connected but not yet heartbeating), red (down) — so a card's state reads without parsing its rows, and the connected badge gains a small breathing live-dot. The redundant elements are gone: the HTTP code moved onto the Status row as a quiet mono caption (it was a second green pill competing with the heartbeat badge, and previously rendered a 200 as green-on-red), and a dedicated error line now appears only when a partition has a real error message. Server cards are bordered/elevated and the partition cards sit on a subtle surface with the rail; all on the Newspack tokens.
- **Lighter worker-tree segment bars and status pills.** The read/segment bars were tall (32px) and fully saturated (solid Cobalt-success green, near-black grey); they're now slimmer (22px) with softened fills (a lighter green for processed, a soft light-grey for the past-probe-end region) so the wide bars don't dominate the light dashboard. The status pills keep their solid-fill-with-white-text look but the green is softened a touch off the heavy success-600 — a new shared `--np-success-soft` token (success mixed ~15% toward white) backs the filled "running"/"connected" pills across BOTH the worker tree and the Aggregator status, so they match. (`dead`/`idle` keep their error/neutral solids.)
- **The Overview Topics charts (Message Rate / Byte Rate / Backlog) are light-themed.** They were dark data panels; they're now light Newspack cards (white panel, neutral grid/axis ink, light tooltip) so the whole Overview reads light. The shared `useTimeChart` hover-column highlight changed from a white translucent (invisible on a light panel) to a neutral grey translucent that reads on both light and dark charts; the categorical series palette is unchanged.
- **The DevTools hub is now fully light Newspack, and the Aggregator + Vault tabs are reskinned to match.** The hub frame + tab bar previously inherited the WP admin color scheme (a dark backdrop under light dashboard cards); the hub is a themed product surface, so it now renders light Newspack (`--np-surface-subtle` frame, `--np-text` tab labels, Cobalt active underline + focus ring) regardless of the admin scheme — dashboards read fully light (light backdrop + light cards + dark data charts / log panes), matching the console's Newspack skin. The Aggregator Status tab and the Vault admin (previously still dark) flip onto the `var(--np-*)` tokens; the Aggregator's `HTTP 200` chip no longer renders green-on-red (the status wrapper that boxed even successful codes in error-red is neutralized — error-red stays only on actual error text). The shared connection banner uses the Newspack error token. The now-unused `useAdminChromeColors` hook was removed.
- **The event dashboards are reskinned to the Newspack theme (light Cobalt-on-white chrome + dark data/log surfaces).** Every event-dashboards panel (Overview, Summary Cards, the worker-status tree, Topology rows, Raw Logs) now reads as Newspack: the chrome (cards, tables, controls, headers, tree) is the light product look mapped onto the `var(--np-*)` tokens, while the genuine data-viz + log surfaces (the Topics charts, the Raw Logs viewer pane) stay dark — exactly as the Topology Console's Newspack skin keeps a dark REPL. The Material-blue accent (`#64b5f6`) and the ad-hoc dark palette are gone; Cobalt leads, status is functional (success/warning/error), and the rate/health/provenance chips use the functional + chart palette. The dashboards now enqueue the shared `newspack-nodes-theme` handle as a style dependency (the new default in `Admin::enqueue_react_page`, registered early on `admin_enqueue_scripts` so sibling plugins can depend on it too) and carry `.newspack-nodes-theme` on the hub root so `var(--np-*)` resolves.


- **Overview fold state persists down to the inner nodes/partitions, and a new segment animates its fills in.** The Overview already persisted the topology-level fold; the within-tree node/partition folds (the nested chevrons) now persist to localStorage too (`overviewPrefs` gains a `collapsed` key), so an expanded subtree survives a reload like the section folds do. And a segment that slides into a partition's bar now mounts with empty fills and animates them in as part of the left→right cascade, instead of appearing pre-drawn at full width.
- **Overview rows are user-orderable, stable, and the folded summary uses the expanded heading style.** (1) Rows no longer auto-sort worst-health-first — a flapping `behind` badge reshuffled the list on every poll; they now render in the user's own **drag order** (a grip handle, native HTML5 DnD), and that order PLUS each topology's fold state **persist to localStorage** (`overviewOrder` / `overviewPrefs`), restored on reload. (2) The folded summary row and the unfolded detail row are now ONE `TopologyRow` (heading always; body only when unfolded), so the compact summary shows the SAME per-partition pills as the expanded view — each partition with its OWN uptime (the folded row previously showed a single bogus shared uptime). (3) The catch-up **ETA is back**: it shows beside the health badge when behind/stalled, and a consumer is no longer counted `behind` until its ETA reaches **1 minute** (`deriveHealth` uses `etaSeconds( behind, read_rate ) >= 60`; a sub-minute backlog drains on its own). Each worker now carries its own `read_rate` (`reconstructWorkers`) to feed the rollup, and `formatEta` is split into `etaSeconds` + `formatEtaSeconds`.
- **The Overview and Topologies tabs are merged into one foldable Overview tab.** There is no longer a separate "Topologies" hub tab; the per-topology live detail tree it carried now folds into the Overview's active rows. Each active topology renders FOLDED by default (a compact summary row); an expand chevron unfolds it in place to the full `TopologyRow` heading + live `TopologySection` detail tree (the old Topologies look), and a topology-level collapse chevron folds it back. A **Fold all** / **Unfold all** toolbar (shown only when ≥1 topology is active) drives every active row at once; the within-tree node fold is a separate, independently-threaded `collapsed` set. Inactive topologies stay in the compact "Stopped" group with no fold affordance. `consoleHref` / `sectionFor` / the detail-row component moved from the deleted `TopologyManager.js` into a new `TopologyRow.js`.
- **New "Messages/s" fleet card.** `SummaryCards` gains a global produced-message-rate card (between Write and Messages · 24h), summing each distinct per-partition source's latest `msgRate` from the topicprobe view (`globalMsgRate`, reading each consumer's view-computed `latest` sample and deduping co-readers of one source by max — the same per-partition-source convention `probe24hTotals` uses — so a topic's partitions sum while two topologies tailing one partition don't double-count).
- **Overview + Topologies tabs lead with a shared fleet-vitals card row.** Both hub tabs now render one `SummaryCards` component at the top — `Topologies` (N · M active) · `Workers` (running / configured `num_partitions` across active topologies) · `Partitions` (on-disk `.pN` log dirs) · `Health` (all systems ok / N behind / N stalled) · `Read` + `Write` (fleet-global byte rates) · `Messages · 24h` + `Bytes · 24h` (produced totals from the topicprobe history) — so the two tabs show identical numbers from one set of pure derives (`fleetSummary`, `probe24hTotals`, `globalRates`), no per-tab drift. Topologies now mounts the same 24h probe replay Overview did.
  - **Fixes the partition/worker count.** "Workers up" counts running workers against each active topology's *configured* `num_partitions` (the expected process count), not the partitions that happened to report a worker in the last snapshot — which under-counted and flickered when a worker briefly dropped out. The on-disk partition count comes from a new `dump_graph` `log_partitions` field (a fresh glob of the `logs/` dir).
  - **Activation toggle + fleet restart + Edit are now on Overview too**, via a shared `TopologyControls` component (extracted from the Topologies row) used by both tabs' active *and* stopped rows — one styled control set, no duplication. Overview gained the rejected-mutation `AlertModal` to match.
  - Overview's active-row columns get `min-width` floors (health / partition pills+tally / uptime) so a 1-partition row's columns line up with a 2-partition row's.
- **Remote Min Retention accepts 0 (= disabled), matching the hub's Minimum Retention field.** The setting (`remote_max_lifespan`) floored at 60s in both the number input's `min` attribute and `sanitize_remote_max_lifespan`, so the browser blocked `0` with "Please select a value that is no less than 60." But `Partition_Node` clamps `max_lifespan` with `\max( 0, … )` and treats `0` as "age never blocks trimming" (pure count-based retention) — the hub `max_lifespan` field already exposes this as `min=0`, "0 = disabled (pure count-based)." The remote twin now matches: `min=0`, clamp `[0, 604800]`, and the description documents the disabled mode. (Blank still means "use the config-file default.")
- **Consumer plugins reliably override the substrate's stock topologies regardless of plugin load order.** A consumer's `register_plugin()` dir (e.g. ELN's `hub-control.tsl`) now always shadows the substrate's bundled copy. Previously both nodes' own dir and consumer dirs were registered via `register_stock_dir()` (each `array_unshift`ed to the front), so resolution priority depended on plugin load timing — nodes' `rest_api_init:1` registration landing after ELN's `plugins_loaded:11` meant nodes' `hub-control` shadowed ELN's. The substrate's own dir is now registered via a new `Topology_Registry::register_builtin_dir()` that appends it as the lowest-priority fallback (pushed to the END, once); consumer `register_stock_dir()` dirs still unshift to the front, so any consumer topology resolves before the builtin while nodes-only deployments still resolve their own via the fallback.
- **Topologies tab: a log row always shows its WRITE rate (`W`), even when the log is being consumed.** Previously a consumed log (`hasCursor`) labeled its rate `R` while still showing the write-rate value — so a consumed log never plainly surfaced how fast it's written, and the label was misleading. `TreeEntity`'s log row now always renders `W <write-rate>`; the consumer's read rate (`R`) already shows on its own node row.
- **SSE+HTTP "be the browser" channel refactored into a `Remote_Link` base with two subclasses, mirroring the JS hierarchy.** Everything in `Remote_Source_Node` except the durable offsetlog moved up into a new `Remote_Link_Node` base (the SSE_In + HTTP_Out patrons, the recurring tick driving `check_stale`/`maybe_connect`, the `workers.heartbeat` mint + RTT bookkeeping, the per-node memcache status snapshot, the `fill()` reply-guard + `send()` relay). `Remote_Source_Node extends Remote_Link_Node` now carries ONLY the aggregation offsetlog via four protected seams (`restore_position` reads the committed cursor, `persist_cursor` commits it every ~5s, `should_connect` keeps the base defaults) — its public API, args, schema, and behavior are unchanged. New `Remote_IPC_Node extends Remote_Link_Node` mirrors the browser console's worker-pivot (JS `RemoteIpcNode`): `send()` rewrites a reply node's FROM to the `_sse:{pid}/{node}` demux pivot and bundles a leading `connect_worker_input {reader}` to `topologies` so both ride one HTTP_Out POST; `connect()` steals the single live stream (static `$active`); `should_connect()` keeps only the active link's tick alive; `SSE_In_Node` gained a session-pid capture from the `connected` handshake (`pid()`). `Remote_Link`/`Remote_IPC` are `Hidden`-category (not palette nodes).
- **Overview tab: three Tachikoma-style Topics panels — Message Rate, Byte Rate, Backlog — over the real 24h `topicprobe.p0` history**, below the supervisor card. The Overview mounts a second `RemoteLink` (`useTopicProbeStream` in `history` mode → `positions=start`) that replays the durable probe log into `topicprobe:view`; `topicChartSeries` rolls the per-reader samples up per topic, and `TopicsChart` draws each panel on the SHARED d3 charting infra the event-logger dashboards use (`@newspack-nodes/shared/hooks/useTimeChart` — grid, color legend, hover tooltip): a multi-series overlaid-area time chart, one series per topic, ranked busiest-first. The in-product mirror of Grafana's Topics dashboard, replacing the per-row in-memory lag sparkline. (d3 is now a substrate dependency — the first nodes-own dashboard to use the shared chart hook; the jest config transforms the d3 ESM packages.) To feed the byte-rate panel the probe record gained one raw field, `Probe_Record::END_BYTES` (the partition's absolute byte position = Σ live segment sizes, from `compute_lag`); message rate derives from `MSGS`, byte rate from `END_BYTES`, backlog from `DISTANCE` — all as deltas across consecutive records, in `TopicProbeView`. The active-topology rows keep the current-lag number from the live `dump_graph` snapshot.
- **TopicProbe record is now LEAN + POSITIONAL — rates and totals are derived by readers, never logged.** This supersedes the earlier "probe computes the rates and writes them into each record" / fat-record design described in the entries below (none of it shipped). The record is a positional array under shared index constants (`Probe_Record`, mirrored PHP `includes/class-probe-record.php` ↔ JS `src/runtime/probe-record.js`, parity-tested) carrying ONLY `[SOURCE, READER, CURSOR_SEG, CURSOR_OFF, END_SEG, END_SIZE, DISTANCE, MSGS]` — small enough that the browser sucks down 24h cheaply; the snapshot instant is the Message TIMESTAMP, not a logged `ts`. `Consumer_Node::probe_stats()` emits it; `compute_lag()` returns `end_seg`/`end_size` (the partition's last segment + size at the snapshot) + `bytes_behind` from ONE `get_segments()` (cursor vs end captured together — never a stale cursor vs a fresh stat). The Overview's rate is Δ`MSGS`/Δts and its backlog is `DISTANCE`, both derived from consecutive records; the Topologies tab's read/write byte rates are derived in `reconstructWorkers` (next entry). `wp nodes status` is now a per-consumer lag table (Reader/Source/Partition/Behind/Msgs); worker liveness is `wp nodes ls`. (Removed the dead `CLI::live_position`/`normalize_probe_position`/`scalar_float`/`format_duration` and `Workers_CI_Node::to_string`.)
- **Worker Status dashboard: the browser rebuilds the rich `workers[]` from the lean `dump_graph` payload.** PHP no longer pre-joins worker attribution — `dump_graph` now ships `graph` (the `.tsl` structure), `workers` (liveness only), `consumers` (per-reader probe STATE), and `logs` (live segment lists) separately. `workerStatusTransform` (new helper `reconstructWorkers`) joins them client-side back into the old rich `workers[]` shape (`handler`/`source`/`cursor`/`behind`/`inputs_status`/…), so `topologyGraph.buildTopologySections`, `TreeEntity`, `SegmentBar`, and `useTopologyManager` are unchanged. Each consumer's `inputs_status` is the live partition TRIMMED back to the probe's `(end_seg, end_size)` snapshot (so the segment bar reflects the probe instant, not the now-grown live partition); disambiguated readers of one source each get their own row. Read rate = Δ(absolute cursor byte position)/Δts and write rate = Δ(partition total live bytes)/Δts are computed across consecutive polls (first sample / cursor-went-backwards / zero Δt → 0, never negative).
- **Byte rates (read + write) are computed by the probe and displayed as-is — no client-side delta of live values.** The Topologies tab derived `byteRates` (read) by deltaing the 15s-stale probe cursor and `writeRates` (write) by deltaing the live 4s `total_size` scandir, divided by the poll interval — so a caught-up consumer's read rate flickered 0 between probe ticks while the write rate kept moving. Now `TopicProbe` computes `read_rate` (Δ`bytes_read`) + `write_rate` (Δ`bytes_total`) from its OWN consecutive 15s samples and writes them into each record; they ride `consumer_rows()` → the worker descriptor, and `workerStatusTransform` reads them straight off (no delta, no live `total_size`/cursor math, no `Date.now()` time base). Read and write now share one cadence and source. (The Topology Console's live node-counter rates — `useGraphRates` over SSE `dump_metadata`, where both come from the worker's ~1s counters — are already consistent and unchanged.)
- **Lag (`behind`) is read from the probe snapshot, not recomputed against a fresh `get_segments()`.** The dashboard + `wp nodes status` were comparing a ~15s-old cursor (from the topicprobe record) against a freshly-statted partition END — so a caught-up consumer looked behind, because the partition grew in those 15s. `Consumer_Node::compute_lag()` already reads cursor + end together; it now also emits `bytes_total` (the end at that instant), and `probe_stats()` carries `bytes_behind` + `bytes_total`. `CLI::consumer_rows()` rows carry `behind`/`total`; `Workers_CI::build_worker_status()` and `Worker_CLI_Command::consumer_behind()` use them and no longer stat the live partition for lag (dropped the per-worker `get_segments()` loop, the dead `CLI::calculate_behind()`, and the unused worker-level `segments` field). The per-segment SegmentBar visual (`inputs_status[]`) still scandirs — that's a position bar, not the lag number.
- **Position readers now read the `topicprobe` log, not memcache + the offsetlog.** The cursor readers move onto the durable `topicprobe.p0` index, completing the TopicProbe migration: `CLI::consumer_rows()` enumerates Consumers from the probe (new `CLI::read_probe_index()` → `Partition_Node::read_tail_index_by()`, a bounded newest-segment-tail read keyed by `offsetlog_dir`, latest-record-wins); `Workers_CI` threads each row's cursor straight into `build_worker_status()` (the dashboard's `behind` is still computed from the live on-disk segments + that cursor); `CLI::live_position()` now takes the pre-read index (the `list` verb reads it once and threads it) and resolves a worker's representative cursor by `worker_type` + the shortest matching `offsetlog_dir`; and `wp nodes status` reads the cursor off its `consumer_rows()` row directly. **Removed** the now-dead memcache path: `Consumer_Node::publish_position()` + `position_key()`/`position_cache_key()`/`POSITION_KEY_PREFIX`/`PUBLISH_INTERVAL_S` (the per-tick memcache publish in `fire()` is gone — the probe is the sole position source), `Workers_CI::get_live_position()`/`read_offsetlog_position()` + its `$cache` property and the `newspack_nodes/workers_cache` filter, `CLI::live_position_for()`/`input_basename()`/`read_offsetlog_entry()`, and the `newspack_nodes/worker_cli_cache` filter. (The offsetlog stays — crash-resume only, checkpointed every 30s.)

- **`Consumer_Node` checkpoints its offsetlog every 30s, not every second.** Now that TopicProbe (not the offsetlog) is the position source, the offsetlog is crash-resume only — `CHECKPOINT_INTERVAL_S` 1 → 30 cuts offsetlog write volume ~30×; a crash re-delivers under 30s of cursor on respawn (at-least-once, unchanged).
- **Topologies tab + Overview tab: a stopped topology's name no longer links to live mode.** Live mode is meaningless for an inactive topology (nothing is running to inspect), so its name now renders as plain text — no link, and no hover underline (the Overview hover rule is scoped to the `[href]` anchor). Only an active topology's name deep-links into the live console. The per-topology **Edit** deep-link is unchanged for both active and stopped topologies.
- **Vault admin: the add-server form label + validation read just "ID"** (was "Server ID"), matching the list column header.
- **Topology Console + debug overlay: a canvas-background click no longer dismisses the transcript.** It still deselects the current node/edge when there is one (the always-present inspector panel stays, showing "select a node"), and autofits when there isn't — but it never collapses the REPL transcript (that stays an Esc / footer-toggle action). The repl-dismiss-on-canvas-click was removed from both the console and the overlay.
- **Topology Console + debug overlay: autofit fills the canvas instead of sitting at native zoom.** `tightViewBoxFor` no longer floors the viewBox to the canvas size (which left small/short graphs marooned in a sea of margin). It now does a fit-all `meet` scale that fills ~90% of the binding dimension (capped at 2× so a lone node doesn't balloon), and treats the unobstructed canvas as the viewport: the expanded REPL transcript's overlay height (`bottomObstructionPx`, reported by `ReplFooter`) is reserved and the graph is fit + centered into the band *above* it, so nodes never hide behind the transcript. Both the console and the debug overlay share this (the overlay now wires the transcript-overlay → autofit plumbing it previously lacked).
- **Refactor: the console and the debug overlay now share one `useGraphSurface` hook** for the ConsoleShell chrome (inspector collapse + auto-expand-on-select, the transcript-overlay → autofit obstruction, palette, REPL-expand state), returning `canvasChromeProps` / `replChromeProps` fragments both spread. Previously each surface assembled its own copy, so canvas/inspector behaviors had to be wired twice and the overlay kept drifting; this single-sources that wiring. No behavior change.


- **Dropped the stock `aggregator.tsl` — the aggregator topology is ELN-only.** Its only node beyond the firehose `Topic` is the ELN `Remote_Job_Rewrite_Node`, so after removing the ELN-specific parts there's nothing substrate-worthy left (unlike `hub-control.tsl`, which keeps a substrate base + ELN override). `newspack-event-logger-nodes` now ships the full `aggregator.tsl`; `make_node Remote_Source` / `Aggregator_CI`'s `graph_for('aggregator')` resolve it via the registered ELN stock dir. `StatusCITest` now exercises the actual substrate stock topologies (`hub-control` / `job-worker`).
- **`register_stock_dir` now gives the LAST-registered stock dir precedence.** `Topology_Registry::register_stock_dir()` prepends (`array_unshift`) rather than appending, so when two stock dirs declare the same topology name the most-recently-registered one wins on `resolve()` (test renamed `test_multiple_stock_dirs_first_wins` → `…_last_wins`).
- **`Topic_Node` no longer JSON-encodes every message just to size it (hot-path perf).** `Topic_Node::fill()` called `Message::packed_size()` on every routed message to self-track `bytes_written`/`largest_msg_sent` — and `packed_size()` is `strlen( wp_json_encode( … ) )`, so it encoded the entire message (a 1MB+ JSONL line for large payloads) only to take its length and throw the encoding away, on the routing hot path. A Topic only routes; its partitions already encode-and-size when they actually write. So `Topic_Node::bytes_written()` / `largest_msg_sent()` now aggregate (sum / max) over the owned `Partition_Node`s, and the per-`fill()` encode is gone — no redundant encode of every message at the routing hop. (`stats`/`dump_metadata` figures are unchanged; the consumer already calls these as methods.)
- **Topology console worker-pivot now routes through one `RemoteIpc` per active worker** instead of a single shared `_sse` (`SseIn`) + `_http` (`HttpOut`) + `_heartbeat`. `useConsoleGraph` makes one `make_node RemoteIpc` per active worker (named `{topology}.p{N}`); the worker's name IS the address, so the cwd is the bare `{topology}.p{N}` (no `_sse/` prefix) and `cd /{worker}` routes straight to its `RemoteIpc`. Each `RemoteIpc` (`extends RemoteLinkNode`) owns an UNNAMED per-link `SseIn` (an internal stream — not registered in Core, so the N per-worker streams don't churn the canvas layout) and SHARES the reserved-name `_http` (HttpOut) + `_heartbeat` (Heartbeat) singletons + the `connected→slot` bridge; only one stream is live at a time (the active worker's `RemoteIpc` steals it on a send/connect, and the visibility/stream gate closes `RemoteIpcNode.active`). The reply-FROM `_sse:{pid}/{node}` wrap + the `connect_worker_input` bundling moved up into `RemoteIpc`, so `HttpOut` is now a dumb POST boundary (POST verbatim; route synchronous replies to its sink by TO — no `_sse` convergence node) and `SseIn` is receive-only (its `fill()` override removed — inbound frames route via the connector's `super.fill`). The reply-pivot wrap is unconditional on any non-empty FROM (the `REPLY_NODES`/`reply-nodes.js` membership list is gone). The `remote-link.js`/`remote-ipc.js` modules are renamed to the `*-node.js` convention. Console-graph node count drops from three shared boundary nodes to one channel per worker; behavior preserved (canvas polls, slot keepalive, visibility gating, status/pid display, Reset Graph). The bare `_sse` request-scope cwd is gone (it had no node behind it); `_http` remains the broadcast-boundary cwd. The seven `_sse/`-prefixed console path helpers (`buildPathOptions`, `parseWorker`, `scopeFromCwd`, `workerPollPath`, `toNeedsSseSession`, `longestWorkerPrefix`, `canEdit`) now use the bare worker form.
- **Raw Logs dashboard now wires through one `RemoteLink` node** instead of hand-wiring `SseIn` (`_sse`) + `HttpOut` (`_http`) + `Heartbeat` (`_heartbeat`) + a `connected→slot` bridge. The hook makes a single `make_node RemoteLink` and drives `connect`/`close`/`setSubscribe`/`send`; the composed children (`rawlogs:link:sse-in`/`:http`/`:heartbeat`) and the slot bridge live inside the node. Behavior preserved (the `list_logs` dropdown, log selection, visibility gating, slot keepalive, Reset Graph). Also hardens `RemoteLinkNode.removeNode()` to close its `SseIn`'s live `EventSource` (base `Node`/`TimerNode` `removeNode` only unregister, so teardown previously leaked the open stream).
- **`HTTP_Out_Node`: preserve caller `FROM`, strip a forwarded reply's leading `_output/`, and honor a new `vault_require_https` config key.** `fill()` now stamps the envelope `FROM` with the incoming message's `FROM` when non-empty (falling back to `_http` only when the caller left it empty), so a heartbeat minted with `FROM=<remote-source>` keeps it and the spoke's reply can route back. `on_curl_message()` strips a leading `_output/` from each forwarded reply's `TO` (the spoke's `HTTP_In` echoes `_output/<from>`, but a worker graph has no `_output`) so `TO=<remote-source>` routes correctly. `fire()` reads `vault_require_https` (default `false`, added to `newspack-nodes-config.php`); when true and the resolved spoke url is not `https://…`, it refuses — rate-limited log, no POST, batch dropped — mirroring the legacy heartbeat HTTPS guard. Foundational to the pull-side heartbeat-status round-trip (issue #41).
- **Vault: dropped the stored `enabled` flag — presence now means enabled.** A spoke is "enabled" by being wired into the graph, so the boolean is gone from `ALLOWED_KEYS`, the add/update/normalize defaults, and `validate_config`. `Vault::get_enabled()` is now an alias for `get_all()`. Config-file servers are now FULLY immutable — `update()` is a no-op (returns false) for them rather than allowing an `enabled` toggle. The `vault` CI's public list/get shape no longer carries `enabled`, the `add`/`update` verbs no longer accept an `enabled` arg, and the `newspack_nodes/vault/changed` action is now 2-arg `( string $id, string $action )` (the `$was_enabled`/`$now_enabled` params are dropped). Existing entries carrying a stale `enabled` key are harmless — reads ignore it and the next `update()` drops it. The hub **Vault** tab drops the per-server enabled toggle/column accordingly (rows show url / has_credentials / is_config + a Test button).
- **Vault tab: the add-server form moved into a modal, and the server table columns are re-proportioned.** An **Add Server** button above the table opens the id / url / username / password form in a modal (the existing `ConfirmRemoveModal` backdrop/ESC/dialog shell is now a shared `Modal` both dialogs use); the modal focuses the first field on open and closes on a successful add (the fresh table row is the confirmation) or on Cancel / ESC / backdrop. The **Add Server** (primary) and **Cancel** actions sit together in the modal footer (status text left-aligned, buttons right) rather than the submit floating in the form body. The `.nodes-vault__modal` shell gained real overlay CSS (dimmed full-screen backdrop + centered WP-admin card) — previously the class was unstyled, so the remove-confirm dialog rendered inline; both dialogs now render as proper modals. The server table (`table-layout: fixed`) now sets explicit column widths — URL 53%, ID 12%, Status 15%, Actions 20% — so a long `https://…` URL no longer wraps while Status/Actions waste width.
- Topology Console: polished the existing skins for legibility — AA-fixed muted text, evened the paper/ink ramps, and split the four status LEDs into distinct hues where they previously collapsed (Blueprint, Nord, Aurora, Neo-Tokyo, SCADA, …). Swiss Brutalist's monochrome and CRT Phosphor Terminal are intentionally unchanged.
- Topology Console (all skins): the canvas grid now stays visible at every zoom — the line is a constant 1px (`non-scaling-stroke`) instead of vanishing sub-pixel when zoomed out — and the decorative title block + corner reticles sit behind nodes instead of painting over them.


- **`kill_readers()` moved from `Config` (static) to `Supervisor` (instance)** — the worker-restart-flag drop now lives with the rest of the supervisor lifecycle control; call it via `Bootstrap::supervisor()->kill_readers( $groups )` instead of `Config::kill_readers( $groups )`.

### Removed

- **Unused members** flagged by a dead-code audit and verified to have no caller anywhere (production, tests, sibling plugins, dynamic dispatch): `Shell_Node::set_show_parse()`, `Config_System\Field::is_option()`, and the `Job_Worker_Node::MAX_JSON_DEPTH` constant.
- **`Bootstrap::register_worker_partitions()`** — the batch "mount every live worker's input Partition" wrapper, superseded by the per-worker `connect_worker_input` pivot (`Bootstrap::register_worker_partition()`, singular). It had no production caller; the cli and HTTP/SSE paths mount one worker at a time.
- **`CLI::base_dir()`** — unused getter; `CLI` builds every path from its private `$base_dir` directly, and the only caller was a getter self-test.
- **`Job_Worker_Node` test-only handler accessors + dead between-jobs callback** — `set_local_handler()`, `set_remote_handler()`, `register_handler()`, `has_local_handler()`, `has_remote_handler()`, `has_handler()`, `jobs_executed()`, `get_stale_timeout()`, `get_max_runtime()`, `memory_pressure()`, and the `set_between_jobs_callback()` machinery (`$between_jobs_cb` + its `fill()` block, unreachable once the setter was gone). Production registers handlers exclusively via the `newspack_nodes/{job,remote_job}_handlers` filters (eager-loaded in the constructor); the JobWorker tests now drive that path via a `register_job_handler` helper and observe internal state via reflection.
- **`Core` closing-queue** — `Core::run_closing()` and `Core::push_closing()`. Node teardown is now synchronous (`remove_node()` stops timers immediately), leaving the deferred closing queue with no producer or consumer.
- **`Node::interpreter()`** — the sibling-`:config` CommandInterpreter getter; the protected `$interpreter` property stays. Tests reach the sibling via `Core::node( "{$name}:config" )` or reflection.
- **More unused accessors** removed by the dead-code audit: `Core::counter()`, the rate-limited `Core::print_least_often()` and `Node::print_least_often()` ("least" variant — the commonly-used `print_less_often` stays), `Lock_Node::path()`, `Lock_Node::request_restart()`, `Topic_Node::num_partitions()`, `Shell_Node::set_variable()` / `show_parse()` / `send_command()` / `write_failures()`, `Settings_Renderer::checkbox_list()`, `Config::set_user_dir()`, `Config::declared_offsetlog_dirs()`, `Config_Utils::sanitize_option()`, and `Config_Utils::sanitize_string()`.

### Fixed

- **SSE dashboards resume from the exact offset on reconnect — no dropped lines on refocus, no gap on a watchdog recovery, no full-window replay.** Every record already carries its source position in `message[ID]` (`"{seg}:{offset}"`, stamped by the Consumer) and its `<sub>.p<partition>` in `FROM`, so the client now tracks the last seen `{seg,off}` per sub+partition (`SseConnectorNode.lastPositions` / `resumePositions()`). A reconnect — the heartbeat-watchdog forced reopen AND a page-visibility refocus — now seeds `positions` from that tracked offset, so the server replays only what was missed (the server already accepted a `{seg,off}` seed; the missing half was the client knowing its position). Applied to every SSE dashboard via `RemoteLink.resumePositions()`: **Raw Logs** (was a blind tail-seek that silently dropped hidden-tab lines) and the **Overview Topics chart** (was a tail-follow that left a gap). When nothing's been tracked yet it falls back to a tail-seek, so a history-seeded link is never re-streamed wholesale. The console (`RemoteIpc`, command replies with correlation ids, not `seg:off`) tracks nothing and correctly tails. All SSE dashboards still disconnect on visibility loss; the watchdog is dormant while closed.
- **Poll-based dashboards refresh immediately on tab re-show, flag a wedged channel as disconnected, and no longer spike worker rates after a long hidden gap.** (1) `useDashboardGraph` fired its only immediate poll at mount, so a hidden→visible transition showed the pre-hide snapshot for up to a full `refreshMs`; it now fires one immediate poll on becoming visible (guarded so initial mount doesn't double-poll), behind the existing visibility gate (still no polling while hidden). (2) The Topology Manager's `connected` reflected only the last poll's error flag, so a wedged/paused channel that returns no error kept the banner green forever; it now also reflects poll FRESHNESS — a last-successful-poll clock drives `deriveConnected`, which marks the dashboard disconnected when that clock is staler than `STALE_POLL_INTERVALS` poll intervals *while the page is visible* (a hidden tab's paused polling is expected, not a disconnect). (3) `WorkerStatusTransformNode` held its probe-cadence rate + segment-animation state across a hide/show gap with no time-awareness, so the first resumed poll divided a whole-gap delta into a one-frame rate spike; it now rebaselines (rate 0, fresh segment baseline) when the inter-sample gap exceeds `GAP_INTERVALS` heartbeat intervals.
- **The worker tree binds each probe row to the right consumer, stops re-rendering the whole tree every poll, and evicts a consumer that died during an outage on schedule.** (1) `reconstructWorkers` disambiguated consumers sharing a source with `String(reader).includes(handler)` — a loose substring, so a handler `req` wrongly claimed reader `prereq.p0`; it now matches by anchored identity (`reader === name`, or `name` followed by a `._-` separator), never mid-token. (2) `TreeEntity`'s `memo` was defeated by `{...props}` spread + unmemoized `NodeRow`/`LogRows`, so every 4s poll re-rendered the entire expanded tree; it now forwards only the named props each child uses and memoizes the row components, so an unchanged subtree doesn't re-render. (3) The per-reader read-step (`steppedRate`/`cursorBytes`) was recomputed once per topology (N×M, most discarded) — hoisted to a single up-front per-reader map (identical output). (4) The topic-probe resume re-baseline now SHIFTS each consumer's lease forward by the outage duration instead of granting a fresh full TTL, so a consumer that went silent before the outage keeps its real remaining lease and still evicts on schedule (resumed consumers still get a fresh lease as their frames land).
- **SSE streams now actively reconnect on a heartbeat timeout instead of trusting the browser's auto-reconnect, and the Overview Topics charts no longer blank out after a long-hidden tab.** (1) `SseConnectorNode` (the shared base of every dashboard + console stream) opened an `EventSource` and tracked `lastEventTime` but had no `onerror` and no watchdog — a half-open socket (worker reaped without a clean FIN, a proxy holding the connection) left the stream `OPEN` with no frames, the browser never fired its internal retry, and the dashboard silently went stale forever. It now runs a heartbeat-timeout watchdog: the server beats every 2s, and after ~10s of total silence (3 missed beats + a grace window so the stream can self-recover first — it does NOT force on the first missed beat) it tears down and reopens; it also reopens on an `error` once `readyState` is `CLOSED` (nonce expiry / a deploy 401, where the browser has given up), while leaving `CONNECTING` to the browser. A recovery reconnect **tail-follows** (clears the positions seed) so a history-seeded link doesn't re-stream its whole 24h window on every drop, and the connector now clears the watchdog + closes the stream in `removeNode()` so a direct removal can't leak the interval. (2) `TopicProbeViewNode` evicted any consumer whose last frame predated a 5-minute wall-clock window — but while the tab is hidden the stream is closed and NO frames arrive, so the first frame after a >5-min hidden gap wiped every consumer (the three Topics panels blanked). Eviction now measures live-stream time: a fill arriving after a gap larger than the TTL re-baselines every consumer's lease before the stale sweep, so an outage no longer counts as the consumers dying.
- **The topology-console palette drag works in Firefox, and its drag ghost/glyphs reflect each node's I/O.** The palette → canvas node drop used native HTML5 drag-and-drop, which silently fails to initiate in Firefox; it now uses the same pointer-events drag the Overview rows use (a ghost follows the cursor, pointer-up hit-tests the canvas SVG and projects the drop). The drag ghost renders the **actual node card** (header, class name, schema-gated in/out ports) as it looks once dropped, and each palette glyph's connector dots are driven by the node schema — `accepts_fill` shows the left/in dot, `has_target` the right/out dot — instead of always drawing both. The console canvas autofit in edit mode no longer reserves a bottom band for the (hidden) REPL transcript.
- **The worker-tree segment bar no longer flaps between live segments and "No segments" every poll, and now paints three regions from each tree's OWN consumer.** The bar's segments came from a GLOBAL, last-consumer-wins trim (`reconstructWorkers`' `endByKey` map, overwritten per consumer row), so with ≥2 consumers of a log and an unstable `consumers[]` order the "winning" recorded end flipped each poll — the trim flipped — and segments appeared then vanished, in lockstep across every tree that shared the log. The bar now carries the FULL live segments through (no global trim) and derives its regions PER TREE: green = read (0→cursor), then the recorded backlog (cursor→its own probe end) is **yellow** when that lag stays within the segment the cursor is in and **red** when it spans a segment boundary (a bigger fall-behind), gray = live bytes on disk past the recorded end (recorded end→live head). Each consumer's recorded `end_seg`/`end_size` rides on its `inputs_status` and is merged per-topology in `collectLogPartitions` alongside the cursor, so two trees of the same log never share each other's offsets. A tree with no consumer of the log (e.g. firehose-as-partition under `aggregator`) has no cursor/end and renders entirely gray, never pulling a probe offset from another tree. `trimToSnapshot` and `endByKey` are removed (dead after the bar stopped trimming); the read-rate (`cursorBytes`) and write-rate (`endPosition`) paths are unchanged.
- **The Overview dashboard segment bar now trims to the consumer offset on partition 1+ (not just P0).** Every concrete per-partition log dir in the `dump_graph` `logs[]` payload was stamped with a hardcoded `partition: 0`, while the consumer rows carried their real partition (parsed from the probe reader name). The dashboard joins `logs[]` to `consumers[]` on `${name}#${partition}`, so the keys only met when the real partition was 0 — P1+ missed the join and fell through to showing raw live disk segments that updated every poll instead of trimming to the ~15s probe snapshot. The partition number is now threaded through from the resolver's enumeration (the loop index paired with the concrete dir name) — `Topology_Registry::resolved_resource_dirs()` and `Log_Cleaner::producer_log_dirs()` / `declared_dirs()` now carry `name => partition` maps, a new `Log_Cleaner::declared_log_partitions()` exposes that map, and `Workers_CI_Node::enumerate_logs()` stamps each log entry with its real enumerated partition. The partition is never parsed back out of a directory name, and `declared_log_dirs()` still returns the same NAME set (the GC sweep is byte-for-byte unchanged). `Log` file-sinks (`build_log_sink_entry`) stay partition 0 — a `Log` is a single non-partitioned rotated file, not a per-partition fan-out, so its hardcoded 0 was correct.
- **Settings-sync ships array option values as JSON, not a lossy comma-flatten.** `Settings_Sync_Node::scalarize()` collapsed arrays with `implode(',', …)`, silently dropping associative-array KEYS — so an option stored as a map (e.g. ELN's `custom_events`, `{event_name => true}`) synced to spokes as a meaningless `1,1,1,…`, and once the spoke's receiver csv-split + `update_option`'d it, the map was overwritten with a list of `'1'`s (all custom-event enable state lost; only the hub/sender kept its copy, which is why it looked fine there). Array values now serialize as `json_encode( …, JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES )` — lossless for both lists and assoc maps — riding the wire as one `Command_Args`-quoted positional token; the receiver `json_decode`s it (comma-split fallback for legacy senders). An unencodable value (malformed UTF-8 etc.) is now SKIPPED with a rate-limited log instead of shipping an empty token that would wipe the option on the spoke (`scalarize` returns null → `push()` drops it).
- **Aggregator Status no longer shows a connected spoke as DISCONNECTED / PENDING.** The dashboard read the wrong memcache key, so every spoke cache-missed and rendered offline (all heartbeats `-`) even while the aggregator was pulling fine. `Remote_Link_Node::status_key()` wrote `np:remote:<remote_partition>` (e.g. `np:remote:firehose.p0`) while `Aggregator_CI_Node`'s `status` verb read `np:remote:<node-name>:p<partition>` — two formats that never met (both sides had green tests pinning incompatible keys; the writer→reader round trip was untested). The reader was also built on an obsolete 3-arg make_node layout (partition at `args[2]`) that never matched `Remote_Link`'s real 2-arg schema (`<vault-id> <remote_partition>`), and it emitted a single partition per node when the node actually fans across every configured partition via the `<partition>` token. Now the writer keys by **node name + remote_partition** (`np:remote:<node-name>:<remote_partition>` — collision-safe for multiple spokes sharing a source), and the reader reconstructs that exact key for each partition `0..Bootstrap::num_partitions_for('aggregator')-1`, substituting the partition token via a new shared `Core::resolve_partition_template()` (the single substitution rule also used by `Topology_Registry::resolved_resource_dirs`, so the GC dirs and the dashboard keys can't drift).
- **Two topologies tailing the same source no longer render duplicate partition rows in the worker tree.** When separate topologies read one log via distinct offsetlogs (e.g. `request-builder` + `job-router` both tail `firehose.p<N>`), `reconstructWorkers` matched the global probe rows to each topology's consumer **by source only**, so each topology's node showed every reader of that source — `P0 P0 P1 P1`, each pill the wrong topology's lag. The graph payload now carries each consumer's offsetlog basename as its unique `reader` id (`Topology_Registry::graph_for`), and the join matches probe rows by that reader (falling back to source for single-reader consumers) — so each topology shows exactly one row per partition with its own `behind`. The same-topology tee fan-out (one consumer → several processors) is unchanged.
- **The settings-sync log (`settings.p0`) is no longer deleted by the log GC, and is written with explicit storage geometry.** `Settings_Event_Writer` writes watched-option-change events to `settings.p0` via a transient `settings:writer` Partition; the orphan sweep (`Log_Cleaner`) didn't know about it (it's declared by no `.tsl`, only consumed downstream) and reaped it. It's now whitelisted in `Log_Cleaner::declared_log_dirs()` (mirroring `topicprobe.p0`), and the writer pins explicit geometry — 5 MiB segments, 2 segments, 1-day retention (`SETTINGS_SEGMENT_SIZE`/`SETTINGS_NUM_SEGMENTS`/`SETTINGS_MAX_LIFESPAN`) — instead of the global defaults. The `_settings:log` node name is reserved (`Node_Names` + `reserved-node-names.json`).
- **The dashboard log catalog is sourced from the GC's declared set, so an externally-written log a topology only consumes shows its segments instead of "No segments".** `Workers_CI_Node::enumerate_logs` (the `dump_graph` `logs[]` the worker-status segment bars read) built its own union of active-topology resolved logs + `Log_Cleaner::producer_log_dirs()`, which omitted logs written outside any `.tsl` and only *consumed* by a topology (e.g. `settings.p0`, the auto-mounted `topicprobe.p0`) — their nodes rendered "No segments" despite real on-disk segments. It now iterates `Log_Cleaner::declared_log_dirs()` — the same set the GC sweep retains — so the catalog and the retention set can't drift. (The per-topology `graph` payload stays active-only; inactive logs in the catalog are inert, referenced by no active graph.)
- **Command error messages no longer show HTML entities (`&#039;`, `&lt;`, `&gt;`) in the dashboard or the cli.** Verb handlers `esc_html()` their dynamic throw messages to satisfy the phpcs `EscapeOutput` sniff, but `interpret()` placed that HTML-escaped string verbatim into the `TM_ERROR` payload — which is plain text bound for a JSON→React text node (which re-escapes) and the `wp nodes` cli terminal, neither an HTML sink. So a topology activate write-conflict (and every other verb error) rendered as `activating &#039;perf&#039; conflicts: a &lt;b&gt;`. `interpret()` now `html_entity_decode()`s the caught message, normalizing it to plain text at the data boundary — fixing the modal and the cli for all throw sites at once.


- **Topology Console: clicking NEW in the editor no longer blanks the whole editor body.** `handleNew` built a raw `{ nodes: [], edges: [] }` draft without the `_repl` anchor that `handleModeChange` adds, so the layout graph had zero nodes, `layoutReady` stayed false, and `ConsoleShell` fell through to the building placeholder — no palette, canvas, or inspector. `handleNew` now wraps the blank draft in `withReplAnchor()` like entering edit mode does.
- **Topology Console (Newspack / Newspack Brand skins): the routing target dropdown is now rounded like the other controls.** The Newspack skins round the header selects, mode group, inspector action buttons, and the `+ add target` chip, but the routing **target** select (`.topology-edit-row__input`) was left with square corners — visibly inconsistent next to the rounded buttons and PATH/SKIN dropdowns. It now gets the same 8px radius.
- **`Remote_Source_Node` ages out a stale heartbeat round-trip so the dashboard Status badge reflects liveness.** `last_heartbeat_response` was a write-once timestamp (set on each `workers.heartbeat` reply, never cleared), so a spoke that went dark would latch the Status badge on `success`. `publish_status()` now nulls `last_heartbeat_response`/`last_heartbeat_rtt` in the snapshot whenever the link is disconnected OR no reply has landed within the node's own `HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL * 4` window (the same span as the slot TTL) — restoring the old clear-on-disconnect semantics and extending them to the connected-but-stale case.
- **Aggregator Status dashboard now shows real per-spoke status (was stuck DISCONNECTED/PENDING).** The dashboard read status fields the current producer never writes (`last_connection_status`, `last_heartbeat_response_status`, `last_connection_error`, `last_connection_response`) — a contract drift from the pull-side cutover that replaced the monolithic ELN `Remote_Source_Node` with the substrate one. The view now derives from the substrate `np:remote:*` snapshot's actual fields: connection badge from `connected`, error from `last_error`, HTTP code from `last_http_code`, connected-count from `connected === true`; the stale `server.enabled` disabled-card styling is dropped. The heartbeat "Status" badge derives from `connected && last_heartbeat_response` (gated on `connected`, since `last_heartbeat_response` is a sticky timestamp that's never cleared — disconnected → `pending`, mirroring the old clear-on-disconnect).
- **`last_sse_heartbeat` ("Server HB") restored.** The deleted ELN `Remote_Source_Node::record_sse_heartbeat()` (which stamped `last_sse_heartbeat = Core::$now` on each received SSE `heartbeat` frame) was never re-implemented in the substrate, so the "Server HB" row went blank. `SSE_In_Node` now records the receipt timestamp on each `heartbeat` frame (reset to null on (re)connect), exposes it via `connection()`, and `Remote_Source_Node::publish_status()` writes it into the snapshot.


- **Topology Console left `_http`/`_heartbeat` orphaned on unmount → white-screen on the next tab.** Each `RemoteIpc` composes the shared reserved-name `_http`/`_heartbeat` singletons lazily (on first send) and deliberately leaves them registered when it tears down ("for the graph to tear down"), but `useConsoleGraph` never completed that contract. The orphaned `_http` survived the console unmount, so switching to a tab whose `useDashboardGraph` does `makeNode( 'HttpOut', '_http' )` (an unconditional register) threw `node name collision: _http already registered` and blanked the dashboard. The console teardown now removes both singletons (before tearing down the spine, so the Heartbeat can unregister from the live router's TIMER set).

### Internal

- **PHPStan now includes the ShipMonk dead-code detector.** `npm run lint:phpstan` runs through `phpstan-deadcode.neon`, so dead-code findings stay in the normal PHPStan/lint-staged/pre-push gate instead of being an opt-in sweep. `npm run lint:deadcode` remains as an alias for the same gate. Most findings on a substrate are public API / WP-CLI entrypoints / JS↔PHP wire constants / test seams, not real dead code — verify against all call paths before deleting.

## [0.18.3] - 2026-06-17

### Fixed

- **Topology console renders every invocation of a `multiple` verb, not just the first.** Two parts: (1) `Classes_CI`'s catalog strip dropped the command `multiple` flag (it kept only name/description/args), so the console never knew a verb was multi-invocation; it now carries `multiple` through. (2) The Inspector matched a verb spec to the *first* matching `verbInvocation` and drew one row, so a node wiring N independent mappings (e.g. settings-sync's 13 `add_setting` lines) showed only 1. A `multiple: true` verb now renders one editable row per invocation, each with a remove (×), plus an "Add" button to append a new one.
- **`Settings_Sync_Node::push()` invalidates the WP options cache before reading.** The long-lived hub-control worker froze an `alloptions` snapshot at its first read, so `push()` shipped stale values: a setting reset-to-default (and `remote_*` edits) stayed invisible to the worker until it respawned (~10 min), looking like "only the periodic sync works." `push()` now drops the options cache first (a static `$invalidate_options_cache` closure seam defaulting to `Config::invalidate_options_cache()`), mirroring `Discovery_Collector_Node`, so every push reflects the current value.
- **`cd /_sse` (request-scope REPL pivot) replies reach the console again.** A `cd /_sse` command is interpreted in the `/command` request process and its reply comes back synchronously in the POST body addressed `TO=_sse:{pid}/_output`; that reply must re-enter through the `_sse` node, which strips its own `_sse:{pid}` head. The "everything sinks into the interpreter" refactor had re-pointed `HttpOut._post()`'s reply intake from the `_sse` node to `this.sink` (the interpreter), so the body reply hit `_router`, failed to peel the unregistered `_sse:{pid}` head, and dropped (`NOT_AVAILABLE`). `_post()` now feeds replies into `Core.node('_sse') ?? this.sink` — restoring `_sse` as the synchronous-intake convergence point while keeping `sink`=interpreter for the canvas. Worker pivots (`/_sse/{worker}`) were unaffected (their replies arrive async over the EventSource straight into `_sse`).
- **The Topology dashboard resolves a `Topic`'s `{partition}` template instead of rendering the literal token.** The graph view recognized only the `<partition>` (angle) token, so a multi-partition `Topic` vertex (`firehose.p{partition}` — e.g. the aggregator's hub fan-in) showed the literal token with "No segments" rather than grouping into one `firehose` entity with its concrete per-partition rows. It now matches both the `<partition>` and `{partition}` tokens.

### Removed

- **Dead `Topology_Registry::basenames_for()` (and its test)** — superseded by the layout-agnostic `resolved_resource_dirs()` (which the GC and dashboard already use). It had no production caller; the `<partition>`-only, Partition-only regex was a stale parser.

## [0.18.2] - 2026-06-17

### Changed

- **`Command_Interpreter_Node` now logs unauthorized commands instead of failing silently.** A rejected command emits a rate-limited `WARNING: unauthorized: <verb> - TM_COMMAND from: <path> payload: ...` audit line (via `drop_message`). This surfaces cross-session REPL/IPC traffic — e.g. a `cmd` issued in one pivoted session reaching another session's interpreter, where the auth gate correctly refuses it. Rejection behavior is unchanged (still refused with a `TM_COMMAND|TM_ERROR` reply); this only adds the audit log.

## [0.18.1] - 2026-06-17

### Fixed

- **`wp nodes cli` no longer dumps other sessions' IPC (including browser traffic) into a pivoted REPL.** The reply path read the shared worker output partition with a `Consumer` targeting `_output`, and `Consumer::forward_line()` *unconditionally* overwrites each emitted message's TO with its target — so every session's replies were rewritten to `_output` and the Dumper's per-PID `to_filter` could no longer drop the ones addressed elsewhere. The output-IPC consumer now sinks through a plain `Node`, which stamps TO from its target only when TO is empty (soft route), so each reply keeps its own TO and other sessions are filtered out. A regression test pins the `Consumer` (unconditional) vs `Node` (soft) TO behavior.

### Security

- **Pinned the `@babel/core` (≤7.29.0) and `js-yaml` (≤4.1.1) transitive dev dependencies to patched versions** (`^7.29.6` / `^4.2.0`) via `overrides`, clearing the Dependabot advisories that had no auto-fix PR.

### Changed

- **Release CI now builds on Node 24 / npm 11** (was Node 20 / npm 10), matching contributors' toolchain so committed lockfiles stay in sync.

## [0.18.0] - 2026-06-17

### Added

- **Consumer gains an opt-in `line_mode` (config verb `set_line_mode`) that forwards one line per poll instead of a whole read-block.** A Consumer whose sink does heavy per-message work (e.g. an LLM enrich) would otherwise drain a full 64 KB block in one `fire()`, freezing the worker's heartbeat through the burst; line mode spreads it across drain cycles so the worker stays live. Enable it in a topology before the first poll: `cmd <consumer>:config set_line_mode`. Default (batch) behavior is unchanged — `poll_active` still pipelines a read every tick; line mode reads only once the buffer is dry of complete lines. Internally `drain_buffer()` is one offset-scanning pass capped by `line_mode` (1 line) or unbounded (batch), a single O(n) scan that also fixes a latent batch-mode cursor drift on an empty (`\n\n`) line (the old `rtrim`+`explode` dropped the empty line's byte, mis-aligning the next read), and `forward_line()` is the per-line emit seam.

### Changed

- **`Tail` is line-only and overrides just the `forward_line()` emit seam, reusing Consumer's buffer/cursor scan.** Tail's duplicated drain loop is gone — it customizes only how a line is emitted (raw `TM_BYTESTREAM` bytes vs an unpacked Message). The unused `block-buffered` / `binary` `buffer_mode`s (set by no `.tsl` — only tests) are removed, and a Tail now supports `line_mode` for free. The inherited overflow guard logs the real runtime node class (`Tail:` / `Consumer:`).

### Changed

- **`arguments()` parsing is centralized in `Schema_Reflection::parse_schema_args()` — a missing token takes the arg's schema `default`, or throws if the arg is `required`.** This retires ADR-11's empty-string short-circuit (the recurring footgun where every config-bearing `arguments()` override had to mirror `if ( '' === $args ) return;` or derive filesystem-root junk like `/p0`). The parser now records the raw string into `$this->arguments` (so `dump_config()` still round-trips) and is the single source of truth for defaults. **Behavior change:** a bare `make_node <Type> <name>` of a node with a `required` arg (Partition `dir`, Consumer `source_dir`, Topic `dir_template`, Hook `hook_name`) now throws at construction (fail fast and loud) instead of yielding an unconfigured node that derives garbage. `Topic`'s `num_partitions` moved from `required` to `default 1` (belt-and-suspenders behind the usual `<config:num_partitions>` token). ADR-11 and its AGENTS.md row are updated to match.

- **Service CIs are no longer draggable in the topology console palette, but keep their inspector verb buttons.** Service CIs are mounted into the request graph (never `make_node`'d), so dragging one from the palette only mints a stray duplicate. The palette now skips the `Service` category, while the class stays in the Classes_CI catalog — so selecting a mounted Service CI in the canvas still renders its command/request buttons in the inspector (which resolves verbs via `catalog.find( shell_name )`, independent of the palette). This is the palette-only hide that `category: 'Hidden'` couldn't give (Hidden drops the class from the catalog entirely, killing the inspector buttons too).

### Added

- **The build kit externalizes `@wordpress/blocks` and `@wordpress/block-library`.** Added both to the kit's `WP_EXTERNALS` allow-list (`global: window.wp.blocks` / `window.wp.blockLibrary`, handles `wp-blocks` / `wp-block-library`), so a dashboard bundle that uses the editor's block APIs (e.g. `pasteHandler`, `registerCoreBlocks`) reuses WordPress's own enqueued registry instead of bundling a duplicate. Additive — only affects bundles that import those packages.

- **The DevTools hub tabs are deep-linkable, and Raw Logs can deep-link a selected log.** The hub page slug is now `newspack-nodes-hub` (was `newspack-nodes-topology`; `Admin::TOPOLOGY_MENU_SLUG` → `Admin::HUB_MENU_SLUG`), and each hub tab has a distinct URL: `?page=newspack-nodes-hub&tab=console` | `&tab=topologies` | `&tab=raw-logs`. `DevtoolsTabHost` gained an opt-in `syncUrl` prop (the hub passes it; the floating overlay and every other consumer stay URL-free): it reads the initial tab from `?tab=<slug>` and mirrors the active tab back via `history.replaceState` (no reload, no back-button spam), preserving sibling params. Tab descriptors gained an optional `slug` (defaults to the tab id). Raw Logs reads `?log=<name>` once on its first non-empty catalog to seed the selection (never clobbering a later user pick) and writes `?log=` when you choose a log. The Topology Manager's console links now point at `?page=newspack-nodes-hub&tab=console&topology=<name>`. No backward-compat redirect for the old slug. Each tab declares the query param it owns (Console `topology`, Raw Logs `log`); switching tabs drops the other tabs' params, so the URL only ever carries the active tab's.

### Fixed

- **Topology Manager headings align, and the redundant per-partition "stalled" pill is gone.** The per-partition stalled pill duplicated both the partition badge and the right-hand health rollup (three "stalled"s); it's removed, leaving the health rollup as the single stalled indicator. The per-partition cluster and the health field now have fixed-width slots so the badges line up across rows.

- **`Node::set_state()` no longer emits a PHP 8.4 deprecation.** Its `$payload` parameter was implicitly nullable (`string $payload = null`); it's now explicitly `?string $payload = null`.

- **The debug overlay behaves when you switch DevTools hub tabs.** Each hub tab now persists its OWN overlay canvas layout — the node-position key is per-tab (`…:hub:<tab>`) instead of one shared `…:hub:local` key that loaded one tab's positions as garbage anchors on every other tab (this happened even with the overlay closed during the switch). And the overlay's graph follows the active tab on a switch instead of collapsing to `_output` or freezing on the previous tab. The destructive auto-resync (a `resetGraph()` on the mount bump that tore down the freshly-mounted tab's nodes) is removed; the build-delegated mount bump is kept but now only makes the overlay rebuild its `_metadata` poll on the fresh backbone — the previous code left that poll hitchhiking the torn-down `_router`, so the graph froze on the old tab (clicking Reset Graph + Reset Layout was the manual workaround). The window position stays global (only node positions are per-tab); the manual chips still work.
- **Switching to the Console tab with the debug overlay open no longer white-screens.** It threw `node name collision: _output already registered`: the Console registers a `_output` node in its mount effect, while the overlay (gated off the Console tab, but via a one-commit-late `useEffect`) still held its own render-registered `_output`. `DevtoolsTabHost`'s tab button now reports the tab change to the host SYNCHRONOUSLY (in the click handler, not only the follow-up effect), so React batches it: the overlay unmounts in the SAME commit the Console mounts, and React runs the overlay's `_output`-removal (passive cleanup) before the Console's `_output`-registration (passive effect) — they never coexist.
- **Saving the Nodes Runtime settings no longer deactivates every topology.** The active-topology set (`newspack_nodes_topologies`) was a registered settings-group option that the settings page never rendered (the Topology Manager is the activation UI). WordPress's `options.php` sanitizes *every* registered option in the group from `$_POST` on Save, and an absent one ran through `sanitize_topologies(null)` → `[]`, wiping the active set on every Save. The field is now **overlay-only** (`ui: false`): still loaded + autoloaded for the per-request config overlay, but outside the settings group and the reset surface, so Save (and Reset) can't touch it. The activate/deactivate verbs + `Supervisor::check_config` keep the conflict protection the form sanitizer used to add, so the now-obsolete `Admin::sanitize_topologies` is removed.
- **Deactivating ALL topologies at once now stops every running worker instead of leaving them up for ~10 more minutes.** When the operator deactivates the whole fleet (e.g. toggling every topology off in the Topology Manager), `check_config()` short-circuited on the empty active set *before* `reconcile_lock_dirs()` — the established stop path — so no worker ever got a restart flag and each self-respawned until its lock aged out. The supervisor now drains every worker lock dir (`*.p<N>.lock.d`, leaving `supervisor.lock.d` untouched) before exiting when it previously had an active fleet. Cold start (nothing was ever active) still exits quietly and drops no flags.
- **The Topology Manager's supervisor restart button matches the per-topology restart buttons.** It shared the worker-tree's `worker-restart-btn` style (smaller, muted, different shape) while sitting directly above the per-topology `↻` controls; it now uses the same `nodes-tm__restart` style, and the now-unused `.worker-restart-btn` rule is removed.
- **Topology names get a fixed-width label column so their status pills line up.** The per-topology heading laid the name and pills in a flex row with no reserved label width, so the `P0`/`P1`/badge pills started at a different x on every row (name length drove it); a `min-width` on `.nodes-tm__name` aligns them into a consistent column.
- **The `example-ai-newsletter` demo now reads its own `example-scored` log, isolated from a real plugin.** Its topology wrote the scored partition/consumer to the bare `scored.p*` path and `Insights_CI_Demo` read the same — but that path is substrate-global, so the demo dashboard showed whatever real plugin's `scored` log happened to be populated (e.g. the product `newspack-ai-newsletter`'s), not its own deterministic data. The demo's scored partition, consumer offsets, and the demo CI's glob are now namespaced to `example-scored.p*` (its `digest.md` was already namespaced), so the example is self-contained.
- **SSE ingress rejects malformed typeless frames (no more `TYPE_UNKNOWN` on disconnect).** Every real SSE frame carries a type flag — the server packs full Messages, and even a tailed log line arrives as a `Consumer`-unpacked Message with the producer's type. So a typeless frame is always malformed: a partial/empty flush (e.g. while a container restarts) that `unpack()` turned into a pristine Message. Forwarding it only earned a rate-limited `_router: message not addressed - TYPE_UNKNOWN`. `SseConnectorNode` (and its `SseInNode` subclass) now drop any typeless frame at the ingress boundary and emit a rate-limited `Core.printLessOften` warning so the malformed frame is visible rather than silently confusing the router.
- **Build kit bundles `@wordpress/icons` instead of externalizing it.** The shared build kit mapped `@wordpress/icons` to a `window.wp.icons` global / `wp-icons` enqueue handle, but `@wordpress/icons` is a build-time package (SVG-as-React-components), not a runtime script — WP exposes no such global and registers no `wp-icons` handle, so the icon was `undefined` at runtime and WP 6.9.1 warns `WP_Scripts::add … dependencies that are not registered: wp-icons`. The kit now omits the mapping, so esbuild bundles the imported icons from `node_modules` and the bundle's `.asset.php` no longer lists `wp-icons`. (Affects any bundle importing `@wordpress/icons` — today only event-logger-nodes' `performance-logger`; rebuild + redeploy that plugin to clear the notice.)
- **Full-page Topology Console REPL transcript no longer overshoots the canvas under the DevtoolsTabHost tab bar.** The console's `ReplFooter` fell back to a viewport-based ceiling (`window.innerHeight − 134`, accounting only for the admin bar + 64px header + 38px repl bar) that never subtracted the Console/Topologies/Raw Logs tab bar the console now renders inside — so a maximized transcript grew ~39px past the canvas top into the header. `TopologyConsole` now measures its `.topology-app` grid height (via a `ResizeObserver`) and passes an explicit `maxHeightPx = max(80, appHeight − 64 − 38)` (the canvas row of the `64px 1fr 38px` grid), mirroring how the debug overlay's Inspector already measures + passes its own ceiling. The ceiling tracks window resizes and admin-menu collapse, so the transcript fills the canvas exactly and stops at its top edge.
- **Debug overlay REPL transcript no longer overflows past the panel under the new tab bar.** The Inspector body's transcript max-height (`replMaxHeight`) subtracted only the 64px header and 40px prompt bar from the panel height; the DevtoolsTabHost tab bar that now sits above the body went unaccounted for, so a maximized transcript ran under it. The transcript ceiling now also subtracts the tab bar's height, which is *measured* from the live `.nodes-devtools__tabbar` element (via a `ResizeObserver`, so it can't drift from `DevtoolsTabHost.scss` and reserves nothing on a single-tab host with no bar), still floored at 80px. The prompt-bar reserve was also corrected from 40px to its true 38px (`.topology-repl__bar`; the panel is content-box, so `frame.h` excludes its border), so the maximized transcript fills the canvas exactly instead of stopping 2px short.

### Security

- **Every Service CI verb now requires `manage_options` — gated by default in the base.** `Service_CI_Node::commands_from_schema()` wraps every derived handler so it calls `require_manage_options()` before the verb runs; there are no public/opt-out verbs. This closes auth gaps where per-verb opt-in was forgotten: `Classes_CI` (`list`), `Topologies_CI` (`list`, `get`, `connect_worker_input`), and `Workers_CI` (`list`, `restart`, `heartbeat`) were previously open and are now gated. The now-redundant per-verb `require_manage_options()` calls were removed from `Topologies_CI`, `Workers_CI`, `Layouts_CI`, and `Raw_Logs_CI` (the base gates them once).
