The auto-injected `help` verb (added by the base interpreter, not declared in any schema) is wrapped too, so it's gated like every schema verb. Worker requests remain excluded via the `NEWSPACK_NODES_WORKER_TYPE` env tag.

### Added

- **Topology Manager surfaces a rejected mutation's reason in an alert modal.** A failed `activate` / `deactivate` / `restart` (e.g. an activate that write-conflicts with the active set) used to be silently swallowed so the toggle just snapped back with no explanation. The manager now pops a one-button `AlertModal` titled with the topology name and showing the rejection reason (the verb's TM_ERROR text — for a conflict, the `describe_conflicts` phrasing), so any operator — not just the one who knows the rule — sees WHY. ESC / backdrop / OK dismiss; the render still never crashes on a rejection.
- **DevTools hub gains the floating debug overlay on every tab EXCEPT the Console.** The hub now renders `<DebugOverlay storageKey="newspack-nodes:debug:hub" />` (Ctrl+\` — the per-page live-graph + REPL inspector) for the Topologies/manager tab and any other non-console hub tab, so you can inspect that tab's live `Core.nodes` graph and run REPL commands against it. The overlay is gated OFF on the Console tab: the console is itself a graph view, so an overlay there nests a console-in-a-console (and binds to no `_command_interpreter`, the "no command interpreter — REPL command dropped" path). The non-console tabs mount the canonical `_command_interpreter` exospine (manager → `useTopologyManager` → `useDashboardGraph` → `mountExospine`), so the overlay's REPL binds to a real interpreter there. `DevtoolsTabHost` gained an optional `onActiveTabChange(id)` callback (fired on mount with the resolved active id and on every switch) so the hub knows which tab is showing; the overlay stays debug-gated as everywhere else (`?nodes-debug=1` / sticky flag).

- **Topology Console offers "Activate now?" after saving a brand-new topology.** A save whose name was NOT in the catalog the console already holds (`useTopologyCatalog` partitions) is a freshly-created topology — it saves inactive, so the console now raises a `ConfirmModal` ("Activate now?"). Confirming dispatches `topologies activate <name>` through the same command client the catalog uses for its verbs (`getCommandClient().send`), then reloads the catalog and toasts; a failed activate toasts rather than crashing, leaving the topology saved-but-inactive. Saving over an EXISTING topology (name already known) shows no prompt. "new vs existing" is snapshotted before the post-save catalog reload so a self-save never mis-classifies as new.
- **`topologies delete` prunes the now-orphaned name from the active set when no stock fallback remains.** Deleting a user `.tsl` that has no stock copy of the same name used to leave a dangling entry in `newspack_nodes_topologies` — an active topology the supervisor would keep trying to spawn against nothing. The `delete` verb now, when `stock_fallback` is false, removes the name from the active-set option (`array_diff`), invalidates the config cache via the same `Config::invalidate_options_cache()` + `Config::reset()` pair `activate`/`deactivate` use, and reports the action in a new `pruned_active` response field. When a stock copy still resolves (`stock_fallback` true), the active entry is left intact so the stock topology keeps running; `pruned_active` is always present (`false` in that case).
- **`topologies` CI gains `activate` / `deactivate` verbs — the first CI writers of the `newspack_nodes_topologies` active-set option.** `activate <name>` materializes the current effective active set (`Bootstrap::get_topologies()`), adds the name, persists the option, invalidates the config cache (the same `Config::invalidate_options_cache()` + `Config::reset()` pair, in the same order, `Supervisor::check_config()` uses so the next `expand_workers()` sees the write), then spawns the fleet immediately via `Supervisor::spawn_fleet()`; it returns `{name, active:true, spawned:<int>}` and refuses an unknown name by throwing `RuntimeException` (→ TM_ERROR), matching `get`/`save`/`delete`, before any write. `deactivate <name>` is the symmetric drain: remove the name, persist, invalidate, then `Supervisor::kill_readers([name])`, returning `{name, active:false}`. Both require `manage_options`.
- **DevTools tab registry — `@newspack-nodes/shared/devtools/tabRegistry`.** The substrate API for contributing a DevTools tab the way a plugin contributes a topology: drop-in, contributed, shadow-by-id. `registerDevtoolsTab( { id, label, host, component, order?, gate?, icon?, fullBleed? } )` (tabs are React, so the registry is JS; `host` ∈ `overlay | hub | both`; re-registering an id shadows the prior descriptor; `order` is normalized to a finite number so the sort comparator can never see `NaN`) and `getDevtoolsTabs( host )` (returns the host's tabs plus `both`, gate-passing, pre-sorted at registration by order then label — the per-call work is just the cheap host/gate filter). The first consumer is the debug overlay's built-in Inspector tab; the full-page admin hub host + PHP bundle registrar land in P2. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-14-devtools-hub-tab-api-design.md`.
- **Shared `DevtoolsTabHost` (`@newspack-nodes/shared/devtools/DevtoolsTabHost`).** The tab bar + selected-tab state + lazy-mount-the-selected-tab chrome, shared by both DevTools hosts (extracted from the overlay host). Renders the bar only with >1 tab, mounts only the selected tab (keyed so its build-before-render runs fresh on switch), and falls back to an `emptyState` when no tab matches the host; the routing `host` always wins over any caller-supplied `tabProps.host`.
- **DevTools hub admin page — Nodes → Hub.** A full-page, capability-gated (`manage_options`) host that renders `host: 'hub'` DevTools tabs through `DevtoolsTabHost`. Registered as a submenu under the existing "Nodes" menu; empty until a plugin registers a hub tab (the Topology Manager is the first, its own spec).
- **`newspack_nodes/devtools_tab_bundles` registrar.** A plugin returns `{ handle, dir, url[, localize] }` for its built tab bundle via this filter; `Admin` enqueues each on both hosts — the Hub page and the overlay-bearing Workers / Raw Logs pages — so a contributor's tabs register wherever their `host` targets (malformed entries skipped; `localize` narrowed to string keys).
- **Topology Manager hub tab — Nodes → Hub → Topologies.** The first `host: 'hub'` DevTools tab: a thin view over `useTopologyManager` listing EVERY topology (active + inactive) with provenance + an active toggle + a fleet restart. Each topology renders a heading (name + `N partitions` subtitle, a source badge — `stock` / `user only` / `user ▸ shadows stock` — and a `role="switch"` active toggle that immediately `activate`s/`deactivate`s) over its body: an active topology reuses the shipped worker-status `TopologySection` live subtree (built from its merged status section) and a `⟳` fleet-restart button; an inactive one shows a "Stopped" row. A rejected activate/deactivate/restart is swallowed so it can't crash the render. The substrate registers its own `event-dashboards` bundle (which carries this tab) via `Admin::register_event_dashboards_tab_bundle()` on the `newspack_nodes/devtools_tab_bundles` filter so the Hub page enqueues it (wp dedupes the double enqueue with Workers / Raw Logs by handle).
- **Topology Manager health & per-partition stall indicators.** Each active topology heading now carries a rolled-up Health dot+label (`stalled` if any partition is stalled, else `behind` if any consumer is behind > 0, else `ok`) and a per-partition `stalled` pill. A partition is stalled when its (process-level) `heartbeat_age` exceeds `heartbeat_interval_s × STALL_PAD` (`STALL_PAD = 3` — the pad tolerates a couple of missed beats without flicker). The `dump_graph` top-level `heartbeat_interval_s` (=`Worker_Base::HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_S`) is now threaded through `workerStatusTransform` into the worker-status model (sticky-scalar, defaulting to 10) so the manager hook can read it; no new poll or backend call is added.

### Changed

- **Example nodes are suffixed `_Demo` to disambiguate from the real `newspack-ai-newsletter` product.** Both plugins defined `Summarizer`/`Scorer`/`Digest_Builder`/`Insights_CI`, so the topology console palette showed duplicates AND `make_node` resolved the example's first (alphabetical `example-` < `newspack-`) — the real plugin's topology could silently instantiate the demo's nodes, and only one `insights` CI could mount into the shared request graph. The example's six node types now carry `_Demo` (`Summarizer_Demo`, `Scorer_Demo`, `Digest_Builder_Demo`, `Releases_Source_Demo`, `Community_Source_Demo`, `Insights_CI_Demo`), and its `Insights_CI_Demo` mounts as `insights-demo` (its dashboard polls `_http/insights-demo`) — so the demo and the product coexist without collision. Pure rename; example jest (36) + phpunit (36) unchanged.
- **Runtime triggers are `TM_REQUEST`, not `TM_COMMAND` — example + guides aligned to the convention.** Tachikoma convention: `TM_COMMAND` is startup/admin (graph construction, config, topology load); runtime triggers and live queries are `TM_REQUEST` handled in `fill()` → `handle_request()`, replying `TM_STRUCT|TM_RESPONSE` to `TO=FROM`. The `example-ai-newsletter` source/digest nodes now take their `TICK`/`FLUSH` triggers as `TM_REQUEST` (dropping the `Schema_Reflection`/`auto_wire_interpreter` `{node}:config` command path) and declare them under a `requests` node_schema key — so the topology console renders a `TM_REQUEST <verb>` button per trigger. Trigger from the REPL with `request_node <node> TICK` / `request_node digest FLUSH`. The guides (`writing-a-plugin.md`, `architecture-guide.md`, `getting-started.md`, `writing-a-dashboard.md`) now teach this convention and the `requests`-schema/button wiring.
- **The bundled AI-newsletter example is renamed `newspack-ai-newsletter` → `example-ai-newsletter`** (namespace `Newspack_AI_Newsletter` → `Example_AI_Newsletter`, text domain / menu slug / mount id / asset handle `newspack-ai-newsletter*` → `example-ai-newsletter*`, CSS prefix `nan-` → `eai-`, topology + log path `/tmp/example-ai-newsletter/`). Pure rename, no behavior change — the example's jest (36) + phpunit (33) match exactly. This frees the `newspack-ai-newsletter` name for the real sibling product plugin now being built on this substrate; the example stays as the teaching walkthrough.
- **Bundled AI-newsletter example: Publisher Insights is now a real analytics dashboard that drafts a newsletter.** Building on the standalone/design-system pass: the populated view is KPI stat cards (Total items / Top score / Sources), a "By source" set of proportion bars, and a score-ranked "Top items" table with inline score bars (decorative bars `aria-hidden`, the value announced adjacent) — all on the `DESIGN.product.md` scale (KPI number at text-xl 32/600). The "Draft newsletter" action now renders a styled preview, copies markdown (guarded — no crash on an insecure-context/missing `navigator.clipboard`, "Copied" only on a resolved write), and **creates a real WordPress draft post** via `apiFetch` (`POST /wp/v2/posts`, a `createDraft` prop seam for tests) showing an "Edit draft" link on success (or an error if the reply carries no post id). The `(untitled)`/`?` item fallback is now one shared `itemLabel()` used by the preview, the markdown, and the draft-post HTML (which escapes it). Shows what a credible dashboard built on the substrate looks like.
- **Bundled AI-newsletter example: the Publisher Insights dashboard is a proper standalone admin page now.** Three fixes (and the `docs/writing-a-dashboard.md` guide updated to match): (1) it registers its OWN top-level menu (`add_menu_page`, `dashicons-chart-bar`) instead of squatting as a submenu under the substrate's "Nodes" menu — a standalone plugin dashboard isn't a Nodes tool. (2) It no longer hides the WP admin bar + menu: the `.nan-insights` root dropped `position:fixed; inset:0; z-index` (that full-bleed/overlay pattern belongs to the Console + DevTools hub) and now lays out in flow inside `.wrap`. (3) Restyled to the Newspack in-product design system (`docs/DESIGN.product.md`) — light Cobalt-led surfaces, Inter, neutral cards/borders, functional error color — plus a real error notice (a failed poll) and an empty state (nothing scored yet) instead of rendering an empty table. The guide now points dashboard authors at `DESIGN.product.md` and at the top-level-menu / in-flow patterns.
- **Topology Manager floats active topologies to the top.** The list now sorts active topologies above inactive ones (alphabetical within each group), so the ones carrying a live worker tree worth watching sit first instead of being scattered by name.
- **Topology Manager heading: per-partition pills moved left, "N partitions" subtitle dropped, pill details fixed-width.** The P0/P1 process pills now sit immediately after the topology name (left of the source badge), so they scan vertically against the tree's per-node pills below; the source badge starts the right-hand group (`margin-left:auto`) with health, toggle and restart. The redundant "N partitions" subtitle is gone — the pills already convey the partition count. Each pill's uptime (`.supervisor-age`) and heartbeat (`.connector-heartbeat`) detail now has a fixed width (right-aligned) and the restart marker a reserved slot, so a pill doesn't reflow as its values change or a stall/restart marker blinks in (scoped to `.nodes-tm__heading` so the supervisor card is untouched).
- **Topology Manager / worker-status tree polish — one consolidated head, console-linked names.** Five changes to the hub tab and its embedded tree: (1) a node's per-partition read-status pills (`P0 R 0 B/s`, `P1 …`) lay out on ONE horizontal flex row (`.tree-node-row`, wrap-on-overflow) instead of stacking. (2) Every log renders as a group label (the log name) + one `P{n}` per-partition sub-row + fold caret — consistent whether or not the log feeds a downstream subtree; a source log renders its subtree once (no per-partition duplication). (3) `displayName` no longer Title-Cases node slugs — the tree is now 1:1 with the console (`job-router`, not `Job Router`). (4) Each topology's heading name is a link to the console scoped to that topology (`admin.php?page=newspack-nodes-topology&topology=<name>`). (5) The fleet-restart button keeps its heading placement but uses the larger `↻` icon, and `TopologySection`'s own header is removed entirely (it's tree-only now); the per-partition process pills (`.topology-partition` — P{n} + uptime + heartbeat + restart_pending) and the ALL RUN / ALL DEAD badge move up into the manager card heading, computed from the topology's `status.workers` via `partitionSummaries`. worker-status.scss (orphaned when the standalone Worker Status dashboard was deleted) is now `@use`d by `TopologyManager.scss` so the embedded tree and hoisted pills are styled.
- **Retired the topology-activation checkboxes from the Nodes Runtime settings page — the Topology Manager's per-topology active toggle is now the sole activation UI.** The `topologies` setting drops its `render` callback and section assignment from `Settings_Schema`, so the schema's field loop skips it (no `Topologies` settings section, no checkbox list). The `newspack_nodes_topologies` option itself stays registered, sanitized (`Admin::sanitize_topologies`), reset-wired, and autoloaded exactly as before — the activate/deactivate verbs (and the operator-override / reset paths) write it unchanged; only the redundant settings-page surface is gone. `Admin::topologies_callback()` and `Admin::topologies_section_callback()` are deleted.
- **Jest suites fail on an unexpected `console.warn` / `console.error`.** `jest.setup.js` now records every non-substrate `console.warn` and every `console.error` (React `act(...)` warnings, deprecations, genuine errors) and re-throws it in `afterEach` — failing the test instead of scrolling past it in the log. The bundled AI-newsletter example's setup gained the same gate. The full substrate suite (1891) and the example suite (36) both pass clean under it; the only latent issue it surfaced was an unwrapped `act()` warning in the Topology Manager "rejected mutation" test, fixed by awaiting the alert modal.

### Removed

- **Standalone Raw Logs dashboard — folded into the DevTools hub as a `host:'hub'` tab (Nodes → Hub → Raw Logs), now its sole home.** Deleted the `newspack-nodes-rawlogs` submenu (its `add_submenu_page` + `RAWLOGS_MENU_SLUG` const + its page-gate entries in `enqueue_event_dashboards_assets` / `enqueue_devtools_tab_bundles`), the `RawLogsPage` full-screen wrapper, and the `#newspack-nodes-rawlogs` mount in `event-dashboards/index.js`. The Raw Logs VIEW (`RawLogs.js`) is now registered as a `fullBleed` hub tab at order 20 (after Console and Topologies) via `event-dashboards/tabs.js` — it owns its own full-height canvas/scroll like the Console. `RawLogs` no longer renders its own `<DebugOverlay>`: the hub provides the floating Ctrl+\` overlay on every non-console tab, and the Raw Logs graph (`useRawLogsGraph`) mounts the canonical `_command_interpreter` exospine, so the overlay's REPL binds to a real interpreter there. `enqueue_event_dashboards_assets` and the `devtools_tab_bundles` registrar now gate on the top-level hub page (`TOPOLOGY_MENU_SLUG`) instead of the deleted Raw Logs page.
- **Standalone Worker Status dashboard — folded into the Topology Manager hub tab (Nodes → Hub → Topologies), now the sole worker-status home.** Deleted the `newspack-nodes-workers` submenu (its `add_submenu_page` + `WORKERS_MENU_SLUG` const + its page-gate entries in `enqueue_event_dashboards_assets` / `enqueue_devtools_tab_bundles`) and the React page it mounted (`WorkerStatus.js`, `WorkerStatusPage.js`, the `useWorkerStatusGraph` hook, and the `#newspack-nodes-workers` mount in `event-dashboards/index.js`). The reusable pieces stay: the `WorkerStatusTransform` / `WorkerStatusView` node classes (consumed by `useTopologyManager`), the extracted `SupervisorStatus` / `SegmentBar` / `TopologySection` / `TreeEntity` components, and the Raw Logs dashboard (which is itself folded into a hub tab — see above). The floating Ctrl+\` debug overlay is untouched.

### Fixed

- **DevTools overlay graph now reports real throughput and accurate ports for the dashboard view nodes.** Two display gaps the overlay exposed (the data plane was always fine): (1) `workerstatus:transform` / `workerstatus:view` / `topologymanager:view` / `rawlogs:view` override `fill()` (they're terminal/no-sink receivers, so base `Node.fill()` — which bumps `counter` — can't run) and so reported `counter: 0` in the overlay even while processing every reply; they now increment `this.counter` in their override. (2) Port visibility is driven by each node's `nodeSchema()` `accepts_fill` / `has_target`, defaulting to `true` (both ports) when a node declares none — so those receive-only view nodes (and `_http`, which POSTs out and routes replies back but never sets a graph `target`) drew a phantom out-port. They now declare `has_target: false`, so the canvas draws only the ports they actually use.
- **Topology Manager hub tab now faithfully embeds the Worker Status dashboard (four deployment-found divergences).** (1) The DevTools hub full-bleed wrapper got no `background`, so WP-admin white showed through behind the manager — it now sets `background: '#1e1e1e'` to match `WorkerStatusPage`. (2) The manager dropped the supervisor card; it now renders the shared `SupervisorStatus` component (extracted into its own `SupervisorStatus.js` module — the segment bar likewise lives in `SegmentBar.js` — both imported directly by the manager and the worker-status tree, decoupling the manager from `WorkerStatus.js`) above the topology list whenever the hook's pass-through `supervisor` is non-null, its restart wired to `restart('supervisor')`. (3) The supervisor uptime rendered as `NaN` because the manager never threaded the clock to the card: `useTopologyManager` now exposes the worker model's `currentTime`, and the manager passes it to `SupervisorStatus`. (4) Every active topology showed "No segments": the manager omitted the logs catalog (3rd `buildTopologySections` arg). `useTopologyManager`'s per-topology status now carries `logs: model.logs` and the active section's build passes it as the 3rd arg, so segment bars render.
- **`topologies` `activate`/`deactivate` verbs validate the topology name (`require_valid_name`, `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+$`) before any option write or spawn/drain**, matching `save`/`get`/`delete`. `deactivate` previously had no name guard and `activate` relied only on `resolve()`, so a malformed basename (path traversal, slashes, whitespace) could enter the `newspack_nodes_topologies` active set that downstream path-builders (`Bootstrap::num_partitions_for`, the GC declared-set, `synthesize_entry`) assume is file-name-safe.
- **Floating debug overlay's Inspector canvas renders full-height again, and the console's WP-admin-hiding CSS no longer leaks onto dashboard pages when the overlay is open.** The shared `DevtoolsTabHost` scroll-wrapper work regressed the overlay's full-screen graph Inspector. Three fixes: (1) the overlay's built-in Inspector tab is registered with `fullBleed: true` (mirroring the Console hub tab) so the host wraps it in `.is-full-bleed` (`overflow:hidden`, flex column) instead of the default scroll container. (2) The overlay height cascade is repaired through the two new wrappers between `.nodes-debug__panel` and `.topology-app`: `.nodes-devtools__tab-content.is-full-bleed` (already a `flex:1` flex column) → a new overlay-scoped `.nodes-debug__inspector` rule (`flex:1; min-height:0; display:flex; flex-direction:column`) → `.topology-app` (`flex:1; height:100%`), so the grid's `1fr` canvas row gets a bounded height again instead of collapsing against an auto-height parent. (3) The console's `body:has(.topology-app)` admin-chrome-hiding scope is tightened to `body:has(.nodes-devtools-hub .topology-app)` — the floating overlay also renders a `.topology-app`, so the bare selector fired `#wpfooter{display:none}` etc. on any dashboard page the moment the overlay opened; scoping to the hub wrapper limits them to the active Console hub tab.
- **DevTools Hub page can scroll again on non-console tabs, and the Topology Console's page-level CSS no longer leaks onto the hub.** The hub page slug (`newspack-nodes-topology`) is shared by the console standalone styles, so the console bundle's `body[class*="_page_newspack-nodes-topology"]` block — `#wpfooter { display:none }`, `#wpbody-content { padding-bottom:0 }`, etc. — applied to the whole hub page whenever the console bundle was enqueued, even when the long scrollable Topology Manager tab was active, cutting off its content with no scrollbar. Two fixes: (1) `DevtoolsTabHost` now mounts the active tab inside a per-tab scroll container (`.nodes-devtools__tab-content`) that scrolls vertically by default; a tab declaring `fullBleed: true` (the Console, which owns its own full-height canvas) opts out via `.is-full-bleed` (`overflow:hidden`, flex column). (2) The console's WP-admin-hiding rules are re-scoped from the body class to `body:has(.topology-app)` so they apply only while the console root is actually in the DOM (i.e. the Console tab is active), never on the manager tab or page scroll.
- **`topologies activate` and `Supervisor::spawn_fleet()` now refuse a write-conflicting topology (restores conflict protection lost when the activation checkboxes were retired).** Retiring the settings checkboxes removed the only UI path that ran `Topology_Registry::find_conflicts()`; the new `activate` verb did a raw `update_option` + `spawn_fleet()`, so an operator could activate a topology that write-conflicts with the active set (two topologies writing the same Partition/offsetlog) — corrupting one log with two fleets. Two layers restored: (1) the `activate` verb computes the prospective active set (`Bootstrap::get_topologies()` ∪ name), runs `find_conflicts`, and on a hit throws `RuntimeException` (→ TM_ERROR, phrased via `describe_conflicts`) BEFORE any option write or spawn, so a conflict is never persisted or spawned; (2) `Supervisor::spawn_fleet()` re-checks the same conflict set as defense-in-depth and refuses (logs via `Core::stderr`, returns 0, spawns nothing) — phrased identically to the existing `check_config` refusal. Killing already-running conflicting workers remains a separate concern; these two layers stop the operator from CREATING the conflict via the manager toggle / immediate spawn.
- **DevTools tab registry is now shared across separately-built bundles (P1 blocker).** The build emits each tab-bearing bundle (`devtools-hub`, `event-dashboards`, `topology-console`) as its own IIFE that inlines its OWN copy of `tabRegistry`. On the hub page all three load, so the prior module-local `tabs` Map / `sorted` memo gave each bundle a SEPARATE registry: the hub host (which imports no tabs) read its empty copy and rendered "No tools registered yet" while the Topologies and Console tabs registered into their own bundles' Maps — no tab ever appeared. The registry store is now a process-wide singleton on `window` (`__newspackNodesDevtoolsTabs`, holding both the `tabs` Map and the `sorted` memo), so every inlined copy shares one registry; register-side memo invalidation is visible to the hub's read. (jest masked it via the single shared module instance behind the `@newspack-nodes/shared` alias.)
- **The hub's debug overlay now renders the active tab's live graph instead of just `_output` (same cross-bundle root as the tab registry).** The runtime `Core` (`src/runtime/core.js`) was a module-local `new CoreImpl()`, so on the hub page — where the active tab's component runs from the `event-dashboards` bundle but the floating `DebugOverlay` runs from the `devtools-hub` bundle — each bundle inlined its OWN `Core` with its OWN `Core.nodes`. The tab built its exospine into one graph; the overlay read the other (empty but for its own `_output`). `Core` is now backed by the `window.__newspackNodesCore` singleton, exactly like the tab registry, so every separately-built copy shares ONE graph (one Core per page — which is what `exospine.js`'s `mountExospine` already documents and its backbone-reuse guard already assumes). Standalone single-bundle dashboards are unaffected (they always had one Core).
- **Bundled AI-newsletter example: removed the stale duplicate `digest.tsl` left by an incomplete rename.** A `rename digest.tsl → newspack-ai-newsletter.tsl` never deleted the original, so the example shipped TWO catalog topologies (`digest` and `newspack-ai-newsletter`) writing the SAME `scored.p<partition>` / `digest.md` paths — a collision if both were activated. The later migration work landed only on `newspack-ai-newsletter.tsl`; the `digest` leftover still used the pre-flat two-arg Partition form (`<config:logs_dir>/scored.log <partition>`, which the layout refactor removed — Partition's schema is `dir, segment_size, …`), so on that topology the dashboard read the partition's log name as `scored.log` (label) and `basenames_for`'s flat-only regex extracted no basename → "No segments". The leftover is deleted; `newspack-ai-newsletter.tsl` (flat `scored.p<partition>` Partition + Consumer-source, `Log <file> <segment_size> <num_segments>` = `digest.md 1 7` + `void_warranty`) is the sole, canonical example topology, and renders the partition as `scored` with its segments.
- **`topologies activate` on an unknown name now throws (→ TM_ERROR) instead of returning a plain error string.** A string return was packaged as a SUCCESS (TM_COMMAND|TM_RESPONSE), so the JS `await activate('bad')` resolved with the error text and the UI looked like it worked. It now `throw`s `RuntimeException` like the sibling `get`/`save`/`delete` verbs, which `interpret()` wraps as TM_ERROR so the client promise rejects.
- **Topology Console bundle entry no longer mounts a dead standalone root.** 5b removed the standalone console page (and its `#newspack-nodes-topology-console` div), but `src/topology-console/index.js` still called `createRoot` on it. The entry now only registers the Console hub tab (`import './tabs'`) plus its node/style side-effects; the dead `createRoot` + `DOMContentLoaded` wiring is gone.
- **Worker Status groups a log's partitions under one logical entity so a source log's consumer subtree renders once, not duplicated per partition.** The prior per-partition-flat presentation (one tree entity per concrete `firehose.p0`/`firehose.p1`) duplicated the entire downstream consumer subtree once per partition: a SOURCE log feeding a consumer (e.g. `firehose` read by Request Builder) rendered `firehose.p0` → full Request Builder subtree (completed, errors, requests, Flame Builder…) AND `firehose.p1` → a second identical copy. React-side fix (the PHP `enumerate_logs` catalog stays concrete-per-dir): the dashboard now GROUPS each log's concrete catalog entries into ONE logical entity, derived from the graph vertex's `<partition>` token — `topologyGraph.concreteLogNames()` returns each match with its partition number (the substituted token VALUE, the all-digits middle), `makeLog()` builds one entity named by the token-stripped vertex (`firehose.p<partition>` → `firehose`, `<partition>-req` → `req`) carrying the matched partitions as sub-rows, with the consumer subtree built ONCE; `makeVertex`/`makeSiblings` revert to the single-entity form. `TreeEntity.LogRows` again renders one P{partition} sub-row per partition (restored `.partition-label-inline` SCSS), and the coupled write/read-rate key is now the **concrete per-partition catalog name** (`firehose.p0`) — byte-identical by construction on both the render (`LogRows`, keyed on the partition's concrete `name` stamped by `makeLog()`) and transform (`workerStatusTransform.recordLog`, keyed on the worker-status `log.name` verbatim) sides, with NO logical derivation in the key path; the logical name stays display-only (group header + `P{partition}` labels). This closes a layout fragility — the prior `${logicalName}-${partition}` key (recovered via `splitConcreteLog()`) agreed across sides only for the `.p{N}` layout and diverged for any other partition-token placement, dropping rates to 0 and stalling segment animations; `splitConcreteLog()` is removed. The fold position-key is one per logical entity per ancestry position again, so folding a log never folds a sibling copy.
- **Worker Status now shows the request-scope producer logs (firehose / jobintake).** These logs are written by ELN's `Log_Manager` / `Job_Intake` in the flat `{producer}.p{N}` layout and declared in no `.tsl`, so the topology-driven `dump_graph` enumeration gave them no catalog entry — the React `firehose.p<partition>` vertex found no concrete match and rendered the raw template token with "No segments". `Log_Cleaner` now exposes a public `producer_log_dirs()` helper (the `newspack_nodes/registered_log_producers` filter × clamped config num_partitions, in the literal `{producer}.p{N}` writer layout) that both the GC declared-set and `Workers_CI_Node::enumerate_logs()` derive from, so the dashboard catalog and the GC share one source; `enumerate_logs()` unions those concrete producer dirs after the topology loop (deduped), statting each like any other flat dir.
- **Raw Logs dashboard reads the flat partition-in-name layout.** It previously globbed/tailed the old nested `{base}/logs/{name}.log/p{N}/` layout, so the picker showed "No logs available" and tailing failed. `Log_Discovery::on_disk()` now globs the first-level dirs under `{base}/logs` and returns the concrete dir basenames verbatim (`firehose.p0`), no `.log` strip; `Raw_Logs_CI`'s `list_logs` keys+labels are those concrete names and `log_status` stats the single concrete dir (no `num_partitions` loop, no nested `/p{N}`), defaulting unknown/empty keys to a `firehose`-prefixed concrete key when present else the first discovered; and the SSE `open_subscription` log-fallback + bare-name fan-out paths build the flat `{base}/logs/{name}.p{N}` consumer dirs. Also corrects the admin storage estimate for the concrete-dir `Log_Discovery::on_disk()` contract: `Admin::total_storage_callback()` no longer multiplies by `num_partitions` separately (the partition dimension is already in the per-partition dir count), which had inflated the estimate ~Nx; the formula is now `segment_size × num_segments × (count of on-disk log-partition dirs)` and the breakdown caption reads "× N log partitions".

- **`Log` file-sinks now render as LOG entities (blue, with segment bars) in the Worker Status topology tree.** A `make_node Log <name> <file> [segment_size] [num_segments]` sink previously surfaced as a white logic node. Three layers: (A) `Topology_Registry::graph_for()` maps the `Log` class to kind `'log'`, emitting `writes` (file basename), `path` (raw path token), `segment_size`, and `num_segments` so the dump can stat the files; (B) `Workers_CI_Node`'s `dump_graph` enumerates each active topology's `Log` sinks, resolves any `<config:KEY>` in the path, stats the flat `{file}.{seg}` monotonic segments (id from the numeric suffix, highest id = current/newest), and appends a `logs` catalog entry per sink (`segment_size` from the Log's positional arg, never clobbering a real segmented-log entry); (C) the dashboard's `collapseGraph` treats kind `'log'` like partition/topic — the node collapses to a log vertex named by its file basename, rendered blue with segment bars from the catalog entry.
- **`Partition_Node::read_latest_value_at( $offsetlog_dir )` — read the newest offsetlog record's VALUE.** The walk to read the latest committed checkpoint (`get_segments(true)` → newest segment → `read_at` → last non-empty line → `Message::unpacked` → VALUE, guarded + try/caught) was duplicated in `CLI::read_offsetlog_entry()` and the `newspack-ai-newsletter` example's `Insights_CI::read_cache_items()`. Both now delegate to this one public reader; `CLI::read_offsetlog_entry()` becomes a one-line wrapper and the example keeps only its `cache.items` extraction. No behavior change (the example gains the reader's explicit `'' === $bytes` guard, which was already a no-op there via the empty-lines path).
- **`@newspack-nodes/shared/hooks/useDashboardGraph` — the shared mount + poll skeleton for poll-based dashboard hooks.** Exports `useDashboardGraph( { mountNodes, poll, refreshMs = 4000, commandClient } )` (owns the exospine mount, the `_http` HttpOut I/O boundary with the lazily-defaulted CommandClient seam, the `bumpBuild` re-render so `useNodeState` rebinds to freshly-mounted view nodes, the immediate mount-time poll, and the page-visibility-gated `setInterval` poll; returns `{ interpreterRef }` so consumers fire awaited verbs against the live interpreter) plus `makeOpId( prefix )` (the monotonic correlation-id helper duplicated in every dashboard hook). The substrate's `useWorkerStatusGraph` (keeping its persisted refresh interval + awaited `restart` verb, wired through the returned `interpreterRef`) and the `newspack-ai-newsletter` example's `useInsightsGraph` now delegate to it. No behavior change. (The SSE-based dashboard hooks — `useRawLogsGraph` and ELN's `useErrorLogGraph` / `useRequestLogGraph` / `useGyroscopeGraph` — are NOT poll skeletons and keep their `_sse`/`_heartbeat` mounts; ELN's `useAggregatorStatusGraph` is poll-based but deliberately ungated on visibility, so it stays as-is.)
- **`@newspack-nodes/shared/pendingReplies` — shared request/reply correlation for dashboard view nodes.** Exports `errorMessage( payload )` (the TM_ERROR payload→string coercion duplicated as `_errorMessage` across view nodes) and `class PendingReplies` (owns the `message[ID]`→`{resolve,reject}` Map: `add` / `has` / `size` / `settle( message )` → boolean / `rejectAll( reason )`). `workerStatusView` + `rawLogsView` (substrate) and the `newspack-ai-newsletter` example's `insightsView` now use it in place of an inline Map + local `_errorMessage`; their hooks call `view.replies.add(...)`. No behavior change. Also adds `@newspack-nodes/shared/test-utils/fakeCommandClient` (`makeFakeCommandClient( replyFor )`) — a shared HttpOut-seam double for hook/node tests.
- **`src/build-kit/` — a shared esbuild dashboard builder (`buildDashboards()`).** The ~85%-identical esbuild logic every consumer's `scripts/build.mjs` duplicated (the `@wordpress/*`→window-global externals, the SCSS plugin, the `index.asset.php` content-hash manifest, the RTL companion, watch vs one-shot) now lives in one module; each `build.mjs` is a ~25-line shell that injects its own `esbuild`/`sass`/`rtlcss` (so a sibling-checkout consumer with no `node_modules` of its own still resolves them) and declares only its aliases + entries. Build output is byte-identical. `npm run watch` now cleans `build/` first, matching `npm run build`. The kit also exports `createJestConfig()` — a jest config factory that bakes in the `@newspack-nodes/*` mappers, the optional React single-copy pins, and the css/scss style-mock ordering (it must come last, or an aliased style import resolves to the mock — a documented footgun), so each consumer's `jest.config.js` is a few lines.
- **`Admin::enqueue_react_page()` — a shared React-dashboard enqueue registrar.** A single static helper performs the enqueue mechanics every dashboard site duplicated: page-gate on `$_GET['page']` (accepts one slug or a list), `build/<tree>/index.js` existence gate, deps+version from the wp-scripts `index.asset.php` manifest (falling back to hardcoded deps + `filemtime`), the `index.css` sidecar (now also activating `index-rtl.css` via `wp_style_add_data` — a latent RTL gap no site previously closed), and the `NewspackNodesData` localize (caller `localize` keys merge in and override the `restUrl`/`nonce` defaults). It returns the script handle (or `null` when it did not enqueue) so callers layer per-tree extras on top. The substrate's `enqueue_topology_console_assets()` + `enqueue_event_dashboards_assets()`, the bundled `newspack-ai-newsletter` example, and `newspack-event-logger-nodes` all delegate to it.

### Changed

- **The DevTools hub is now the top-level "Nodes" landing page (Console + Topologies tabs); the standalone Topology Console page is removed.** The top-level `newspack-nodes-topology` menu page renders the hub mount (`#newspack-nodes-hub`) via `Admin::render_hub_page()` (was `render_topology_page()` printing the console div); the separate `newspack-nodes-hub` submenu and its `HUB_MENU_SLUG` const are gone. The hub bundle (`enqueue_devtools_hub_assets`) and contributor tab bundles (`enqueue_devtools_tab_bundles`) now enqueue on the top-level page (`TOPOLOGY_MENU_SLUG`) instead of the old hub submenu slug. The console loads on the hub as a contributor tab bundle: the new `Admin::register_topology_console_tab_bundle()` (hooked on `newspack_nodes/devtools_tab_bundles`) appends the `newspack-nodes-topology-console` bundle carrying the partition snapshot (`topologyPartitions` / `activeTopologies` / `configNumPartitions`) the React dropdown reads — the localize computation moved out of the deleted `enqueue_topology_console_assets()` (and its `admin_enqueue_scripts` hook) into that registrar. Workers / Raw Logs stay as submenus under "Nodes".
- **The debug overlay's `DebugPanel` is now a thin DevTools tab host.** It keeps the floating-window concerns (draggable/resizable frame via `useDebugFrame`, the non-passive wheel eater, the page-scroll lock callback ref, the resize-handle map) and adds a tab bar driven by `getDevtoolsTabs( 'overlay' )` that lazy-mounts ONLY the selected tab (keyed on the active tab id so each tab's build-before-render runs fresh on switch). The original body (the live-graph inspector + REPL) now lives entirely in `InspectorTab`, reached through the registry — the duplication between `DebugPanel` and `InspectorTab` is gone. `DebugOverlay` imports `./tabs` so the built-in Inspector tab registers before the host first renders; the tab bar is hidden when only one overlay tab is registered.

- **Worker Status now enumerates the flat partition-in-name log layout — one flat row per concrete partition dir, alpha-sorted, no `.p{N}` parsing.** `Workers_CI_Node`'s `dump_graph` log enumeration is driven off `Topology_Registry::resolved_resource_dirs( $name, Bootstrap::num_partitions_for( $name ) )` (the layout-agnostic Phase-1 resolver) instead of the old nested `logs/{name}.log/p{N}` scandir: for each active topology it emits ONE `logs` catalog entry per concrete partition dir, NAMED by that dir (`requests.p0`) and carrying that single partition's segments — the resolver's `0..N-1` expansion replaces the old `max(configured, max_disk+1)` padding (a missing dir stats to empty segments). `build_worker_status` / `build_log_status_entry` stat the flat concrete dir (`{logs_dir}/{name}.p{N}`) with no nested `/p{N}` and no `.p{N}` parse; a worker's `inputs` / `inputs_status[].name` is now the concrete physical dir (the Consumer's recorded `source_log`, already concrete in the flat layout, falling back to `{source_basename}.p{N}`). The dashboard tree renders each concrete catalog entry as its own flat log row (the old "single log entity + indented P0/P1 sub-rows" model is gone): a partition-token graph log-vertex (`graph_for` emits the `<partition>`-bearing basename verbatim) expands into one flat log entity per matching concrete catalog entry, matched parse-free by the vertex's literal text on either side of the `<partition>` token (never reading a partition number out) and rendered alpha-sorted (siblings `requests.p0`/`requests.p1` land adjacent — that's the grouping). The coupled write/read rate key in `workerStatusTransform` ↔ `TreeEntity` keys on the concrete entry name (`log.name`) on both sides identically — no `.log` strip, no `-partition` suffix. Dead `Workers_CI_Node::collect_partition_count_overrides()` (its only caller was the old padded enumeration) is removed, and the pre-P0 `scored.log <partition>` Partition/Consumer signatures in `docs/writing-a-dashboard.md` are corrected to the flat `scored.p<partition>` forms. Both partition-token matchers reject sibling logs that merely share a prefix: the dashboard's `concreteLogNames()` keeps a candidate only when the literal text brackets it AND the substituted middle is a non-empty all-digits partition number (so `firehose.p<partition>` no longer grabs `firehose.priority.p0`), and `Workers_CI_Node::segment_size_for()` requires a word boundary after the override basename (so a `job` override no longer scales `jobs.p0`).
- **`Log_Cleaner`'s declared-set is now layout-agnostic (resolved-path membership, no `.p{N}` regex).** `declared_log_dirs()` / `declared_offsetlog_dirs()` derive their keep-set from a new `Topology_Registry::resolved_resource_dirs( $name, $num_partitions )` resolver instead of `basenames_for()` / `offset_basenames_for()`. The resolver expands each `write_set` token over `0..num_partitions-1` (substituting BOTH `<partition>` angle and `{partition}` curly), resolves its `<config:…>` tokens, and takes the first path segment under `logs_dir` / `offsets_dir` — so the partition token may sit *anywhere* in a declared path (`<config:logs_dir>/<partition>-req` → `0-req`/`1-req`; nested `req/<partition>` → first-level `req`) instead of only the `{basename}.p{N}` suffix the old regex required. `sweep()` now globs `{dir}/*` (`GLOB_ONLYDIR`) and keeps purely by membership in that resolved set — the `/\.p\d+$/` regex is gone, so an undeclared first-level dir is an orphan and is swept regardless of its name shape. The producer union (`registered_log_producers` × clamped config num_partitions → `{producer}.p{N}`) stays literal — it is ELN's real fixed request-scope writer layout. Per-topology partition count is still the SPAWN-aligned `Bootstrap::num_partitions_for()` (not `synthesize_entry`), and each sweep stays independently fail-closed: **the config ROOT is now the gate, not just the declared-set emptiness.** A single-pass collector resolves `<config:logs_dir>` / `<config:offsets_dir>` ONCE and skips a sweep whenever that root is unresolvable (`''`), *regardless* of the producer union — closing a data-loss hole where an unresolvable `logs_dir` (token namespace not established in some context) left the producer names protecting the set non-empty while every topology dir was absent from it, so the sweep deleted every topology dir (`requests.p0`, …). The `declared_log_dirs()` / `declared_offsetlog_dirs()` accessors now derive from that one collector (no double pass over `Topology_Registry::list()`), returning `[]` on an unresolvable root. **In normal supervisor/WP-Cron operation the root always resolves** (`Bootstrap::ensure_runtime_wired()` registers the `<config:…>` namespace before the GC tick), so this is defense-in-depth for the data-loss-critical path. The `workers.cleanup_status` diagnostic verb now mirrors the real sweep — globbing `{logs}/*` (`GLOB_ONLYDIR`, no `.p{N}` regex) and taking basenames — so its `on_disk_basenames`/`orphans` match what the GC would actually delete (including non-`.p{N}`-shaped names like `0-req`). The shared `<ns:key>` token resolver is promoted to `Core::resolve_config_tokens()`, used by both `Topology_Registry` and `Workers_CI_Node`. Dead `Topology_Registry::offset_basenames_for()` (its only caller was the removed `declared_offsetlog_dirs` loop) is deleted. **Known edge:** a `Log` whose `<file>` nests into a `logs/` *subdir* would create a first-level dir not in any `write_set` (a `Log` is not in `write_set`) and would be swept — but no topology does this; a `Log`'s flat `{file}.{seg}` segments sit at the first level as *files*, which `GLOB_ONLYDIR` skips. **Known follow-up:** the resolver expands each `write_set` token over `Bootstrap::num_partitions_for()` (the topology fan-out), not a per-node Topic/Partition count arg — same behavior as the old `basenames_for × num_partitions_for`; a Topic whose own count differs from the topology default is out of scope here.
- **`Log_Cleaner` is now log-only, flat-layout, and liveness-free.** `cleanup_orphan_partitions( $base_dir )` (the `$num_partitions` param is gone) sweeps the flat `logs/{name}.p{N}/` and `offsets/{name}.p{N}/` data dirs against a *config-declared* set — every on-disk topology's `Topology_Registry` Partition basenames (for logs) and Consumer offsetlogs (for offsets), each expanded over the topology's **SPAWN-aligned** partition count (`Bootstrap::num_partitions_for()` — catalog → frontmatter → config default, clamped `[1, MAX_PARTITIONS]`, so the declared set can never be narrower than the fleet the supervisor actually spawns; a bare `synthesize_entry()` would default a frontmatter-less topology to 1 partition and sweep its live `.p1+` dirs), unioned with each PHP-registered producer from the new `apply_filters('newspack_nodes/registered_log_producers', [])` filter × the global config `num_partitions` (same clamp) — and runs every supervisor config-check tick (no `LOGS_DIRTY_OPTION` gate). It no longer reads worker locks, lock dirs, ipc dirs, or `CLI::ls_workers()`: a dir is kept iff its declaring topology still exists on disk, swept otherwise, regardless of liveness (the worker-vs-GC race is the Supervisor's concern via `reconcile_lock_dirs()`). Each sweep is independently fail-closed — an empty declared LOG set skips the log sweep, an empty declared OFFSET set skips the offset sweep — so a not-yet-loaded app never wipes live data. firehose/jobintake (request-scope producers in no `.tsl`) stay protected via `registered_log_producers`. The `declared_log_dirs()`/`declared_offsetlog_dirs()` accessors are config-derived (no `$base_dir` param). **Known limitation:** `write_set()` recognizes only `make_node Consumer` offsetlogs, not `make_node Tail`; no production or example `.tsl` uses Tail, so a Tail offsetlog under `offsets/*.p*` is not in the declared set and would be swept — extend the registry parser if a Tail-with-offsetlog topology ever ships.
- **Orphan `ipc/` dir GC moved from `Log_Cleaner` to the `Supervisor` tick.** The ipc-dir sweep (reaping `ipc/{type}.p{N}` dirs for workers no longer in the fleet) is worker-lifecycle, not log retention, so it left `Log_Cleaner::cleanup_orphan_partitions()` and became `Supervisor::cleanup_orphan_ipc()`, called every config-check tick (~15s) right after the log/offset sweep. It reuses the supervisor's already-built `worker_locks` fleet instead of re-enumerating workers via `Bootstrap::expand_workers()` + `CLI::ls_workers()`. A live worker's own `locks/{name}.lock.d` defers its ipc reap; an unrelated worker's lock does not. (`Log_Cleaner`'s own log/offset sweep was subsequently rewritten to the flat-layout, config-declared, liveness-free model — see the `Log_Cleaner is now log-only` entry above.)
- **Breaking (topology authors): `Partition`, `Consumer`, and `Topic` no longer take a standalone `partition` argument.** `Partition` takes a resolved `dir` (was `base_dir`+`partition`); `Consumer` takes `source_dir` + `offsetlog_dir` (was `source_base_dir`+`source_partition`+`offsetlog_dir`); `Topic` takes a `dir_template` carrying a `{partition}` token it substitutes per child via `str_replace`. The partition now lives in the path *name* — flat layout `logs/jobs.p0/{seg}.log` instead of `logs/jobs.log/p0/{seg}.log`, and `Consumer` offsetlogs flatten the same way (no inner `/p0`). `Partition::read_latest_value_at()`, `CLI::input_basename()`, `wp nodes status`'s per-Consumer "behind" calc, and `Topology_Registry`'s topology-enumeration parsers (`basenames_for` / `segment_size_overrides_for` / `write_set` offsetlog) all read the flat layout; IPC input/output mounts drop their redundant trailing `/p0`. (The supervisor GC's *log* orphan-sweep still scans the old nested layout — its layout-agnostic rewrite is deferred; it fails closed, deleting nothing, against flat dirs.) In-repo topologies are updated to the flat / `{partition}`-token forms. Existing on-disk data is in the old layout and should be cleared — no automatic migration.
- **Breaking (topology authors): `Log` is now a subclass of `Partition` (`Log extends Partition`).** A Log is a Partition that writes the message **VALUE** (the producer's payload, not the packed envelope) into a **file** layout `{file}.{seg}` (`out.log.0`, `out.log.1`, … — monotonic, highest id = current; no bare `{file}`). It inherits Partition's segments, size-driven rotation (`segment_size`), count+age retention (`num_segments`/`max_lifespan`), the multi-writer rotate lock, batch/flush, and `allow_large_writes()`/`void_warranty()` — including the **4 KB PIPE_BUF cap**, so a VALUE larger than 4 KB is dropped unless the cap is lifted (the old Log was uncapped). Args change to `make_node Log <name> <file> [segment_size] [num_segments]`. `fill()` drops TM_ERROR/TM_EOF/TM_REQUEST (append-only; EOF never closes it). In-repo topologies are updated; old on-disk `{file}`/`{file}.N` data is not read by the new layout (local-only — clear it, no automatic migration).
- **`Tail` is now a subclass of `Consumer` (`Tail extends Consumer`).** It reads a Log's `{file}.{seg}` segments via a `Log_Node` source and emits the raw bytes per `buffer_mode` (line/block/binary) as `TM_BYTESTREAM`, inheriting Consumer's durable offsetlog cursor, snapshot co-commit, live-position publish, behind/ETA, checkpoint cadence, and segment-roll follow. A fresh Tail with no durable cursor defaults to **end-of-file** (only bytes appended after start) and resumes from its offsetlog checkpoint on restart — fixing the old every-restart full re-read. Args: `make_node Tail <name> <source_file> [offsetlog_dir] [buffer_mode]`. (Known edge: in line/block mode, a non-newline-terminated trailing partial that lands exactly at a segment boundary is dropped on roll — not reachable for `\n`-terminated logs, which is every in-repo producer; a cross-segment partial carry is a future follow-up.)
- **Worker Status tree fold is per-instance and persisted.** Each tree entity's key now encodes its tree *position* (ancestry path, e.g. `firehose.log>request-builder>requests.log`), so folding a log/node collapses only *that* occurrence — a repeated multi-writer log or a node reached via two parents folds independently per place. Fold state persists to `localStorage` (`newspack-nodes-worker-status-collapsed`, a JSON array of position keys) and is **trimmed on each render to the keys currently on the page**, so a removed or renamed topology's stale fold entries are dropped.
- **Worker Status joins convergent sibling nodes onto one line.** When several logic nodes share the same downstream (e.g. `digest`'s `community` + `releases` both feed `summarizer`), they render comma-joined (`Community, Releases`) with the shared subtree drawn once instead of duplicating the whole `summarizer → … → digest:log` chain under each. Joining applies only when the siblings AND their shared downstream are all logic nodes with a non-empty shared subtree, so a multi-writer LOG still repeats under each writer and a tee's divergent sinks stay separate.
- **`workers.dump_metadata` renamed to `workers.dump_graph` and now carries a per-topology `.tsl` graph.** The dashboard verb's name reflects that it returns a topology graph (and disambiguates from `Command_Interpreter_Node`'s per-node `dump_metadata` canvas snapshot, untouched). The payload gains `graph` — a map of active-topology → `{ nodes, edges }` parsed from the topology `.tsl` via `Topology_Registry::graph_for()` (node `kind` from the `make_node` class token, the read/written log from the node's path/source arg, edges from `connect_node` + `set_*_target`). The Worker Status dashboard uses this `.tsl` graph as the topology *structure* (so producer→log→consumer nests correctly and Partition/Consumer nodes collapse into their log) and the existing `workers[]`/`logs` payload as the live-status overlay. Sole consumer is the substrate Worker Status dashboard, so the rename is internal.
- **Worker Status now renders one foldable node/log tree section per topology instead of a flat global pipeline.** The dashboard groups workers by `type` into `TopologySection`s (header with per-partition status/uptime/heartbeat pills + ALL RUN/ALL DEAD badge + fleet restart, followed by the topology's `TreeEntity` tree); the flat `pipeline-flow` render (with `WorkerConnector` + `LogSection` and the cross-type topo-sort) is removed. A log now renders only as a child of a producer/consumer node within its topology, so an orphan log with no producer/consumer worker is no longer shown on its own — that log is cruft the supervisor's housekeeping GC reaps anyway, so the flat view was only displaying soon-to-be-deleted dirs. Fold state is owned by `WorkerStatus`. The supervisor card, header chrome (title, total W/R rates, refresh select), connection banner, and loading gate are unchanged.
- **The Inspector's Routing section is now hidden for sink-only nodes (`has_target` false).** Both the live (view-mode) and edit-mode Routing sections gate on a new `nodeHasTarget( node, catalog )` resolver (`node.has_target ?? schema.has_target ?? true`) that mirrors the SchematicCanvas OUT-port gating — so a node that emits to no target (e.g. `Dumper`, `Log`, `Partition`, `Topic`, `Job_Worker`, `HttpIn`) no longer shows a target picker or a `target →` row. Consumers that inline the debug overlay (e.g. `newspack-event-logger-nodes`) must rebuild to pick this up.
- **Dashboard bundles now cache-bust on the wp-scripts content hash, not `filemtime`.** Because the registrar reads `index.asset.php`, the enqueued script version + dependencies come from the build manifest (e.g. `react-jsx-runtime`, `wp-element`, `wp-i18n`) instead of the previous hardcoded `[wp-element, wp-components, wp-api-fetch, wp-i18n]` + `filemtime` pair.
- **Live cursor positions are now keyed per-reader (offset-dir identity), not by the shared log path.** `Consumer_Node::position_key()` takes a single `reader_id` (the offset-dir name `{source_basename}.p{N}`) instead of `{source_base_dir, partition}`, and `Consumer_Node::publish_position()` publishes under that per-reader key (new `position_cache_key()` accessor; ephemeral readers with no offsetlog skip publishing). Previously two readers tailing the same log (e.g. `firehose` and `firehose.job-router`) clobbered one shared memcache key, so the Worker Status dashboard could show one reader's cursor for the other. `Workers_CI_Node::get_live_position()` reads the matching per-reader key, so each Consumer row now reports its own live position — and the disambiguated reader gets the live memcache value instead of falling back to the staler offsetlog checkpoint.
- **`wp nodes status` is now per-Consumer, sharing the dashboard's enumeration.** The per-Consumer offset-checkpoint scan moved into `CLI::consumer_rows()` / `CLI::read_offsetlog_entry()` (reading the real packed-Message checkpoints), and `Workers_CI_Node` now delegates to it — one source of truth for both the dashboard and the cli, instead of two divergent scans. `wp nodes status` gains a **Source** column and shows each Consumer's own Behind, keyed by its per-reader position (`CLI::live_position_for()`), against its real source log. Previously it probed `offsets/{worker_type}.p{N}` (worker types never match the source-named offset dirs) and read a flat checkpoint shape production doesn't write, so every worker's Behind was a `firehose`-reader guess. The now-unused per-worker `CLI::saved_position()` (same broken probe) is removed; `CLI::live_position()` / `input_basename()` remain for the per-worker `list` verb.

### Removed

- **`Log_Cleaner`'s worker-lock / live-descriptor coupling and its dirty/fleet options.** Gone: the `LOGS_DIRTY_OPTION` and `FLEET_DESCRIPTORS_OPTION` options (and the Supervisor / `Topologies_CI_Node` arming sites that set them), the `expected_basenames()` / `active_descriptors()` / `has_lock_for()` / `orphan_dirs()` methods, the nested `*.log/p{N}` glob model, the `num_partitions` sweep param (and the now-dead `Supervisor::$num_partitions` property + `num_partitions_for_test()` hook whose only reader was that param), and the substrate-side `newspack_nodes/expected_log_basenames` filter (replaced by `newspack_nodes/registered_log_producers`, a list of producer basenames). The log GC now derives its keep-set purely from config (topology declarations + producer registration) — see Changed. The `workers.cleanup_status` diagnostic verb drops its `logs_dirty_option` / `fleet_descriptors_option` fields and reports the flat-layout `on_disk_basenames` vs `declared_log_dirs()` diff.
- **`Log` overwrite mode, `max_size`/`max_rotations`, and the logrotate-style `.0` shift-rename / date-named pruning** (superseded by the inherited monotonic-segment model — `Log extends Partition`, see Changed). `Log` no longer carries its own file handle, `rotate()`/`shift_rotations()`/`prune_rotated()`, the `MODE_APPEND`/`MODE_OVERWRITE` constants, or a `rotate` TM_REQUEST.
- **`Tail`'s arbitrary-single-file polling, inode/size-shrink rotation detection, in-memory from-zero position, and the `READ_CHUNK` constant** (superseded by the inherited segmented-cursor model — `Tail extends Consumer`, see Changed). Tail now reads `{file}.{seg}` segments, not an arbitrary file.

### Fixed

- **Shell `var name=value` works without spaces, and `<…>` interpolation is quote-aware (PHP `Shell_Node` + JS `shell-node.js`).** `var foo=bar` (no spaces around `=`) silently did nothing — the `var` command required `=` as its own token, so only `var foo = bar` worked, diverging from the `.tsl` frontmatter parser. It now splits on the first `=` (value may be empty or multi-word, `=` preserved in the value) and **fails loud** on a missing `=` or empty name. Separately, `interpolate()` ran over the whole line before quote handling, so it expanded `<…>` even inside single quotes; it is now quote-aware — single quotes and backticks leave `<…>` **literal** (standard shell semantics), double quotes and unquoted text still expand. This enables deferring a token to a downstream binder, e.g. a `Topic` line `<config:logs_dir>/jobs.p'<partition>'` expands the dir now and hands the raw `<partition>` to `Topic`. Both the substrate PHP shell and the topology-console JS shell are fixed in lockstep.
- **IPC dirs for topologies/partitions no longer active are now garbage-collected.** Nothing swept `ipc/`, so an `ipc/<type>.pN` dir for a removed topology (e.g. `ipc/firehose-workers-and-jobs.p0`) lingered forever. `Supervisor::cleanup_orphan_ipc()` (see Changed — moved out of `Log_Cleaner`) purges any `ipc/*.pN` dir not in the active fleet's `{type}.p{N}` set, gated on the dir's own descriptor lock (`{type}.pN.lock.d`) so a same-type straggler defers it but unrelated workers don't. Legacy `ipc/<x>/locks` cruft (no `.pN` suffix) is skipped.
- **Offsetlog dirs orphaned by a source being dropped from a topology are now garbage-collected.** When a Consumer's source was removed from a topology, its `offsets/<name>.pN` dir leaked forever — the cleaner only ever swept `offsets/` by partition *number* (on fleet shrink), never by *name*, so it surfaced as a phantom consumer (e.g. `digest / summarized / -`). `Log_Cleaner::cleanup_orphan_partitions()` now also purges any `offsets/*.pN` dir whose basename isn't in the config-declared offset set (`Log_Cleaner::declared_offsetlog_dirs()`, which resolves each on-disk topology's TSL offset dirs via `Topology_Registry::resolved_resource_dirs()`). Guarded fail-closed so a not-yet-loaded app can't wipe live offsetlogs; arbitrary offsetlog names are handled (the match is by declared basename, not a string-mapped log name).
- **`Partition_Node::rotate_segment()` no longer leaves an empty `locks/` dir as a sibling of the log it writes.** The per-segment rotate lock was `mkdir`-ed under `dirname(base_dir)/locks` — leaving empty `locks/` cruft under `logs/`, `offsets/`, and every `ipc/<worker>.pN/`, because only the inner `.rotate.lock.d` was `@rmdir`'d in the `finally`. The lock now lives inside the partition's own data dir (`{base_dir}/p{N}/.rotate.lock.d`), so it's removed when that dir is rotated/GC'd and never creates a sibling.
- **Worker Status dashboard showed a phantom `firehose.job-router.log` with "No segments".** When two readers tail the same log under disambiguated offset dirs (`firehose` vs `firehose.job-router`, so each keeps a separate cursor), the `dump_metadata` builder inferred the input log name from the offset-dir name — minting a nonexistent `firehose.job-router.log` whose segment scan came up empty. The Consumer now records the real source log basename (`source_log`) in its offsetlog checkpoint, and `Workers_CI_Node` labels/scans segments by that real log while keeping the cursor lookup keyed by the offset-dir identity — so disambiguated readers render the correct shared log (with its segments) without bleeding cursors into each other. Falls back to the old offset-dir inference for checkpoints predating the `source_log` field.
- **Worker Status dashboard padded every log's partition rows to the global `num_partitions`, minting phantom slots.** A log declared in a `var num_partitions = 1` topology (e.g. `scored.log`) showed an empty `P1` row whenever the global default ran 2 partitions, because `Workers_CI_Node::enumerate_logs()` padded *every* log to the single global count. It now pads each log to ITS owning topology's `num_partitions` (`Bootstrap::num_partitions_for()` × `Topology_Registry::basenames_for()`, mirroring the existing per-Partition `segment_size` override map), falling back to the global default only for logs no topology declares (e.g. `Topic`-fanned logs, which size to the global anyway). On-disk orphan partitions left by a shrink are still surfaced (`max(configured, max_disk + 1)` is unchanged). Also memoizes `Topology_Registry::frontmatter()` (the lone un-memoized sibling parser) so the per-topology partition-count lookup doesn't re-read each `.tsl` per `dump_graph`.

## [0.17.0] - 2026-06-12

### Added

- **Eslint rule banning the deprecated `isSmall` Button prop.** `react/forbid-component-props` rejects `isSmall` (deprecated in WP 6.2 for `size="small"`) at the JSX-attribute level. This repo has no current usage, but it owns the canonical shared JS the sibling plugins inline via `@newspack-nodes/shared` — the same rule now guards all three repos so the family can't drift back.

- **Live-mode targets editor in the topology console + debug overlay.** The edit-mode targets UI (target chips with a clearable `×` + "+ add target…" dropdown) now renders in the Inspector's live/view branch too, so an operator can **disconnect** a running node's targets — previously only live *connect* (via canvas drag) was possible. The chip `×` dispatches a runtime `disconnect_node <node> <target>`; the dropdown reuses the existing `connect_node`. Edits mutate the running node's `target` only — **no `.tsl` persistence** (that stays edit-mode's job). The editor reads the node's full uncollapsed `targets` (not the headOf-collapsed / registration-polluted graph edges) so a path target disconnects by its exact value, and is hidden for reserved nodes. New `useGraphHandlers.onRemoveEdge` (mirrors `onConnect`); no new runtime verbs.
- **`send_struct` shell builtin (PHP `Shell_Node` + JS `ShellNode`).** `send_struct <path> <json>` sends a `TM_STRUCT` message whose VALUE is the parsed JSON to `<path>` — the structured-data counterpart to `send` (which sends a `TM_BYTESTREAM` string). Single-quote the JSON so the quote-aware tokenizer keeps it as one token (`send_struct echo '{ "foo": 23, "bar": 42 }'`), exactly as Tachikoma's `send_hash` did. Invalid JSON surfaces the decoder's error and sends nothing (the builtin runs in the Shell, before the command-interpreter's central catch, so it reports the error itself). Renamed from Tachikoma's `send_hash`/`TM_STORABLE` to `send_struct`/`TM_STRUCT` to match our wire vocabulary.

### Changed

- **JS base `Node` `arguments` is the trivial Tachikoma getter/setter, mirroring PHP.** The base `set arguments` now only stores the raw string — it no longer auto-walks `nodeSchema().arguments` onto declared properties. The positional walk moved to an exported `parseSchemaArgs( node, args )` helper (the JS analog of PHP's `Schema_Reflection::parse_schema_args`), which a node opts into from its own setter override; `SseConnectorNode` now does so explicitly. This matches PHP `Node::arguments()` (trivial) + the `Schema_Reflection` trait, closing a JS/PHP fidelity gap. An empty `arguments` string is now a no-op (PHP-faithful) rather than applying schema defaults; `Timer_Node` is unaffected (its `interval_ms` comes from `setTimer()`, not the walk).
- **`Hook_Node` passes the message VALUE to the WordPress hook, not the whole positional envelope.** `do_action()` / `apply_filters()` now receive `message[VALUE]` (the payload) instead of the 7-field message array, so hook callbacks work with the data directly. In filter mode the `apply_filters()` return is always adopted as the new `VALUE`, and `TYPE` is set from its shape — a list-array return marks the message `TM_STRUCT`, anything else `TM_BYTESTREAM` (the prior "drop a non-list return and forward the previous message with a warning" behavior is gone). Hook_Node also forwards via `parent::fill()` now, so it participates in the uniform `target`→`TO` stamping contract. **Callbacks registered on a Hook_Node's hook must take/return the payload, not a `[TYPE, …, VALUE]` array.**

### Fixed

- **`SseConnectorNode` drops frames from a closed (or reopened-past) stream.** The `msg`/`heartbeat` listeners now bail when their EventSource is no longer the connector's current one, so a late frame delivered after `close()` — a teardown race, or a test double that retains listeners — never reaches the torn-down sink. Without the guard the forwarded frame hit a sink-less node and `fill()` threw (`fill requires a wired sink`); graph teardown is now race-safe.
- **JS runtime missing `TM_NOREPLY` reply-control flag (PHP/JS fidelity drift).** The browser substrate's `message.js` lacked `TM_NOREPLY = 512` and the JS `ShellNode` / `CommandInterpreterNode` had no `want_reply` machinery — so the JS side could never suppress a reply the way the PHP runtime does for script/topology-load commands. Ported the PHP behavior: `message.js` now exports `TM_NOREPLY = 512`; `ShellNode` gains a `wantReply()` accessor (default `true`) and a `stampNoreply()` step that ORs `TM_NOREPLY` onto built `TM_COMMAND` messages (in `parse()` and `sendCommand()`) when reply is unwanted; `CommandInterpreterNode._respond()` now suppresses the routed reply for a `TM_NOREPLY` command, surfacing only an error to stderr — mirroring PHP `Command_Interpreter_Node::interpret()`.
- **`Hook_Node::fill()` counted each message twice.** The switch to `parent::fill()` (which increments `counter`) left a redundant local `++$this->counter`; removed so the node counts each message once.

## [0.16.2] - 2026-06-12

### Changed

- **`SseConnectorNode` (`_sse`) now tracks stream liveness, and the Raw Logs dashboard reads it for "Xs ago".** The connector — the one node that sees every inbound frame — stamps a public `lastEventTime` on each `msg` AND on the server's idle `heartbeat` event (snooped, not routed, so the topology-console transcript is unaffected), and clears it on `close()`. The Raw Logs viewer sources its "Xs ago" staleness from `_sse.lastEventTime` instead of row arrivals, so an idle-but-healthy stream resets the counter on each heartbeat instead of climbing like a dead connection; a real drop (no heartbeats) leaves it frozen and "ago" climbs as the intended warning. Sibling application dashboards (event-logger-nodes) read the same `_sse.lastEventTime`.

## [0.16.1] - 2026-06-12

### Changed

- **`Job_Worker_Node` dispatches on the entry-level `k` field, not `type`.** jobs.log / jobintake.log entries carry the job kind (`job` | `remote_job`) under `k` — the same firehose category field `Job_Intake` writes verbatim, `Job_Router` carries through, and the hub's `Stream_Merger` rewrites `job`→`remote_job` — so the executor reads `k` to pick the local vs. remote handler map. This makes the kind field uniform end-to-end (firehose category → jobintake → jobs.log → worker) with no rename at any hop, and fixes jobintake-sourced jobs being silently dropped when a topology wires `jobintake:consumer` straight to `jobs:partition` (bypassing the router, e.g. event-logger-nodes' `combined` topology). Any plugin that hand-builds a job entry must key the kind as `k`, not `type`.

## [0.16.0] - 2026-06-11

### Added

- **`Partition_Node::void_warranty()`** — lifts the 4 KB PIPE_BUF write cap WITHOUT acquiring the per-partition exclusivity lock; the no-lock sibling of `allow_large_writes()`. The caller asserts it is the partition's sole writer (e.g. a worker that already owns the topology lock); concurrent writers + this = silent torn-write corruption, so the name is deliberately alarming. `dump_config` round-trips the distinct verb. The lock-acquiring `allow_large_writes()` remains for cross-process write targets.
- **`Consumer_Node` snapshots a named node's state into the offsetlog** (Tachikoma's `cache_type=snapshot`). `set_snapshot_node()` — a `:config` verb — co-commits `{offset, cache}` as ONE record on each checkpoint, taking the node's duck-typed `save_state()`, and restores it via `restore_state()` on respawn. So a stateful node like the request-builder resumes its in-flight cache aligned with the resumed read offset — no separate state file, no offset/cache drift. The offsetlog is `void_warranty`'d (the worker is its sole writer); a missing snapshot node logs loudly rather than dropping state silently.
- **`Topology_Registry::find_conflicts()` / `write_set()`** — detect when two enabled topologies would write the same file (a data partition or a Consumer offsetlog) and corrupt it. The write-set is parsed from `make_node Partition` / `make_node Topic` paths (both append to the log at their path arg) + `make_node Consumer` offsetlog args; conflicts are reported as topology pairs + the shared resource.
- **Write-conflict enforcement at both gates.** `Admin::sanitize_topologies()` rejects the *whole* topology selection when the enabled set has a write-conflict — it raises a settings error naming the conflicting pairs and keeps the previously-saved set rather than persisting a config that corrupts its own logs. The supervisor re-checks the active set in `check_config()` and refuses to spawn any worker (exits loudly; the cron retries each minute) when the set conflicts — a second line of defense for a config-FILE override that bypasses the admin UI. Together these replace the cross-process protection the per-partition `allow_large_writes` lock used to provide, letting worker-output partitions drop the lock for `void_warranty`.
- **Registration edges on the topology canvas.** `dump_metadata` now emits a per-node `registrations` field — the node-name `register()` listeners, keyed by event — via the new `Node::registered_listeners()` accessor, and `parseMetadata` turns each into a dashed, informational canvas edge from emitter to listener (`SchematicCanvas` `is-registration`: dotted sage, event-name hover tooltip, no edit-mode hit-target). Event wiring that was previously invisible is now drawn. Edges appear only between two **visible** nodes: a registration whose emitter is hidden scaffolding (e.g. every Timer's `TIMER` subscription to `_router`) draws nothing, since `parseMetadata` already skips scaffolding. Both producers — PHP `cmd_dump_metadata` and JS `dumpMetadataPayload` — emit the field only when non-empty, keeping them byte-identical (PHP `[]` vs JS `{}`).
- **`register` / `unregister` REPL verbs** (both the worker-tier and in-browser interpreters) — Tachikoma's `register <source name> <target name> <event>` / `unregister <source name> <target name> <event>`. `register` wires `target` as a node-name listener for `event` on `source` (`source->register( event, target )`); `unregister` drops it. The means to create the registration edges live from the console — the siblings of `connect_node` / `disconnect_node`. Validation matches the reference (source + target must exist for `register`; `unregister` skips the target check so a vanished target can still be cleared), and registering an event the source hasn't declared surfaces as a command error.

### Changed

- **Timer scheduling state moved onto `Timer_Node` itself.** A timer now carries its own `interval_ms` / `oneshot` / `next_fire` / `active` / `fire_count` as public properties, and `Event_Framework::set_timer()` takes just the node (`set_timer( Timer_Node $node )`) — the framework reads the cadence off the node and stamps the first fire. `Timer_Node::set_key()` is folded into a `key()` getter/setter, and `is_active()` / `fire_count()` are dropped in favor of the public `$active` / `$fire_count`. Partition's heartbeat timer arms via `arguments()` + `key()` to match. The Router-hitchhike preconditions stay fail-loud: no-arg `set_timer()` throws if the timer is unnamed or no `_router` is present (now covered by tests asserting each message).
- **SSE drain test seam is the `check_slot` Closure, not `set_test_mode()` / `set_test_iterations()`.** Those helpers — and the bounded-iteration counter inside `run_stream_loop()` — are removed; tests bound the loop by assigning a counting closure to the production slot-liveness seam `SSE_Out_Node::$check_slot`. Production behavior is unchanged: `connection_aborted()` plus the real slot check still terminate the stream.
- **`Consumer_Node` reads one block per poll (drain-then-read), not all segments at once.** `poll()` now dispatches through a `$poll_cb` function pointer (Tachikoma's `$self->{fill}`): each tick drains the already-buffered block, then reads at most one `READ_BLOCK_BYTES` (64 KB) block via `get_batch()`, then yields the event loop — mirroring Tachikoma `fire()` + `Partition::process_get`, so a worker draining a backlog can't monopolize the drain loop. `publish_position()` is throttled to once per `PUBLISH_INTERVAL_S` (1 s) instead of every tick. The internal `$line_remainder` became `$buffer` (read-ahead). The removed public `MAX_POLL_BYTES` constant is replaced by `READ_BLOCK_BYTES`; `Reqgrep_Command` (event-logger-nodes) carries its own `READ_CHUNK_BYTES` instead.
- **`Classes_CI_Node` extends `Service_CI_Node` like the other service interpreters.** Its single `list` verb is now declared in `node_schema()` carrying its handler, and the base constructor derives the dispatch table — replacing the bespoke `__construct()` + `command_table()`. Behavior is unchanged (same catalog output); it just stops being the one service CI that hand-rolled its own command table.

### Fixed

- **Consumer snapshot restore is order-independent — a forward-referenced snapshot node no longer discards its cache on every restart.** `set_snapshot_node()` now only records the name; the offsetlog read and `restore_state()` are deferred to the first `poll()` (a new `poll_init` phase) which runs inside the drain loop, after the whole topology graph is built. A per-node-serialized topology that emits `set_snapshot_node request-builder` *before* `make_node request-builder` previously logged "snapshot node missing or has no restore_state(); discarding restored cache" and dropped the request-builder's in-flight LRU cache on every worker recycle; the restore now finds the by-then-built node and resumes its state aligned with the resumed read offset. `arguments()` no longer does offsetlog I/O at construction; the IPC-input Consumer seeks `next_offset('end')` at build and `poll_init` overrides with the durable checkpoint when one exists (resume wins).

- **Topology console Connect↔Disconnect toggle flips again (and works for the in-browser JS tee).** `dump_metadata` now stamps a reserved `_header.pwd` carrying the requesting session's reply pivot — the reverse_cwd, i.e. the inbound message FROM, which is the exact target a `connect_node` with no target stores on a Tee. Both tiers emit it: the worker's `cmd_dump_metadata` from the envelope FROM (full snapshot only), the in-browser `dumpMetadataPayload` as the bare `_output`. `parseMetadata` exposes it as `graph.pwd` (and preserves each node's FULL `targets`, since canvas edges head-collapse every session's pivot to one shared `_repl`), and the Inspector toggles on an exact `node.targets.includes(parsed.pwd)`. Previously the matcher reconstructed a hardcoded path whose middle segment (`_http`) the v0.15.0 `_Node` rename changed to `_output`, so it never matched and the button was stuck on "Connect". Two further mismatches are reconciled: the `pwd` arrives ending in the POLLING node's reply segment (`…/_metadata`, since the canvas polls FROM `_metadata`) but a tail target ends in the shell's `_output`, so `canonicalReplyPivot()` forces the final segment to `_output` (used by both the matcher and the optimistic patch); and `dumpMetadataPayload` now reports the SHELL name (`Tee`, stripping `_Node`) like the worker does, so the in-browser tee's Connect button doesn't vanish on the next poll under a `TeeNode` class.
- **Live-graph gestures reflect immediately instead of waiting out the ~5s metadata poll.** The Tee tail/disconnect optimistic patches were wrong — `tail` replaced the whole fan-out array with the string `_output`, `disconnect` cleared ALL of a Tee's edges.
